# DepTime backlog

**How to read this**
- **IDs** look like `DT-<EPIC>-NN` and are referenced from the docs and code. Put the ID in commit messages and PR titles.
- **Priority:**
  - `P0`: blocks the milestone.
  - `P1`: needed for the milestone's goal.
  - `P2`: nice to have.
- **Size:** `S` ≤ ½ day · `M` 1–2 days · `L` 3–5 days.
- **Direction:** free, open source (MIT), local-first ([ADR 0007](docs/adr/0007-open-source-local-first.md)).

| Milestone | Exit criterion |
|---|---|
| **M0 Spikes** | S1 go/no-go (false-absent ≤ 10%); S2–S7 answered |
| **M1 Local alpha** | Public repo; nightly index published; `uvx deptime mcp` works from PyPI; benchmark ≥ 70% |
| **M2 Distribution** | Registries, plugin, snippets, demo GIF, 5 rename-map pages |
| **M3 Optional hosted** | Public endpoint for web clients, with limits and a privacy notice |

---

## Current state: public alpha, v0.1.12 (since 2026-11-10)

- 0.1.12 (tag on `74d4e3c`) is on PyPI, the MCP Registry (latest) and GitHub (Latest), published by `release.yml`; a fresh `uvx --from deptime==0.1.12` install pulls the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 26 index with `index pull --verify` and answers with the query changes it brings: `check_symbols` reads llama-index-core 0.14.25's `ServiceContext.from_defaults` present, with what it raises ("ValueError: ServiceContext is deprecated. …"; DT-DATA-67), and `explain_error` reads fastmcp 3's "FastMCP() no longer accepts `log_level`" as `FastMCP.__init__(log_level)` removed after 2.14.7, with `run_http_async` (DT-CORE-47). What changed since 0.1.11 is in `CHANGELOG.md`.

- The repo is public. `uvx deptime mcp` installs from PyPI, and pushing a `v*` tag publishes through trusted publishing (`release.yml`).
- Every index published since 2026-11-07 has a signed build provenance attestation; `deptime index pull --verify` (0.1.9 and later) and `gh attestation verify` check it (DT-OSS-09, SECURITY.md).
- `build-index` runs at 04:17 and 16:17 UTC (twice a day since DT-DATA-04; nightly before) and replaces the rolling `index` release. The first full builds (2026-10-28) indexed 52 packages and 3,371 releases into a 78.9 MB `.gz`. - Not covered yet: names numpy only creates at runtime, such as 1.x's `numpy.emath`, the scalar types 1.x adds in a loop (`numpy.core.longfloat`) and `ndarray.tostring` (DT-DATA-02), names computed at import time, such as instructor 1.17's provider factories (DT-DATA-51), the names inside standard-library modules that changed before Python 3.9, which typeshed no longer models, and their classes' members (DT-DATA-47 covers module-level names from 3.9 to 3.15; which modules each Python has is covered, DT-DATA-06). Left out on purpose (`namespace_skip`): the folders without `__init__.py` in litellm's bundled proxy server and selenium's generated DevTools bindings.

**Holdout 2026-11e (DT-QA-11), first run 2026-11-10:** the first set scored after the fixes designed from 2026-11d's misses (DT-DATA-63 to 67 and 69, DT-CORE-46 to 48). On 2,052 uses real projects make of a covered package that still work after an upgrade, and 140 that break, in 163 upgrades of repositories that pin a release (all 53 packages searched, plus 2 repositories picked for huggingface_hub's top-level functions called with keywords; none asked about before), `check_symbols` flags 0.05% of the working ones (1/2,024; precision 99.3%, recall 99.3%; 28 working uses `unknown`) and `diff_versions` 0.2% (4/2,052; precision 97.2%, recall 97.9%). Both find all 22 huggingface_hub breaks and flag none of its 113 working uses. On 28 new tracebacks from GitHub issues, `explain_error` gets the root cause and boundary for 85.7% (24/28; 68.5–94.3), precision 92.3%, replacement 71.4% (10/14). Pre-registered in `42e1496`, scored once on the index built at `a1f1563` (`bench/holdout/2026-11e/results-2026-11-10.md`). None of 2026-11d's causes comes back. The new misses are DT-CORE-50 to 55, and for the scorer's reading of a parameter removed into `**kwargs` (3 of `diff_versions`' 4 false positives), DT-QA-12; they're scored on the next set (DT-QA-12).

**Holdout 2026-11d (DT-QA-10), first run 2026-11-10:** the first set scored after the fixes designed from 2026-11c's misses (DT-DATA-59 to 62, DT-CORE-41 to 45). On 2,279 uses real projects make of a covered package that still work after an upgrade, and 176 that break, in 174 upgrades of repositories that pin a release (all 53 packages searched, plus 10 repositories picked for uses 2026-11c didn't exercise; none asked about before), `check_symbols` flags 0.9% of the working ones (20/2,224; precision 89.4%, recall 96.6%; 55 working uses and 2 breaks `unknown`) and `diff_versions` 0.7% (17/2,279; precision 90.9%, recall 96.0%). 15 of each tool's false positives come from one upgrade, dspy 2.6 code asked about dspy 0.1.5, a placeholder release that ships no `dspy` module (DT-DATA-63). On 30 new tracebacks from GitHub issues, `explain_error` gets the root cause and boundary for 86.7% (26/30; 70.3–94.7), precision 96.3%, replacement 47.1% (8/17). Pre-registered in `20be3ab`, scored once on the index built at `41ebc67` (`bench/holdout/2026-11d/results-2026-11-10.md`). 2026-11c's causes don't come back, and the exercise stratum reads mcp 2's `mcp.types` and crewai's calls right. The new misses are DT-DATA-63 to 69, DT-DATA-51 (chromadb) and DT-CORE-46 to 48; they're scored on the next set (DT-QA-11).

**Holdout 2026-11c (DT-QA-09), first run 2026-11-09:** the first set scored after the fixes designed from 2026-11b's misses (DT-CORE-36 to 40, DT-DATA-55 to 58, DT-DATA-45 and DT-DATA-52's transformers rules). On 1,841 uses real projects make of a covered package that still work after an upgrade, and 128 that break, in 159 upgrades of repositories that pin a release (all 53 packages searched; none asked about before), `check_symbols` flags 0.5% of the working ones (9/1,801; precision 93.2%, recall 99.2%; 40 working uses and 4 breaks `unknown`) and `diff_versions` 0.3% (6/1,841; precision 95.5%, recall 99.2%). On 35 new tracebacks from GitHub issues, `explain_error` gets the root cause and boundary for 88.6% (31/35; 74.0–95.5), precision 93.9%, replacement 50.0% (4/8). Pre-registered in `bfc0348`, scored once on the index built at `21b60e0` (`bench/holdout/2026-11c/results-2026-11-09.md`). **Holdout 2026-11b (DT-QA-08), first run 2026-11-08:** the first set scored after the fixes designed from 2026-11's misses (DT-CORE-32 to 35, DT-DATA-52 to 54). On 2,542 uses real projects make of a covered package that still work after an upgrade, and 146 that break, from 176 upgrades of repositories that pin a release (all 53 packages; none asked about before), `check_symbols` flags 1.5% of the working ones (38/2,503; precision 78.7%, recall 95.9%; 39 `unknown`) and `diff_versions` 0.2% (5/2,542; precision 96.6%, recall 95.9%). On 37 new tracebacks from GitHub issues, `explain_error` gets the root cause and boundary for 91.9% (34/37; 78.7–97.2), precision 97.1%, replacement 35.0% (7/20). Pre-registered in `8572ac4`, scored once (`bench/holdout/2026-11b/results-2026-11-08.md`). Both tools' six missed breaks turned out to be label errors (gradio 3 sets its event listeners on each instance, which the probe can't see); corrected after scoring, recall is 100% for both (140/140). The misses are DT-DATA-56 (mcp 2's `mcp.types` reads missing: 16 of the 38 false positives, and `diff_versions` reports `mcp.types.Tool` and the rest removed in 2.0.0), DT-DATA-55 (10), DT-CORE-39 (5), DT-CORE-38 (6), DT-DATA-52, DT-DATA-45, and for the tracebacks DT-CORE-36, DT-CORE-37 and DT-DATA-57; they're scored on the next set (DT-QA-09). On the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 22 index (built 2026-11-08, with all of those but DT-DATA-45), a re-run of the same set (a check, not a score): `check_symbols` flags 1 working use of 2,499 (0.04%; precision 99.3%, recall 100%; 43 `unknown`), transformers 5's `Qwen2TokenizerFast` (DT-DATA-52); `diff_versions` 3 of 2,542 (0.1%; precision 97.9%, recall 100%); the tracebacks root cause + boundary 100% (37/37), replacement 40.0% (8/20). On the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 23 index (built 2026-11-09, DT-DATA-45 and DT-DATA-52's transformers rules), a re-run (a check): `check_symbols` flags none of the 2,499 working uses (precision 100%, recall 100%); `diff_versions` 1 of 2,542 (0.04%; precision 99.3%, recall 100%); the tracebacks unchanged.

On the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 22 index (built 2026-11-08, with DT-CORE-36 to 39 and DT-DATA-55 to 57), a re-run (a check): `check_symbols` 0.2% (6/3,519; precision 95.4%, recall 98.4%), `diff_versions` unchanged, the tracebacks root cause + boundary 100% (35/35), replacement 76.9% (10/13). **Benchmark (2026-11-05): 191 real tracebacks from 50 packages** (`bench/`): root cause 93.7%, root cause + boundary 93.2%, replacement 64.8% (53.8% without curated hints). The 32 cases added that day scored 68.8% root cause + boundary when first run (DT-QA-01), against 96.2% on the 159 before them, which fixes were designed from; their misses are assigned below. On the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 16 index (DT-DATA-22, 2026-11-05), seven misses pass, four of them new cases: root cause 96.9%, root cause + boundary 96.9%, replacement 67.0% (56.0% without curated hints); the 32 new cases score 90.6%. With DT-CORE-27 and DT-CORE-28 (no format change), 97.9%, and the new cases 96.9% (31/32); replacement 68.1% (57.1%) once reassembled. Five standard-library cases (DT-DATA-06), collected after that fix and scored once, all pass: 196 real cases, root cause + boundary 98.0%, replacement 68.1% (57.1%). The `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 17 index (DT-DATA-49, DT-DATA-46, 2026-11-06) makes weaviate's `_Collections._get_all` case pass: 98.5%, replacement unchanged. Six standard-library name cases (DT-DATA-47), collected after that fix and scored once, all pass: 202 real cases, 98.5%, replacement 68.5% (57.6%). With DT-CORE-02 (same day, no format change), the click and marshmallow subclass cases pass: 99.5% (201/202). The 35 tracebacks of the 2026-11 holdout joined after their first scoring (2026-11-07): 237 real cases, 97.9%, replacement 66.7% (57.1%). With DT-CORE-35 (same day, no index change), polars' `AttributeRemovedError` case passes and fastmcp's two `tools.tool` cases get the right root cause: root cause 99.6%, root cause + boundary 98.3% (233/237), replacement 67.6% (58.1%). On the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 20 index (built 2026-11-08), fastmcp's two cases pass (DT-DATA-54): root cause 99.6%, root cause + boundary 99.2% (235/237), replacement 67.6% (58.1%); the two left are pydantic-ai's `instrument` (DT-DATA-52) and polars' `sort`, a known gap. All 32 cases collected 2026-11-05 pass. The 37 tracebacks of the 2026-11b holdout joined after their first scoring (2026-11-08): 274 real cases, root cause 98.5%, root cause + boundary 98.2%, replacement 62.4% (54.4% without curated hints); its three misses are known gaps (DT-CORE-36, DT-CORE-37, DT-DATA-57). With DT-CORE-36 (same day, no index change), openai's `create(file_ids)` passes: root cause 98.9%, root cause + boundary 98.5% (270/274), replacement 62.4% (54.4%). With DT-CORE-37 (same day, no index change), scikit-learn's `SCORERS` passes: root cause 99.3%, root cause + boundary 98.9% (271/274). On the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 22 index (built 2026-11-08), matplotlib's `w_xaxis` (DT-DATA-57) and pydantic-ai's `instrument` (DT-DATA-52) pass, and mcp's two `inputSchema` cases and redis' `indexDefinition` get the right replacement: root cause 99.6%, root cause + boundary 99.6% (273/274), replacement 65.6% (58.4%); the one left is polars' `sort`, a known gap.

**Holdout (DT-QA-04, 2026-10-31): root cause + boundary 72.4%** (21/29), root cause 82.8%, replacement 0%, on 29 tracebacks from TimeMachine-bench's verified subset (`bench/tmb/`), pre-registered in `22dea55` and scored once on the same index. That's above the 70% floor and below the 80% target, at about ±15 points. Breaks already in `bench/cases` score 76.9% (all with the right root cause), and new breaks 68.8%. 32 of the 47 labelled breaks on covered packages are in scope. The eight misses are assigned below: DT-DATA-25 (4), DT-DATA-22, DT-CORE-21, DT-DATA-02 and DT-CORE-02 (`bench/tmb/results-2026-10-31.md`). The `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 6 index (DT-DATA-29) changes no case. Fixes now aim at these misses, so later scores on these cases aren't holdout numbers: with DT-CORE-21 (2026-10-31) the `ndarray.itemset` case passes, for 75.9% (22/29) and root cause 86.2%. With DT-DATA-25 (2026-11-01) the three `numpy.product` cases and the `numpy.math` case pass, for 89.7% (26/29) and root cause 89.7%. The format 8 and 9 indexes (DT-DATA-33, DT-DATA-34) change no case. With DT-CORE-06 (2026-11-01), replacement is 31.8% (7/22), from 0%. The format 10 index (DT-DATA-36, 2026-11-02) gives three django cases the right replacement: 45.5% (10/22), and no other case changes. Counting the names DT-DATA-22 records as not public API as present (`compare.py --internal`), the pre-registered rule, which also samples a plain module's imports from its own package, falls from 18.2% to 1.1% (`bench/s1/results-2026-11-05-internal.md`). The `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 17 index (2026-11-06) leaves it at 1.2%; instructor's rate over every runtime name falls from 10.5% to 9.7% (DT-DATA-28; `bench/s1/results-2026-11-06.md`). transformers, covered since DT-DATA-16, was probed the same day from its own plan and is reported apart from the ten (DT-DATA-05): 0.4% sampled (1/250) and 0.4% of every runtime name (257/58,438), 246 of them in 4.57.3 (`bench/s1/results-2026-11-06-transformers.md`). After DT-DATA-39 and DT-CORE-22 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 14, 2026-11-04), 32 of the 50 (anthropic's `Anthropic(proxies)`, fastapi's `Query(regex)`, httpx's `Client(cert)` and selenium's chrome `Service(log_path)`, all from DT-DATA-39), and 32 of the 35 are graded right (`bench/s2/results-2026-11-04b.md`). **M1:** the benchmark bar (≥ 70%) now holds on fresh cases: 72.4% on the TimeMachine-bench holdout, 88.6% on the 2026-11 holdout, 91.9% on 2026-11b's, 88.6% on 2026-11c's, 86.7% on 2026-11d's and 85.7% on 2026-11e's.

The 2026-11e holdout's misses (DT-QA-11, scored 2026-11-10): DT-CORE-50 (a call with too many positional arguments: the one missed break per tool), DT-CORE-52 and DT-CORE-51 (two and one of the tracebacks), then DT-CORE-53, -54 and -55; all query-side, so no `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` bump. DT-DATA-09 stays open. 2026-11c's and 2026-11b's misses are done (DT-DATA-59 to 62 and DT-CORE-41 to 45; DT-CORE-36 to 39, DT-DATA-55 to 58, DT-DATA-45 and DT-DATA-52), and DT-CORE-40 but for transformers' `AdamW` and scikit-learn's `SCORERS`.

## ✅ Done (2026-10-17)

- [x] Problem and prior-art research, specs, architecture, self-hosting, risks, privacy/security docs, and ADRs
- [x] Ingestion: PEP 691 client, version policy, sha256-verified download, safe extraction, griffe public-path snapshots, diffs, replacement mining, span-encoded SQLite index
- [x] Resilience: a failed run never wipes an index; a PyPI outage rebuilds from cached snapshots; per-package failure isolation
- [x] Query layer: 5 tools, confidence semantics, `<=X` deprecations, unindexed-version windows, `never_existed`
- [x] **Local mode:** `deptime mcp` (stdio); first-run background download + daily refresh check + hot swap; `deptime index pull|status|pack`; offline mode
- [x] **Index distribution:** `pack` → `manifest.json` + `index.sqlite.gz`; nightly `build-index.yml` publishing to a rolling `index` release, with a shrink guard
- [x] Hosted mode (optional): `/mcp` (stateless, spec 2026-07-28) + `/v1/*` + anonymous limits
- [x] OSS hygiene: MIT LICENSE, PyPI metadata, CONTRIBUTING, CODE_OF_CONDUCT, SECURITY, issue forms (wrong answer / coverage / bug), PR template, `release.yml` (trusted publishing)
- [x] Claude Code plugin (stdio `uvx deptime mcp` + skill + hook), validated with `claude plugin validate`
- [x] 53 offline tests, including a real stdio launch, distribution and pipeline end to end. Verified the built wheel via `uvx` over stdio on real httpx wheels.

Method and pass bars: [docs/07](docs/07-risks-and-spikes.md).

- [x] **DT-SPIKE-01**: S1 static-analysis accuracy on dynamic packages · `P0` `M` · **go/no-go**
  - AC: a false-absent rate measured on 10 packages × 5 versions × 50 runtime-sampled symbols, with the script in `bench/s1/`. Pass at ≤ 10%; otherwise build the sandboxed dynamic fallback and re-measure; stop if it's still above 10%.

  - **Sample:** 10 packages: langchain, langchain-community, langchain-core, openai, pydantic, huggingface-hub, litellm, pydantic-ai-slim, instructor, numpy (transformers is out of coverage). 5 releases each, evenly in time. Sampled at runtime in `.github/workflows/s1.yml` only (no network, a throwaway user, wheels-only installs).
  - **The gate was amended after the first run.** It leaves out a plain module's imports from elsewhere in its package (`hf_api.hf_hub_url`), which the index excludes by design. With them in, the rate is 549/2,452 = 22.4%; the README gives the reasoning.
  - **Results by package:**
    - 0% on huggingface-hub, langchain-community, langchain-core, openai and pydantic-ai-slim (0 of 14,813 langchain-community runtime names missed);
    - langchain 1.2%, instructor 6.3%, pydantic 8.0%, litellm 15.6%, numpy 21.2%.
    - 37 sampled misses would read as removed.
  - **Where the misses go:** litellm → DT-DATA-23, numpy → DT-DATA-25, pydantic → DT-DATA-26, langchain → DT-DATA-27, instructor → DT-DATA-28.

Triage of the 39 misses: several warnings in one function, of which only the first is read (DT-DATA-42: pydantic `validator` and `Field(min_items)`, gradio, langgraph, pydantic-ai); deprecation helpers and decorators (DT-DATA-37: sqlalchemy `Query.get`, redis `close`, polars `groupby`, streamlit `image(use_column_width)`); `'deprecated'` defaults and docstrings (DT-DATA-38: scikit-learn `base_estimator`, ollama `embeddings`); text the miner misses (DT-CORE-23: openai, django `CheckConstraint(check)`, matplotlib); a false removal (DT-DATA-43: starlette's status codes); a case-only parameter rename (DT-CORE-24: mcp); and removals with no deprecation text in the source at all (langchain `MultiQueryRetriever`, `mcp.types.Content`, dspy `OpenAI`, mistralai `MistralClient`, `huggingface_hub.InferenceApi`, flask `JSONEncoder`), which need DT-DATA-08 or DT-DATA-07. With 11 hints the precision estimate is loose; re-score after each extraction fix, and grade a random sample of the index's hints at ≥ 0.75 before 1.0.

The 13 gained: langgraph `config_schema`, pydantic `Field(min_items)`, pydantic-ai `result_type`, django `CheckConstraint(check)`, gradio `allow_flagging` (DT-DATA-42); starlette `HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY` (DT-DATA-43); sqlalchemy `Query.get`, polars `groupby`, streamlit `experimental_get_query_params`, redis `close` (DT-DATA-37); ollama `embeddings`, scikit-learn `base_estimator`, pytest `console_main` (DT-DATA-38).   - AC: `.xz` under 100 MB (the `.gz` until DT-DATA-35); nightly incremental under 30 min; p95 under 20 ms; cold build fits Actions limits (or a matrix plan).

The slowest call by far is `openai-httpx-proxies-internal` at 560 ms, the search of other packages for an error raised inside a dependency (DT-CORE-16). Size and cold build pass; p95 and the nightly don't yet.

With DT-DATA-41, a run in which no package changed took 8 minutes end to end (build-index run 41495147430): 3 for the per-package jobs, 16 seconds to assemble, 3.4 to pack. A package with a new release is still assembled in full (litellm about 7 minutes, django 3.4), so a typical nightly should take 10–20 minutes; the nightly target is met, the p95 one isn't yet.

The `.xz` is 68.3 MB (from 67.7), the `.gz` 109.7 MB (from 108.7). p95 for `explain_error` is the same on the format-10 and format-11 indexes, timed in alternation (59–72 ms on both, on a machine under load).

Two query plans had read every row of a package (DT-CORE-16); with them fixed, the slowest case is 7.4 ms. Without installed versions p95 is 6.4 ms (from 105), and with every covered package installed 24.8 ms (from 120); the slowest call in either is 222 ms, a Python 3.9 message that names no class (`__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'app'`), whose owner is searched in all 52 packages. The `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 15 index (DT-DATA-14, same day) still passes: `.xz` 71.1 MB, cold build 150 minutes (litellm's job 114), `explain_error` p95 3.7 ms.

- [x] **DT-OSS-07**: Supply chain · `P1` `S`
  - AC: GitHub Actions pinned to SHAs; Dependabot for `uv.lock` and actions; `pip-audit` in CI.
  - Done 2026-10-31: every action is pinned to the commit its major tag pointed at (no change in behaviour), with the release in a comment. `.github/dependabot.yml` proposes weekly `uv` and `github-actions` updates, minor and patch grouped per ecosystem; griffe gets a PR of its own, since a new extractor can change snapshots without a `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` bump. A ci job runs `pip-audit --strict` over every package in `uv.lock`, with pip-audit itself locked in an `audit` dependency group. Its first run found certifi 2026.7.22 still trusting the emSign roots behind compromised eMudhra intermediates (PYSEC-2026-4320); the lock now has 2026.10.23.
- [x] **DT-OSS-08**: Windows support for index replace and hot swap · `P1` `S` · deps: SPIKE-07
  - AC: download and refresh succeed while the server holds the old file open (e.g. versioned filenames plus a pointer file).
  - Done 2026-11-05: SQLite opens files without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` on Windows, so `os.replace` onto `index.sqlite` failed while any `deptime mcp` had it open, and the refresh kept the old index ("could not refresh"). A download now goes to a file named after the build, `index-<sha256[:16]>.sqlite`, and the meta file, already replaced atomically, names it under a new `file` key (only a name of that shape beside it counts). Every place that opens the index reads the name there (`index/location.py`); without it, a locally built index or one downloaded before this change, the index is `index.sqlite` as before. A server that refreshes switches to the new file and closes the old store a minute later; one that finds another process installed a newer file switches at start; files nothing names are deleted once nothing holds them, and the rest at a later refresh. Temp files are per process now (`mkstemp`), so two downloads at once don't clobber each other, and a rename the OS refuses while another process reads the meta file is retried. `ingest --into-downloaded` (CI's publish job) moves the downloaded file to `index.sqlite` first. `ci.yml` runs the whole suite on `windows-latest` (294 passed); the open-file tests simulate Windows' refusal on other systems and get the real one there. Released clients ignore `file`: one that finds no `index.sqlite` downloads again.
- [x] **DT-OSS-09**: Signed index provenance · `P2` `M`
  - AC: `build-index.yml` produces GitHub artifact attestations; `deptime index pull --verify` checks them; documented in SECURITY.md.
  - Done 2026-11-07 (`5ebee33`): the publish job signs a SLSA provenance attestation for `index.sqlite.xz`, `index.sqlite.gz` and `manifest.json` with `actions/attest` before uploading them; if signing fails, nothing is published. The Sigstore bundle is published beside them as `index.sigstore.json`. `deptime index pull --verify`, or `DEPTIME_VERIFY_INDEX=1` for every download including the background refresh, runs `gh attestation verify --bundle` on the downloaded file after its sha256 check, so it needs the GitHub CLI but no login. DepTime then requires the certificate to name the index URL's repository, `.github/workflows/build-index.yml@refs/heads/main`, a GitHub-hosted runner and the SLSA predicate, and to list the file's digest. It compares these exactly itself, because `gh` before 2.97 and 2.102 matched `--signer-workflow` and `--source-ref` loosely. A file that fails is not installed. The metadata file records the run and commit, and `index status` shows them. The first signed index is build-index 42377215169 (7 minutes, 53 packages). From an empty home with no GitHub login, `pull --verify` installed it in 11 s and named commit `5ebee33` and that run. A changed manifest fails `gh attestation verify`. `bench.yml` now pulls with `--verify`, so every published index is checked the way users check it. Documented in SECURITY.md ("Verifying the index"), docs/04, 05, 07 and 08.
- [x] **DT-OSS-10**: A failed first download recovers without a restart · `P0` `S`
  - AC: an index that appears on disk is opened on the next call (done); the refresh thread retries with backoff while there is no index; the note names the real cause (failed download, offline).
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`afdf8a0`): the refresh thread retries after 30 s, 1, 2, 5 and 10 minutes, then every 30 minutes, until an index lands or another process puts one on disk. Until then the note names the error and the next attempt ("could not download the index (ConnectError: …) … retrying in the background in 1 min"); with `DEPTIME_OFFLINE` set it says so and never retries.
- [x] **DT-OSS-11**: Package requests, and what an unsupported package gets · `P2` `S`
  - Why: packages are added from breaks seen in issues; users have no way to ask for one, and a `not_covered` answer doesn't say what DepTime does cover or how to ask.
  - AC: an issue template for a package request (distribution, import names, releases in use, an example error); `not_covered` answers point to `coverage` and to the template; a coverage page generated from the published index at each release lists every package with its first and latest indexed release and the build date, and says how an unsupported package is answered.
  - Done 2026-11-07: the package request form asks for the releases in use and an example error; `explain_error`'s note names the distributions it doesn't index and points to `coverage` and the form, and so do the other tools' notes; `docs/coverage.md`, written by `tools/coverage_page.py` from the published index (first from the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 18 index), lists each package with its first and latest indexed release and the build date, and says how an unindexed package is answered. Regenerate it at each release.
- [x] **DT-OSS-12**: `deptime index pull` prints a traceback when a download fails · `P2` `S`
  - Why: on 2026-11-09 GitHub answered 504 for the release's assets for a while after a publish (an incident on its side); `index pull` installed nothing and kept the current index, as it should, but showed a Python traceback, since it caught only a failed provenance check.
  - Done 2026-11-09: a network or HTTP error, a checksum or size mismatch, a bad file or a schema it can't read exits 1 with one line that says why and that nothing was installed ("could not pull the index (HTTP 504 Gateway Time-out from …/index.sqlite.xz)"); a redirect's signed query string stays out of it. The server's refresh already reported these and retries (DT-OSS-10).
- [x] **DT-OSS-13**: One agent guide that every coding agent reads · `P2` `S`
  - Why: the repo's guide for coding agents was `CLAUDE.md`, which only Claude Code reads; Codex, Cursor, Copilot, Gemini CLI and others read `AGENTS.md`. The paste-in snippet for users' projects was itself named `AGENTS.md`, so a tool that follows that convention would take it as rules for `integrations/snippets/`.
  - Done 2026-11-10: the guide is `AGENTS.md`, and `CLAUDE.md` is a one-line import of it. Not a symlink: Git on Windows checks a symlink out as a text file by default, and Claude Code would load that file and not fall back to `AGENTS.md`. Claude Code 2.1.322 also loads `AGENTS.md` by itself when a project has no `CLAUDE.md`; the import covers older versions. The snippet is now `integrations/snippets/agents-md-deptime.md`. The guide also gained the benchmark regression command, `$XDG_CACHE_HOME`, a date on each verified API, the untrusted-data rule as its own invariant (10), and the plugin check re-run on 2.1.322.

### Data (DT-DATA)
- [x] **DT-DATA-01**: Index Tier A with the coverage policy · `P0` `M` · deps: SPIKE-01, SPIKE-04
  - AC: ≥ 90% of selected releases indexed for each package; failures listed in the workflow summary.
  - Done so far (2026-11-02): 6,568 of 6,569 selected releases are indexed; the lowest package is dspy at 87 of 88 (0.1.5 has no module `dspy`). `deptime ingest --summary` writes each package's share and failures to the run summary, and the publish job uses it. To close: check the summary on a published run.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (build-index run 41495147430): the run summary, also printed to the publish job's log because signed-out viewers and the API can't read summaries, reads "52 packages: 6570 of 6571 selected releases indexed (99.98%)… Every counted package meets it", with one row: dspy, 87 of 88 (0.1.5 has no module `dspy`).
- [ ] **DT-DATA-02**: Index Tier B · `P1` `M` · deps: DATA-01
  - AC: same bar as DATA-01; numpy indexed via its `.pyi` stubs; compiled-only gaps noted in `coverage.toml`.
  - Done so far: names numpy's `__init__.py` imports and `__init__.pyi` defines (`add`, `float64`) are indexed for 2.0 and 2.1 too. `numpy.emath.sqrt` is indexed for 2.x once a `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 4 index is published (DT-DATA-24); in 1.x `numpy.emath` is still only an attribute from the stub.
  - Gap (2026-10-31): methods the stub leaves out and only compiled code registers, through `_core/_add_newdocs.py`. Since DT-DATA-29 the only public `ndarray` one is `tostring` (stub: "deprecated and therefore excluded"; removed in 2.3), so it isn't worth a reader of numpy's `add_newdoc` table. Noted in `coverage.toml`. 1 holdout miss (`tmb-numpy-ndarray-tostring-sonipy`).
- [x] **DT-DATA-03**: Cold-build strategy for CI · `P1` `S` · deps: SPIKE-04
  - AC: the first full build completes (split into a matrix if needed); the snapshot cache stays within Actions cache limits.
  - Done: one job per package builds and caches its own snapshots, then a publish job assembles the index from them and from the published index; only snapshots stay on disk; a timed-out build still saves its cache. A cached snapshot is reused only if its format and its import names match (2026-10-28), so a `coverage.toml` edit reaches every release without a format bump.The first full builds (2026-10-28) took 35–61 min with every job green; the slowest is litellm (25 min, 290 releases). The snapshot cache is 2.3 GB of the 10 GB limit, including entries from older snapshot formats that expire unused.
- [x] **DT-DATA-04**: Freshness SLO + yanked handling · `P1` `S`
  - AC: a new release is indexed within 24 h (nightly); yanked releases dropped on rebuild; build date shown by `coverage` and `index status`.
  - Done 2026-11-05. **Schedule:** the last ten scheduled `build-index` runs started 6–8 h after the 04:17 UTC cron and about 24 h apart, so a release uploaded just after a run started waited up to ~27 h. The cron now fires at 04:17 and 16:17 UTC; a no-change run takes 8 minutes (DT-DATA-41). **Measured each run:** the summary says whether every selected release uploaded more than 24 h before the run is indexed, up to each package's newest, lists the ones that aren't with their age, and names the newest indexed release and its age. Its first run (build-index 42128327643, 2026-11-05, every package reassembled in 53 minutes) flagged dspy 0.1.5, uploaded 2.5 years earlier and never analysable (no `dspy` module), so only releases newer than a package's newest indexed one count now; the newest indexed release was streamlit 1.67.0, uploaded 4 h before the run. **Yanked:** a release yanked after it was indexed leaves the index at the next rebuild, from the snapshot cache too (CI's assembly), and isn't kept as a failed build (a test now shows it). **Build date:** `index status` has `built_at` and `coverage` `index_built_at` (both already did); `coverage` now also gives each package's `latest_released_at`. **Answers past the index:** an installed release newer than every indexed one was answered as if it were the newest: a name the index has there read `present_in_installed_version`, and one it never had read `never_existed` at `medium`, "may be misremembered". Now the first reads `symbol_removed` (or `param_removed`) after the newest indexed release, with no first release without it, `pin_to_restore` up to it, at `medium`, saying no indexed release shows the change and naming the other causes; the second is `low` and says DepTime can't tell whether a later release adds it; `check_symbols` says names added after the newest release read missing. Both benchmarks are unchanged.
- [x] **DT-DATA-05**: Lazy-module support (`__getattr__` re-exports) · `P1` `L` · deps: SPIKE-01
  - AC: langchain/transformers false-absent rate below 10%.
  - Progress 2026-10-28: DT-DATA-20 records names a module `__getattr__` serves when they are compared with literals or looked up in a module-level literal table (langchain-community's lazy `llms` imports). Names computed at runtime are still missed.
  - Measured 2026-11-06 on the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 17 index (S1, `bench/s1/results-2026-11-06*.md`): langchain 0.4% (1/242 sampled), transformers 0.4% (1/250; 257 of 58,438 runtime names, five releases from 4.26.0 to 5.22.2, probed without torch). Both are under the bar. transformers' misses are one shape: a model package's `__init__` hands `_LazyModule` `define_import_structure(_file)`, which at runtime exports what every module file in its folder lists in `__all__` or decorates `@requires`, while its `if TYPE_CHECKING:` imports are generated and sometimes stale. In 4.57.3, `models/__init__.py` leaves out 15 models (SmolLM3, Apertus, Ernie4_5, …) and `models/t5gemma/__init__.py` star-imports `configuration_encdecgemma2`, which doesn't exist, so `transformers.SmolLM3ForCausalLM` read absent in every 4.x release.
  - Done 2026-11-07 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 18): a griffe extension reads a package whose `__init__` hands `_LazyModule` `define_import_structure(...)` as transformers' runtime does: every name its folder's modules list in `__all__` or decorate `@requires`, as an import from the module that lists it unless the package already binds it, and without the `define_import_structure` helper the module replacement drops. On the published index, `transformers.SmolLM3ForCausalLM` and t5gemma's classes read present from 4.53.0 (from 5.0.0), 4.48-4.51's unresolved `Pop2PianoTokenizer` and five other top-level classes resolve, and the 289-541 `define_import_structure` paths per release are gone; transformers' spans fall from 65,234 to 63,965 as runs join. S1 on transformers: 0.0% sampled (0/250), 15 of 58,438 runtime names (from 257; `bench/s1/results-2026-11-07-transformers.md`); the ten stay at 1.2%; both benchmarks, S2 and the golden tests are unchanged. instructor's provider names, computed at import time, moved to DT-DATA-51.
- [ ] **DT-DATA-51**: Names computed at import time (instructor 1.17's provider factories) · `P2` `M`
  - Why: instructor 1.17's `v2/__init__.py` lists `*PUBLIC_FACTORY_ATTRS.keys()` in `__all__`, a mapping a comprehension builds from provider specs in another module, and its `providers/<name>/__init__.py` files forward every attribute with `__getattr__ = make_getattr("openai", ("client", ...))`. S1's instructor misses (6.3% sampled, 9.7% of runtime names) are these.
  - AC: read the two shapes statically (a comprehension over literal specs; a module `__getattr__` made by a helper of the package that forwards to named modules) and re-measure instructor in S1.
  - Looked at 2026-11-08 (1.17.0): `PUBLIC_FACTORY_ATTRS` is a comprehension over `PROVIDER_SPECS`, a literal mapping of `_spec(...)` and `_openai_compat_spec(...)` calls whose `from_function` and `client_module` come from keyword arguments or from the helper's own body, so reading it means evaluating those calls with their defaults. Left out of the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 23 batch for that reason.
  - Left out of the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 24 batch too (2026-11-09), for the same reason. Next step: evaluate a call to a helper of the package's own whose arguments are literals, binding its keyword defaults and the literal assignments in its body, and only that; the 2026-11c holdout scores instructor as it stands.
  - 2026-11d holdout: chromadb 0.5 and 0.6's `utils.embedding_functions` imports each of its submodules and writes their embedding-function classes into `globals()`, so `SentenceTransformerEmbeddingFunction` reads missing there (2 false positives for each tool, one upgrade).
- [x] **DT-DATA-52**: Names that still work through runtime shims: module aliases and keywords taken by `**kwargs` · `P2` `M`
  - Why: from the 2026-11 holdout. fastmcp 3.2-3.4 registers `fastmcp.tools.tool` in `sys.modules` as an alias of `fastmcp.tools.base`, so imports of it work through 3.4.11, though the file went in 3.2 (the boundary DepTime gives is earlier); `transformers.tokenization_utils` and its `PreTrainedTokenizer` still import in 5.x, while `diff_versions` reads them removed; pydantic-ai's `Agent(instrument=)` left the signature in 1.95 but `**_deprecated_kwargs` took it until 2.0, so `explain_error` says last 1.94.0 where the case says 1.107.
  - Also the replacement misses there: `eval_strategy` for transformers' `evaluation_strategy`, huggingface_hub's `split_torch_state_dict_into_shards` for `shard_checkpoint` (both in the deprecation text), mcp 2's snake_case `input_schema` for `inputSchema`, gradio's `buttons` (got `copy`).
  - Since DT-CORE-35 (2026-11-07), fastmcp's two `No module named 'fastmcp.tools.tool'` cases read `module_removed` at 3.1.1, the last release with the file; with the alias they'd read 3.4.11 and pass.
  - Done so far (2026-11-07, `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 19-20): `sys.modules[alias] = <module>` at a module's top level (in `if` and `try` blocks too; the key a literal, `__name__`, an f-string of those or a sum; the value another `sys.modules[...]` or a module the file imported) is recorded per release as `releases.module_aliases`, path -> module, and so is a submodule whose name its package rebinds (DT-DATA-54). `explain_error` reads a module importable at the installed release as present, and counts those releases for one that later became only a name: fastmcp's two cases read `module_removed` at 3.4.11 and pass. The format-20 index has aliases in 3,313 releases of 25 packages. Left: keywords a function hands to a helper as a dict (pydantic-ai 1.95-1.107's `consume_deprecated_instrument(_deprecated_kwargs, 'Agent')` pops `instrument`), transformers 5's `tokenization_utils`, and the replacement misses above.
  - AC: snapshots record `sys.modules[...] = ` aliases a package's `__init__` makes, and keywords a function hands to a helper that consumes them by name; the fastmcp and pydantic-ai boundaries and the transformers module match the runtime.
  - Done 2026-11-08 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 22, live in build-index 42515969306): a function that hands its **kwargs mapping to another function as an argument (`_utils.consume_deprecated_instrument(_deprecated_kwargs, 'Agent')`) takes the deprecations of the names that function looks up in it, read as its own **kwargs, with the literals the call passes filling in the message; a helper that takes out one name and warns after an early return (`if 'instrument' not in deprecated_kwargs: return None`) reads as deprecated as a whole, and that warning is the name's. Only names taken out of the mapping count: pandas' `SeriesApply(kwargs=kwargs)` deprecates its own `convert_dtype` parameter, which a caller's **kwargs never reaches. Radius on the 17-release sample plus pydantic-ai-slim 1.100.0: pydantic-ai's `Agent.__init__` and `run*` methods gain `instrument`, `builtin_tools`, `output_retries`, `prepare_tools`, `prepare_output_tools` and `event_stream_handler` (126 names on 52 records), langchain-community 0.3.31's `DatabricksVectorSearch` search methods `filters` (12); nothing else changes. On a local index of pydantic-ai-slim 1.94.0-2.23.0 the `instrument` case passes (accepted up to 1.107.10, deprecated in 1.95.0, gone in 2.0.0). On the format-22 index the benchmark's `instrument` case passes (`param_removed`, last 1.107.10). Left: transformers 5's legacy module aliases, served by a `sys.meta_path` finder from a dict (`_LEGACY_MODULE_ALIASES`), and `Qwen2TokenizerFast`, served by its `_LazyModule`; reading them would mean reading transformers' own machinery.
  - Code done 2026-11-08 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 23, with DT-DATA-45's `bound` names): transformers' remainder is read from coverage.toml rules (`[[package.served]]`), since its machinery is written three ways across 5.x (`_create_tokenization_alias` in 5.0, `_create_module_alias` and a loop over `image_processing_*.py` in 5.4, `_LegacyModuleAliasFinder` with `_LEGACY_MODULE_ALIASES` in 5.21). A rule holds in a release whose named file matches its regular expression, checked on all 46 releases from 4.57.3 to 5.22.2: each matches none of the 4.57 releases and every release from the one that brought the code. What a rule serves is bound like DT-DATA-45's imports, so it counts as public only where some release had it as public API: `XTokenizerFast` as `XTokenizer` in `transformers`, `transformers.models` and each model package (5.0 on), `XImageProcessorFast` likewise (5.4 on), `tokenization_utils`, `tokenization_utils_fast` and `image_processing_utils_fast` as their replacements with every name those bind, and `models.x.image_processing_y_fast` as `image_processing_y`, serving `XImageProcessorFast` too (5.4 on). On 4.57.6, 5.0.0, 5.4.0 and 5.22.2, `removed` events for `*TokenizerFast` fall from 207 to 86 in 5.0, `*ImageProcessorFast` from 391 to 27 in 5.4, the `_fast` modules from 2,259 to 31; `check_symbols` reads `Qwen2TokenizerFast`, `tokenization_utils.PreTrainedTokenizer` and `models.vit.image_processing_vit_fast.ViTImageProcessorFast` present in 5.22.2 and `diff_versions` no longer reports them. Of the 86 left, 60 are classes of the `tokenization_*_fast` modules 5.0 removed; the other 26 are ten models' `XTokenizerFast` that `_LazyModule` serves through `SLOW_TO_FAST_CONVERTERS` (DT-DATA-58); what stays removed is removed (`tokenization_utils.Trie`, the `tokenization_*_fast` modules, 5.4.0's `BlipImageProcessorFast`, whose fallback name `image_processing_blip` doesn't define).
  - From the 2026-11b holdout: transformers 5.22 still serves `Qwen2TokenizerFast` (both tools read it removed), and 13 replacements are missed, most stated in release notes, migration guides or deprecation text: transformers 5's `StaticCache` and `AutoModelForImageTextToText`, `torch.optim.AdamW`, `langchain_classic`, mcp 2's `Client`, dspy's `ChainOfThought`, huggingface_hub's `get_token`, sqlalchemy's `get_table_names`, matplotlib's `sharex`/`sharey`; pydantic-ai's `MCPServerHTTP` gets `MCPServerSSE`, not `MCPToolset`.
  - Live 2026-11-09 (build-index 42560611346, rows as its dry run 42543976365's): transformers 5.0's `removed` events for `*TokenizerFast` fall from 207 to 86 and 5.4's for `*ImageProcessorFast` from 391 to 30; 5.4.0's `removed` events from 3,249 to 1,229 in all. `Qwen2TokenizerFast`, `tokenization_utils.PreTrainedTokenizer`, `image_processing_utils_fast.BaseImageProcessorFast` and `models.vit.image_processing_vit_fast.ViTImageProcessorFast` read present from their first release to 5.22.2. Electra's, LED's, Longformer's, Lxmert's and Mvp's `XTokenizerFast` in their model packages now follow the `XTokenizer` there, so they read removed in 5.18 with it. Holdout re-runs: 2026-11's transformers false positives 2 → 0, 2026-11b's 1 → 0. Left: the 26 `SLOW_TO_FAST_CONVERTERS` paths (DT-DATA-58); the replacement misses above move to DT-CORE-40.
- [x] **DT-DATA-59**: A re-export of a submodule name its package rebinds follows the submodule · `P1` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11c holdout, its one missed break (both tools): polars 1.9 code run on 0.19.6, `pl.int_range(n)`, which needs `end` there. `polars/functions/range/__init__.py` binds `int_range` to the function its submodule `int_range` defines, and DT-DATA-54 reads `polars.functions.range.int_range` as that function. But `polars.functions.int_range` and `polars.int_range`, which import it from there, read as the module (from 0.19.1), so they have no signature. 17 polars paths read this way, among them `col` (0.19.2 to 2.1.0), `lit`, `len`, `repeat`, `date_range` and `linear_space`, so `check_symbols` can't check their keywords. Nine paths in five other packages (pinecone, gradio, sqlalchemy, litellm, langchain-openai) have the same shape; some may be right (`sqlalchemy.orm.mapperlib` is the module `sqlalchemy.orm.mapper`).
  - AC: an import of a name from a package that rebinds it binds the object the package binds, as DT-DATA-54 does for the package's own path; `polars.int_range` has 0.19.6's signature and the case's `int_range(_)` is flagged; the other nine checked against the runtime.
  - Code done 2026-11-09 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 25, with DT-DATA-61 and DT-DATA-62): an import from outside the package of a submodule's name the package rebinds binds the object the package binds; one inside the package keeps the module, since it may run before the rebinding (sqlalchemy 2.0's `from . import mapper as mapperlib`). On the real wheels: polars 0.19.6's `int_range` has `(start, end, step=1, ...)`, and `check_symbols` reads the case's `int_range(_)` missing `end`; `lit`, `len`, `repeat`, `date_range`, `linear_space` and the other polars paths get their function's signature (`col` is an instance, `polars.functions.col.col`, as its own path reads); gradio 6.31's `cli.custom_component` and `cli.commands.custom_component` read as the `Typer` instance and `cli.sketch` as the `launch` function, langchain-openai 1.7.4's `custom_tool`, litellm 1.108.4's `compress` and pinecone 5.4.2's and 8.1.2's three re-exports as functions, all as the runtime has them; `sqlalchemy.orm.mapperlib` stays the module.
  - Live 2026-11-10 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 25, build-index 42761344010 on `e438429`, 212 minutes cold; 6,766 releases, xz 49.9 MB; rows as its dry run 42737122271's but for the two releases uploaded during it and DT-CORE-44's and 45's hints): polars has 63 fewer spans and 75 fewer events, its rebound names reading as their functions; numpy's `memmap` and `printoptions` (and `numpy.matlib`'s) read as the class and the function, as at runtime, not as `numpy._core`'s modules (57 more events, `memmap`'s members); gradio, pinecone, litellm and langchain-openai lose 1-5 events each. `check_symbols` reads the 2026-11c case's `int_range(_)` missing `end` (the holdout's one missed break; re-run, 124 of 124 breaks found). `diff_versions` returns the events that say so (`end` and `start` optional after 0.19), but the pre-registered rule reads a parameter that became required only going up, so 1.9 → 0.19.6 still counts as missed there (DT-QA-10). TimeMachine-bench, S1 (1.2%), S2 (42 of 50), S3 (100%), the golden tests (82) and the curated hints (328, no problems) are unchanged; p95 10.6 ms, as on the format-24 index on the same machine.
- [x] **DT-DATA-60**: pydantic 2's names served by `getattr_migration` · `P1` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11c holdout: 8 of its 15 false positives. pydantic 2 keeps v1 modules such as `pydantic.error_wrappers` as `__getattr__ = getattr_migration(__name__)`. That serves each name in `MOVED_IN_V2` (`'pydantic.error_wrappers:ValidationError': 'pydantic:ValidationError'`) with a warning that it moved, `DEPRECATED_MOVED_IN_V2` and `REDIRECT_TO_V1` likewise, and raises `PydanticImportError` for `REMOVED_IN_V2`. The index sees only the module's `__getattr__`, so `pydantic.error_wrappers.ValidationError` reads missing after 1.10.27 in `check_symbols` (4) and removed in 2.0 in `diff_versions` (4).
  - AC: a `[[package.served]]` rule reads the tables in `pydantic/_migration.py` (keys `module:name`); a served name reads present and deprecated, with its target as the replacement, and a `REMOVED_IN_V2` name reads removed, in the releases whose source has them; the case's 8 uses read right.
  - Code done 2026-11-09 (with the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 25 batch): a fourth `served` shape, a `table` of import paths without `modules`, serves each key (`module:name`) as the path its value names, in a release that has the key's module and doesn't bind the name, with `message` as its deprecation (`{key}` and `{value}` as the table writes them). Three pydantic rules read `MOVED_IN_V2` ("`{key}` has been moved to `{value}`."), `DEPRECATED_MOVED_IN_V2` (the deprecated object's own warning; 2.0-2.0.2 spread a comprehension of v1's `pydantic.utils` names into it) and `REDIRECT_TO_V1` (2.0.3 on; its first sentence, the same in all 62 releases from 2.0.3 to 2.14.3, whose link changed in 2.14). The miner reads `module:name` as `module.name`, and "importing from X instead" (0.8); of the live index's 3,771 deprecation texts none changes. On pydantic 1.10.26, 2.0, 2.0.3, 2.10.3 and 2.14.3: `pydantic.error_wrappers.ValidationError` reads present and deprecated from 2.0 ("has been moved to `pydantic:ValidationError`", hint `pydantic.ValidationError`), `utils.to_camel` as `alias_generators.to_pascal`, `validate_arguments`, `tools.parse_obj_as` and `class_validators.validator` as the deprecated functions, `utils.deep_update` from `pydantic.v1.utils`; `PyObject` reads removed in 2.0 and served again from 2.0.3, as pydantic's tables have it.
  - Live 2026-11-10 (the same build): pydantic gains 28 spans of served names, present and deprecated from 2.0 (`PyObject` from 2.0.3). The curated hints for `error_wrappers.ValidationError`, `generics.GenericModel` and `decorator.validate_arguments` give way to the same targets from pydantic's own messages (0.85), as a curated hint does when the library states it. The 2026-11c re-run loses its 8 pydantic false positives (4 for each tool).

A release with no code for its import names that no same-version pin provides (DT-DATA-17) is analysed from the first distribution it pins with `==` at another version that has the code; a pin to a project PyPI doesn't have is skipped. A shim's warning may come from a helper named as one (`WARNING_HELPER_RE`) with `message=`, as the function-body reader already allowed: polars' `issue_deprecation_warning(message=..., version=...)`. A lazy loader's `__getattr__, __dir__, __all__ = _attach(__name__, submod_attrs=TABLE)` (scientific-python's `lazy_loader`, which huggingface_hub 0.12-0.27 copies) serves the names the module-level literal table lists, each from its submodule; griffe lists no `__getattr__` bound by unpacking, so the module's AST summary records it. - [x] **DT-DATA-67**: A function whose body only raises reads as a working function · `P2` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11d holdout: llama-index-core 0.14's `ServiceContext.from_defaults` is a classmethod taking `**kwargs` whose body only raises "ServiceContext is deprecated", and the probe labels calls to it as breaks (an object built to fail when called, DT-QA-10). `check_symbols` misses all 3 uses and `diff_versions` 1. openai 1.x's `APIRemovedInV1Proxy` for `ChatCompletion` is the same idea (2026-11c).
  - AC: a function or method whose body, after its docstring, is a single `raise` of anything but `NotImplementedError` is recorded as raising, with its message: `check_symbols` says so (not plain `present`), `diff_versions` reports the change to a raising stub, and `explain_error` can quote the message.
  - Code done 2026-11-10 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 26), but for `explain_error` (split out as DT-CORE-49): a function or method whose body after its docstring is a single `raise` records `raises` (`Type: message`, the type alone when the message isn't a literal), unless it is abstract, an overload, or raises a subclass-must-implement error (`NotImplementedError`, pandas' `AbstractMethodError`); only a source with one `raise` is parsed, which keeps a pandas 2.1.1 snapshot within 1 s of its time without it. New nullable `raises` columns on `spans` and `members` (older clients ignore them; a reader of an older index gets NULL), a new event kind `now_raises` (severity next to `param_removed`) when a callable becomes such a stub, and `SymbolCheck.raises`. The holdout scorer counts a call to one as flagged by `check_symbols`, and a `now_raises` on the called path, upgrading, by `diff_versions`. On real wheels: llama-index-core 0.14.25 has 6, all real (`ServiceContext.from_defaults` and `__init__`, `set_global_service_context`, `LLMPredictor.__init__` "This class is deprecated. Use any LLM class directly.", `VectorMemory.get_all`); pandas 2.1.1 one, `Index.sort` "cannot sort an Index object in-place, use sort_values instead".
  - Live 2026-11-10 (the same build): 67 spans and 487 members record `raises`; 54 `now_raises` events in 12 packages (transformers 11, matplotlib 10, llama-index-core 9). The dry run had 68: 14 were a message that changed in a callable that already raised (transformers 4.53's `EnvironmentError` → `OSError`), which `a1f1563` (assembly only) no longer reports. The 2026-11d re-run finds the llama-index-core calls; reading `ServiceContext.from_defaults` without calling it, which the probe labelled removed, still reads present.

Members of `_pytest`'s classes are a few hundred classes. Both need a `params` column on the internal tables and `explain_error`'s parameter logic to read them, so it waits for the next `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` batch rather than hold up format 26.

A property's deprecation is now read from its getter's body, as a function's is. urllib3's 41 releases rebuilt on the live index: `Retry.DEFAULT_METHOD_WHITELIST` is a member to 1.26.20 with "Using 'Retry.DEFAULT_METHOD_WHITELIST' is deprecated and will be removed in v2.0. …", and the case reads `symbol_removed`, last 1.26.20, `urllib3<=1.26.20`, `DEFAULT_ALLOWED_METHODS` second in its replacements: the first is `Retry.DEFAULT_METHODS_ALLOWED`, from 1.26.0-1.26.5's own misspelt message, which ties at 0.85 and sorts first (unqualified targets sort before qualified ones; noted for DT-CORE-48). Reading getters adds `deprecated` events for real property deprecations: urllib3 +6 (`NewConnectionError.pool`, `Retry.BACKOFF_MAX`), pandas +57 (`GroupBy.grouper` in 2.2, `DatetimeIndex.dayofweek` in 3.1, `is_monotonic` in 1.5).

It serves more than the other 12: the `XTokenizer` names too, for Electra, LED, LayoutLM, Longformer, Lxmert and Mvp, at the top level and in `transformers.models` from 5.0 and in their model packages from 5.18, where the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 23 index reads them removed; and mt5's `T5Tokenizer` and `T5TokenizerFast` in `transformers.models.mt5`. 38 paths in 5.22.2.

On 4.57.6, 5.0.0, 5.17.0, 5.18.0 and 5.22.2, the six models' 36 paths read present from 5.0.0 to 5.22.2, as at runtime, and nothing else changes. Their 122 `removed` events in 5.0 become their members compared with `BertTokenizer`'s or `RobertaTokenizer`'s, as with DT-DATA-52's served names, and `RobertaTokenizer`'s own member events move from the label `transformers.BartTokenizer` to `transformers.LEDTokenizer`, now its shortest public path. mt5's two read present from 5.0, since 4.26-4.47 had them as public API. Bloom's, Realm's, RetriBert's and MT5's top-level names stay removed, as at runtime. The 48 `moved` hints from the top-level names to their model packages (`transformers.ElectraTokenizer` → `transformers.models.electra.ElectraTokenizer`) are gone, and the removed `tokenization_*` modules' classes now point at the served names (`models.electra.tokenization_electra.ElectraTokenizer` → `transformers.ElectraTokenizer`). - [x] **DT-DATA-53**: Deprecations a module `__getattr__` raises when a public name is read · `P2` `S`
  - Why: the only two names on the 2026-11 holdout that warn when read, matplotlib's `rcsetup.interactive_bk` and weaviate's top-level `AuthApiKey`, aren't flagged deprecated by either tool. DT-DATA-22 keeps the warning only for private names a module `__getattr__` serves.

  - Done 2026-11-07 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 20): a module-level `class __getattr__` (matplotlib's `@_api.caching_module_getattr`) serves each public property it defines, with the deprecation its `deprecated(...)` decorator states; a static or class method of a class the module imports counts as a warning helper, its arguments looked up in the module's tables first (weaviate's `_Warnings.root_module_import(name, map_[name])`); and a served name a stub or a type-checking import already recorded takes the warning. On the real wheels, matplotlib 3.10.0's `rcsetup.interactive_bk`, `non_interactive_bk` and `all_backends` read "deprecated in 3.9. Use `matplotlib.backends.backend_registry.list_builtin(...)` instead", and weaviate 4.14.1's top-level `AuthApiKey` "Dep010: Importing AuthApiKey from weaviate is deprecated. Import AuthApiKey from its module: weaviate.auth". Measured on the next holdout (DT-QA-08).
  - Fix after the format-20 builds (013ac72, no format bump): the method lookup crashed (`TypeError`) on a helper called through a module alias (sqlalchemy's `_util.warn_deprecated(...)`), so sqlalchemy 2.0.24-2.0.40 and transformers 4.54.0-4.55.4 had no snapshot on the dry run and the first main build, which kept their format-18 rows; both builds were green, so a build's log is now checked for `FAILED` and carried-over releases before it is trusted.
- [x] **DT-DATA-55**: A base class imported through the package's own re-export of another distribution's class · `P1` `S`
  - Why: on the format-20 dry run, langchain-community 0.4's `BasePDFLoader(BaseLoader)` imports `BaseLoader` from its own `document_loaders.base`, which imports it from langchain-core. griffe finds that alias inside the package, so the base isn't recorded as external (`ClassRecord.external` is only `abc.ABC`), and the MRO skips it: `PyPDFLoader.load` and `AsyncHtmlLoader.load` read missing (3 of the 11 false alarms left on the 2026-11 re-run).
  - AC: a base that resolves to an alias griffe can't follow is recorded by the alias's target path; those three read present through langchain-core; next `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` build.
  - On the 2026-11b holdout (2026-11-08): 10 of `check_symbols`' 38 false positives and 2 of `diff_versions`' 5 (`PyPDFLoader.load`, `ObsidianLoader.load`; `TextLoader.load` reads missing too), so `P1`.
  - Done 2026-11-08 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 21-22, live in build-index 42515969306): `_external_bases` follows a base that is an alias inside the package to the path it names, and records that path when its package wasn't loaded (a name followed into the package itself, such as one a module `__getattr__` serves, isn't another distribution's). On real wheels (langchain-community 0.3.31 and 0.4.2 with langchain-core 1.7.6): `BasePDFLoader`, `ObsidianLoader` and `TextLoader` record `langchain_core.document_loaders.BaseLoader`, and `PyPDFLoader.load`, `ObsidianLoader.load`, `TextLoader.load` and `AsyncHtmlLoader.load` read present, checked in langchain-core 1.7.6. On the 17-release sample, external bases change for 336 langchain-community classes, 88 sqlalchemy classes (`typing_extensions.Protocol` and `TypedDict` through `sqlalchemy.util.typing`) and one numpy class (`distutils.ccompiler.CCompiler`); no MRO changes. A `typing_extensions` base read as a distribution DepTime can't check, which would have turned names those 88 classes lack from `missing` to `unknown`, as it already did for the 7,373 classes of the format-20 index with such a base (openai's and mistralai's TypedDicts). The query now reads `typing_extensions.X` as `typing.X`, and a TypedDict base as `dict`, which it is at runtime (no change on either holdout). On the format-22 index, 36,279 classes record an external base (35,202 before), and the 2026-11b holdout's langchain-community false positives go from 10 to 0.
- [x] **DT-DATA-56**: mcp 2's `mcp.types`, a star import of the `mcp-types` distribution · `P1` `M`
  - Why: from the 2026-11b holdout. mcp 2.x's `mcp/types/__init__.py` is `from mcp_types import *` (with `__all__`), mirroring the separate `mcp-types` distribution, which isn't covered. So the index has no `mcp.types.Tool`, `TextContent`, `CallToolResult` after 1.30.0: `check_symbols` reads them missing (16 false positives in one upgrade), and `diff_versions` and `symbol_history` say they were removed in 2.0.0, which anyone upgrading an MCP server to 2.x would be told.
  - AC: names a module star-imports from another distribution are recorded as an alias to it (the module and its `__all__`); with `mcp-types` covered, `mcp.types.X` resolves through it at the newest release mcp's requirement allows (as DT-CORE-32 does for aliases), and with the target not covered the answer is `unknown`, never `missing` or `removed`; mcp 1.x → 2.x reports only real changes (`Tool.inputSchema` → `input_schema`).
  - Measured first (2026-11-08, the 53 packages' newest wheels): four packages star-import another distribution's module without a literal `__all__`: mcp (`mcp.types` and its `jsonrpc`, `methods`, `version` from `mcp_types`), google-generativeai (`protos` from google-ai-generativelanguage), weaviate-client (`agents` from `weaviate_agents`) and matplotlib (`pylab` from numpy). Every mcp 2.x release pins `mcp-types` at its own version (2.0.0 to 2.3.0).
  - Done 2026-11-08 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 21-22, live in build-index 42515969306): a distribution the release pins at its own version that has a module its code star-imports is analysed with it, in one griffe collection, so griffe expands the star import and the names get the sibling's records (`ApiSnapshot.sibling_files`), recorded only where the release binds them; mcp-types itself isn't covered. A star import of code nobody read is recorded per public module (`ApiSnapshot.star_sources`, `releases.star_sources`), and `check_symbols` asks that module for a name the index doesn't list under it, in the covered package at the newest release the requirement allows (matplotlib's `pylab` in numpy), else `unknown`; `explain_error`'s `never_existed` says so, at `low`. A name removed in a release that comes back under the same parent in another case or with other underscores is a replacement at 0.8 (`renamed_names`, as DT-CORE-24 does for parameters). On the real wheels (mcp 1.29.0, 1.30.0, 2.0.0, 2.0.1, 2.3.0): mcp 2.3.0 has 270 names under `mcp.types` (none before), `Tool` the class `mcp_types._types.Tool` with its members; at 2.0.0, 70 `removed` events under `mcp.types` instead of 450, all real (`CallToolResult.isError`, `Tool.inputSchema`, `Content`, `Cursor`), and 6 top-level `kind_changed` instead of 57 (mcp's re-exports read as aliases before); 59 renames mined (`Tool.inputSchema` → `input_schema`, `McpError` → `MCPError`), and `explain_error` on `'Tool' object has no attribute 'inputSchema'` at 2.3.0 gives `input_schema`. google-generativeai 0.8.6's `protos.Part` and `protos.HarmCategory` read `unknown`, no longer `missing`. On the live index's events the rename rule pairs 360 names, mostly streamlit's generated protobuf stubs (163), mistralai (73) and transformers (60); a sample of 40 were all real renames (pandas `Groupby` → `GroupBy`, dspy `JsonAdapter` → `JSONAdapter`). On the format-22 index, mcp 2.3.0's `mcp.types.Tool`, `TextContent` and `CallToolResult` read present, the 2026-11b holdout's 16 mcp false positives go to 0, and the two `inputSchema` benchmark cases get `input_schema`.

- [x] **DT-DATA-54**: A submodule name its package rebinds is the object, still importable as the module · `P1` `S`
  - Why: after `from .patch import patch` in instructor's `__init__`, `instructor.patch` is the function, but griffe lists the submodule, so the index had a module there, with no signature: `check_symbols` couldn't see that 0.3's `patch()` requires `client`. 282 module paths of the format-18 index have a same-named function or class child (litellm 61, polars 31, numpy 21; not all are rebound).
  - Done 2026-11-07 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 20): the walker records what an explicit import, or a star import whose module exports the name, binds the name to, first, then the submodule's names under the same path, since `import instructor.patch` still finds the module, and adds the path to `releases.module_aliases`. `explain_error` reads a module importable at the installed release as present. On the format-20 dry run: instructor 0.3.4's `patch` is the function `(client)`, so the 2026-11 holdout's `instructor.patch(create=, mode=)` break is flagged by both tools; fastmcp 3.1.1-3.4.11's `fastmcp.tools.tool` is the `tool` function and importable as the module, so its two traceback cases pass.
- [x] **DT-DATA-06**: Stdlib module availability per Python version (typeshed `stdlib/VERSIONS`) · `P1` `S`
  - AC: `No module named 'distutils'` on Python 3.12 reads `module_removed` with 3.11 as the last version with it; a module newer than the Python in use reads not yet added; a standard module the Python has isn't called a version change.
  - Done 2026-11-05: `src/deptime/stdlib_versions.txt` holds the module lines of typeshed's `stdlib/VERSIONS` at `e2609bd` (2026-10-27, Apache 2.0, credited in the file), less the three it marks as absent at runtime (`_typeshed`, `sys._monitoring`, `sys.__jit`); `stdlib.py` reads it at query time, so the index doesn't change. A missing module takes the lifetime of its longest listed prefix (`distutils.util` is `distutils`'). The Python version is `"python"` in `installed_versions` or a freeze, else the version every frame path names (`lib/python3.12/`, `python3.13t`, Windows' `Python312\`, the Microsoft Store's `Python.3.12_`, and IPython's and JupyterLab's frame lines), reported as the new `signal.python_version`; it is found in 79 of the 228 benchmark tracebacks. A finding has `package` `python`, the versions as `3.11`/`3.12`, `pin_to_restore` `python<=3.11` or `python>=3.11`, and `high` confidence; without a version, the module's change at `medium`, and a module added by 3.9 (the oldest typeshed supports) is taken as present. A module the Python has gets a note instead (platform-only, or built without its system library: `_lzma`, `_sqlite3`), and so does a submodule typeshed doesn't list under a parent that's there. When a library's code makes the import, the explanation says so. `ImportError`s for names inside standard modules (`collections.Mapping`) are still `not_covered`, with a note (DT-DATA-47). Five real cases, collected after the fix from GitHub issues and scored once (`bench/cases/python.jsonl`: distutils ×2 and imp ×2 on 3.12, cgi on 3.13 in IPython), all pass; no other case changes.
  - Gap: typeshed no longer lists modules removed before about 3.9 (`formatter`, `parser`, `symbol`, `macpath`, `dummy_threading`), so those still read `not_covered`.
- [x] **DT-DATA-47**: Names inside standard modules per Python version · `P1` `M` · deps: DATA-06
  - Why: many real breaks are removed stdlib names, not modules: `collections.Mapping` (3.10), `inspect.getargspec` and `asyncio.coroutine` (3.11), `configparser.SafeConfigParser`, `ssl.wrap_socket`, `pkgutil.ImpImporter`, `locale.format` and the `unittest` aliases (3.12). typeshed's stubs guard them with `sys.version_info` checks.
  - AC: `cannot import name 'Mapping' from 'collections'` on Python 3.10 reads `symbol_removed` with 3.9 as the last version with it; the names come from typeshed's stubs read statically, never from importing.
  - Done 2026-11-06 (no index change): `src/deptime/stdlib_names.txt`, built by `tools/stdlib_names.py` from typeshed's stubs read with `ast`, lists the 1,327 module-level names whose presence changes between Python 3.9 and 3.15, with the minor versions that have each. typeshed's commit `e2609bd` (the one `stdlib_versions.txt` comes from) models 3.10 and later, so 3.9 comes from `e8ba06f` (2025-08-24), the last commit that modelled it, and a change between 3.9 and 3.10 counts only where that commit's own version checks show it. Stub rules decide what is public (definitions, `as` re-exports, star imports through the source's `__all__`, `__all__`); platform checks count both ways. Checked against the standard library of CPython 3.10, 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 (never an indexed package), 15 names were present where the stubs show none, so they are left out: typeshed drops some deprecated names a module `__getattr__` still serves (`sqlite3.version` in 3.12 and 3.13), dates some late (`io.text_encoding` is 3.10, not 3.11), and some platform constants (`os.O_EXEC` on macOS). A removed name whose object another public module still exports in the first version without it points there, nearest path first: the 26 `collections` ABC aliases give `collections.abc.*` as a replacement and a same-object `fix` at 0.75. `explain_error` answers an `ImportError` or `module ... has no attribute` for such a name like a standard module (DT-DATA-06): `symbol_removed` or `symbol_not_yet_added` with `package` `python`, versions `3.9`/`3.10`, `pin_to_restore` `python<=3.9` or `python>=3.11`, `high` with a Python version and `medium` without; a name the Python has gets a note (shadowing, platform). The Python version also comes from the file an `ImportError` names (`(/usr/lib/python3.10/collections/__init__.py)`). Six real cases, collected after the fix from GitHub issues and scored once (`bench/cases/python.jsonl`: `collections.Mapping` on 3.10, `asyncio.coroutine` ×2 on 3.11, `configparser.SafeConfigParser` on 3.13, `ssl.wrap_socket` and `pkgutil.ImpImporter` on 3.12), all pass, and the Mapping case gets its replacement: 202 real cases, root cause + boundary 98.5%, replacement 68.5% (57.6% without curated hints); no other case changes.
  - Left: names removed in 3.8 or earlier (`threading.Thread.isAlive`, `base64.encodestring`), which typeshed no longer models; class members (`unittest.TestCase.assertEquals`, `pathlib.Path.link_to`), which an `AttributeError` names through an instance (DT-CORE-02's shape); and the replacements What's New gives for removals without a same-object path (DT-DATA-48).
