A hint applies to every path of the same object (a re-export, an inherited method); a pair the library's own text already gives at ≥ 0.75 keeps that source. ADR 0006 now sets mechanical checks in place of reading each hint by hand: the quote is verbatim at its URL (`pytest -m network tests/test_curated.py`), names both the old name and its replacement (offline test), and the index has both (`bench/curated_check.py`, run by `bench.yml`). Running them changed 6 quotes and 9 URLs. Assembly only: build-index run 41954502966 (38 minutes, 6,597 releases, xz 70.9 MB) published 1,103 curated rows, the same as a local build, and the benchmarks and S2 match the local scores case by case. Real replacement 56.1% → 67.1% (9 cases: flask `escape`, marshmallow `pass_many`, mcp `McpError`, pandas `error_bad_lines`, three pytest arguments (two hooks' `path`/`startdir` and `skip(msg)`), selenium `desired_capabilities`, starlette `route`), the holdout 68.2% → 77.3% (numpy `NaN`, pandas `error_bad_lines`), S2 32 → 42 of 50. Guides are where many cases' evidence comes from too, so the benchmark and S2 also report the figure without curated hints (56.1%, 68.2%, 32 of 50).
- [ ] **DT-DATA-09**: Pydantic model fields treated as `__init__` kwargs · `P2` `M`
  - Why: a pydantic model without an `__init__` of its own takes its fields as keywords (langchain's chat models, openai's types, pydantic-ai's settings), but the index has no signature for it: `check_symbols` can't check the keywords, and `diff_versions` can't say a field became required, which breaks every call that leaves it out.
  - Approach (looked at 2026-11-08): give such a class an `__init__` made from the annotated fields of the classes in its MRO up to `pydantic.BaseModel`: keyword-only parameters, required where there's no default (`Field(...)`, or `Field()` without `default` or `default_factory`), named by `Field(alias=)` where there's one. Keep a `**data` parameter unless the MRO sets `extra='forbid'` and has no base from another distribution, whose fields the snapshot can't see: pydantic's default `extra='ignore'` drops an unknown keyword silently, so a removed field then reads `param_removed` with `absorbed_by_var_keyword`, a change in behaviour and not an error.
  - Left out of the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 24 batch (2026-11-09): no benchmark case needs it yet, and two shapes need reading first. langchain's models define `__init__(self, **kwargs)` and call `super().__init__(**kwargs)`, so the signature to replace is a pass-through, not a missing one. `extra=` is set by `model_config = ConfigDict(...)`, a v1 `class Config`, or a base from another distribution, which the snapshot can't see. The 2026-11c holdout has two such calls (crewai 0.1.32's `Agent(cache=)` and `Task(expected_output=)`, labelled `works` by hand): pydantic drops the keywords they pass, which the planned `**data` keeps working.
- [x] **DT-DATA-12**: Ingestion never drops an indexed release by accident · `P0` `S`
  - AC: a failed retry, `--versions` and a PyPI outage keep already-indexed releases (from the snapshot cache, else rebuilt from the index) (done); `ingest` refuses a downloaded index without `--into-downloaded` and marks it locally built only after a rebuild (done). CI still starts each night from an empty index, so carrying releases forward there needs the previous published index as a base (see DT-DATA-03).
- [x] **DT-DATA-14**: Record each release's `Requires-Dist` · `P1` `S`
  - Why: DT-CORE-16 searches other packages for an error raised inside a library, and what that library requires says which to search first. A pin DepTime suggests could later be checked against the same specifiers.
  - Done 2026-11-05 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 15): a snapshot records the analysed artifact's `Requires-Dist`, each requirement normalized, with its extras and markers, at most 500. A wrapper release's are those of the distribution it pins (DT-DATA-17). An sdist's `PKG-INFO` counts only when it lists some: older setuptools left them to `requires.txt`, so openai 0.26.x's are unknown, while litellm's poetry sdists list theirs. The index keeps them as a JSON column on `releases`, NULL when unknown. Released clients don't read it, so the schema version stays 1. On seven real releases (openai 1.55.0 and 0.26.0, litellm 1.65.0.post1, fastmcp 4.1.6, langchain-community 0.2.0 and 0.3.27, sqlmodel 0.0.22), the symbols match the published index exactly. Filled from PyPI's JSON API for the 2,564 releases of the 17 packages that raise in benchmark cases, the column is 2.2 MB, or 24 KB under xz.
  - Verified 2026-11-05 on the first `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 15 index (build-index run 42044165626, 6,599 releases): every span, event and replacement row equals the format-14 index published that morning, and no release was added or lost. Every release has its requirements except seven openai 0.26.x sdists. The column is 7.0 MB unpacked; the `.xz` grew 0.2 MB (70.9 → 71.1), the `.gz` 0.2 MB (113.8 → 114.0).
- [x] **DT-DATA-16**: Store inherited members once per class, then index transformers again · `P1` `M` · deps: SPIKE-04
  - Why: transformers 5.21.0 has ~1.5M public paths, 87% of them inherited methods repeated across ~3,700 model classes (2026-10-28), so it was taken out of `coverage.toml`.
  - AC: snapshots record each class's own members and its bases; queries resolve `Class.member` through the bases; transformers is back in coverage with the `.gz` still under 100 MB.
  - Progress 2026-11-05: `own_members = true` in a package's coverage entry stores each class's own members once, keyed by the class's canonical path, with its MRO (tables `classes` and `members`); the package's spans then have no class members. The store looks `Class.member` up through the MRO when queried and returns rows shaped like `spans` rows, so the tools don't change. A member's events are stored once, at its defining class's label (its shortest public path), which is where `diff_versions` reports them; a subclass's member history is worked out from its states release by release, as a build would. Hints mined from a deprecation are stored at the label too, and named as the subclass's member when it has the same one.
  - Checked: snapshots built that way expand to exactly the classic ones, path for path (all 1,629,729 of transformers 5.22.1; httpx, pydantic, langchain-core, django, weaviate-client). Indexes built both ways from the same two or three releases answer `spans`, `span_at`, per-path events, hints and dotted suffix searches the same for every path (httpx, langchain-core, weaviate-client, pydantic, django's 100,487) or for a sample (transformers 5.0.0 and 5.22.1: 20,200 paths, 2,850 suffixes). Hints differ only where the classic index also kept a curated hint for a subclass beside the library's own one for the same object (pydantic's `RootModel`, django's `log_deletion`). Classic indexes are unchanged row for row, and both benchmarks case by case.
  - transformers' two releases: 1,812,296 spans and 980,360 events become 39,004 spans, 120,102 member rows, 19,077 class rows and 87,878 events (1.21 GB → 117 MB unpacked); assembly takes 7 s instead of 180 s, and lookups are as fast (dotted suffix search p95 1.8 ms). A snapshot is 2.0–2.6 MB instead of 18–37 MB.
  - An index with such a package has schema version 2, which deptime 0.1.5 and older don't load: they keep the index they have and say to upgrade (checked with 0.1.5's `index pull`). 0.1.6 (2026-11-05) reads it, and transformers went back into `coverage.toml` with `own_members = true` the same day, after DT-DATA-50 gave its Auto classes their members.
  - Verified 2026-11-06 on the published index (`build-index` run 42155950668, 176 minutes, 138 of them transformers' snapshots; the first run lost its runner to a GitHub shutdown after 10 minutes and published nothing): schema 2, 53 packages, 6,749 releases, transformers' 145 (4.26.0–5.22.1) in 65,234 spans, 178,134 member rows, 34,138 class rows and 267,482 events. The `.xz` grows from 76.2 to 90.0 MB, under 100 MB; the `.gz`, already 121 MB without transformers, is 141.9 MB. Both benchmarks are identical case by case, and the golden tests pass, among them transformers' `GPT2Tokenizer.from_pretrained` (inherited from `PreTrainedTokenizerBase`) and `AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained`. `explain_error` p95 over the benchmark tracebacks is 3.7 ms, as before, once a bare-name search reads member rows only for packages stored with own members (it scanned them for sqlalchemy's `get`: 29 ms).
- [x] **DT-DATA-17**: Follow a wrapper release to the distribution that holds its code · `P1` `S`
  - Why: since 3.3.0, `fastmcp` is an empty wheel that pins `fastmcp-slim==<same version>`, so its 31 newest releases have no snapshot (2026-10-28). typer 0.12.0 (`typer-slim`) and pinecone 2.2.2–5.0.1 (`pinecone-client`) have the same shape.
  - AC: when a release has no code for its import names and pins one distribution at the same version, ingest analyses that distribution's artifact and records it as the source file; fastmcp 3.3.0+ is indexed. A pin to a different version (dspy 0.1.5 → `dspy-ai==2.4.5`) is still reported as failed.
  - Done 2026-10-31: ingest follows the first same-version pin that has code for the import names (typer 0.12.0 pins the empty `typer-cli` before `typer-slim`), and each snapshot records the analysed artifact as `source_file`. `build-index` run 41196805868 (26 minutes, warm caches) added 48 releases: fastmcp 3.3.0–4.1.4 (31), pinecone 2.2.2–5.0.1 (16) and typer 0.12.0; 6,564 releases in 93.3 MB. dspy 0.1.5 still fails, as intended. On that index the fastmcp `fastmcp.server.proxy` case passes and no other case changes (real root cause + boundary 94.3% → 95.0%).
- [x] **DT-DATA-18**: Snapshot builds keep memory flat across releases · `P2` `S`
  - Why: building litellm's 290 releases in one process grows by ~45 MB per release and never gives it back, peaking at 11.9 GB of a 16 GB runner (2026-10-28). Only cold builds are affected; nightly runs build a few releases.
  - AC: memory after each release returns to a fixed baseline (e.g. clear griffe's caches, or build each release in a subprocess); litellm's cold build stays under 4 GB.
  - Done 2026-10-29: griffe 2.3's dataclasses extension memoizes `_dataclass_parameters` per `Class` in an unbounded `functools.cache`, and each cached class keeps its whole package tree alive through its parents. `build_snapshot` now empties griffe's module-level caches after every release. Live objects after a litellm build stay at ~19k instead of growing by ~95k per release; litellm's 100 newest releases built in one process peak at 552 MB (CI passed 5 GB ten minutes into the same build before). Snapshots are unchanged (three pydantic-ai releases rebuilt with identical symbols), so no format bump.
- [x] **DT-DATA-19**: Index density: boundaries that fall between indexed releases · `P0` `M` · data-policy decision
  - Why: older minor series are indexed only at their first and last release, so a patch-level removal or addition (chromadb `API` removed in 0.4.15; only 0.4.0 and 0.4.24 are indexed) gets the wrong boundary. 17 bench misses, the largest group (bench cases with `fixed_by` DT-DATA-19).
  - AC: measure the index size of indexing every release since 2023-01-01 (or every release for Tier A); decide; re-run the bench. Until then DT-CORE-18 keeps the wording honest.
  - Measured 2026-10-29 by building every candidate release of all 52 packages locally, over the published format 4 index: today's policy selects 3,422 releases; every release since 2023-01-01, 6,381 (16 of the 17 cases; sqlalchemy's 1.4.29 is from 2021); every release of every minor series active since 2023, 6,557 (all 17); the latter for Tier A only, 5,883 (15). The whole-series index is 93.2 MB `.gz` against 79.9 MB (both packed locally), and 955 MB unpacked: Tier A adds 13.0 MB (langchain 9.9 MB: only 2 of its 349 `0.0.x` releases were indexed, and the rest add 75,000 import paths from mid-2023; weaviate-client 1.9 MB) and Tier B 0.3 MB, so a Tier-A-only policy saves nothing. Spans split only where the API changes, so most packages grow 0–30% for 2–25× the releases. On that index all 17 cases pass and no other case changes: real cases root cause 95.0%, root cause + boundary 94.3%. A single-process cold build needed DT-DATA-18 first (langchain and litellm projected at 21–24 GB).
  - Decided 2026-10-29: every release of every minor series active since 2023 (`whole_series = true` in `coverage.toml`). It leaves ~7 MB below the 100 MB target, and the index now grows faster than before: releases older than 12 months are no longer thinned to their series' first and last. At 2023–2026 rates that's 1–5 MB more per year (the 13.3 MB covers ~2.8 years, and 9.9 MB of it is langchain's 2023), so watch the size in each build.
  - Done 2026-10-29: `build-index` run 40901087042 published it (6,508 releases, 92.9 MB, 38 minutes on warm caches; litellm built 503 more releases in 10 minutes, at most 1.1 GB in use). On it all 17 cases pass and no other case changes.
- [x] **DT-DATA-20**: Names served by a `__getattr__` deprecation shim count as present · `P0` `M`
  - Why: many libraries keep a removed name alive for a few releases through a module `__getattr__`, so static analysis dates the removal too early. Found in the shipped wheels: pydantic-ai 1.100.0 maps `'BuiltinToolCallPart': 'NativeToolCallPart'`; fastmcp 2.12.5 has `if name == "BearerAuthProvider":`; also werkzeug `__version__` (3.0) and django `timezone.utc` (4.1–4.2). 5 bench misses.
  - AC: snapshots read string literals that a module's `__getattr__` compares against or looks up (and dicts such as numpy's `__deprecated_attrs__`), record those names as present and deprecated, and take a mapped target as a replacement. Still static: never import the package (ADR 0002).
  - Done 2026-10-28: snapshots parse a module's `__getattr__` with `ast` (never imported) and follow which names reach a `return`: `name == "X"`, `name in <literal or module-level dict/set/list>`, `or`/`and` of those, and guards such as `if name != "utc": raise`. A name whose branch raises stays absent (numpy 2.0's expired attributes); conditions it can't read serve nothing; `try`, `with` and `match` bodies aren't followed (numpy 1.26's `try: __deprecated_attrs__[attr]` shape is still missed). Served names are kind `getattr` (canonical: the `__getattr__`), and real members win. Their deprecation text is the branch's `warn(...)`, with the f-string filled in from the source (the name, the module, a rename table's target), so pydantic-ai's rename map yields "use `pydantic_ai.NativeToolCallPart` instead"; a deprecation-type category counts too (django's `RemovedInDjango50Warning`). Checked on the shipped wheels: pydantic-ai-slim 1.100.0, fastmcp 2.12.5, werkzeug 3.0.6, django 4.2.16, numpy 1.26.4 and 2.0.2. On a scratch index of the boundary releases all 5 cases get the right boundary (django and pydantic-ai also the replacement); they pass once a `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 4 index is published. Latest releases of all 52 packages gain about 250 names (most: langchain-community 104 lazy `llms` imports, weaviate 24, instructor 24, litellm 23, click 22). werkzeug's "Use feature detection or …" now mines the replacement `feature` at 0.6 (DT-CORE-12).
- [x] **DT-DATA-21**: Index names re-exported from another distribution · `P0` `S`
  - Why: `flask.escape` (from markupsafe, flask ≤2.3) and `typer.clear` (from click) are absent from the index, so DepTime answers `never_existed`, a confident wrong answer. 2 bench misses; it also inflates S1's false-absent rate.

Read with `ast`. Module imports, stdlib and `typing_extensions` plain imports, renames, imports under `if`/`try`, and plain imports in subpackages stay out: probed on the first and latest indexed release of all 52 packages, subpackage plain imports were about 90 names, nearly all dependencies imported for use (`chromadb.server.fastapi.Request`, `crewai.flow.runtime.BaseModel`). What's kept is about 260 names, mostly sqlmodel (~115 per release) and typer (22). Recorded as kind `alias` with the foreign target as canonical and no signature. On a scratch index both cases get the right boundary: `flask.escape` is now present from 2.2.0 (alias), deprecated 2.3.0 (shim), removed 3.0.0; typer 0.26.0 removes `clear`, `echo_via_pager`, `edit`, `open_file`, `pause` and `unstyle`, and the click names it vendored change kind. They pass once a `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 4 index is published. flask's "Import 'markupsafe.escape' instead" isn't mined as a replacement yet (DT-CORE-06).
- [x] **DT-DATA-22**: Decide whether to index private names that users import (`flask._app_ctx_stack`, `pymongo.database._check_name`) · `P1` `S`
  - 3 bench misses, plus `flask._request_ctx_stack` in the holdout. Today they come back `never_existed`; at minimum the explanation should say private names aren't indexed. The third is an implementation import another library patched: `instructor.core.patch.handle_response_model` (a plain `from ..processing.response import ...` in instructor ≤1.15.1, wrapped by openinference).
  - 2026-11-05 (DT-QA-01): 5 of the 32 new real cases have this shape, the largest group of their misses: motor importing `pymongo.cursor._QUERY_OPTIONS`, haystack calling weaviate's `_Collections._get_all`, llama-index using weaviate's `batch_wrapper._ContextManagerWrapper`, llama-index importing `IDF_EMBEDDING_MODELS` from `qdrant_client.qdrant_fastembed` (an implementation import there from 1.14), and fastapi-pagination importing SQLAlchemy's `AsyncConnection` from sqlmodel. Integration libraries reach into these names, and their users get the errors. Raised to P1.
  - **Done 2026-11-05 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 16): indexed, apart from the public API.** Snapshots record what modules bind that isn't public: private names, imports from outside the standard library, the private names a module `__getattr__` serves (with its warning), and every name in a private module, with the module itself. Kind and canonical path only; not dunders, names only type checkers see, annotations without a value, deleted names, class members or test modules. They go into their own table, `internal_spans` (no events, so `diff_versions` is unchanged; released clients don't read it). `explain_error`, `check_symbols` and `symbol_history` read a path's public and internal spans together and flag `internal`. A removed import points to what it imported (0.75 when that's in the package and still there, 0.6 when it's another distribution's), else to the one other module that defines the same name (0.6). Missing private members of classes, and members of private classes, aren't claimed never to have existed: `low`, and the explanation says DepTime can't tell.
  - Measured first, on the latest release of all 52 packages (915,137 public paths): private definitions and non-stdlib imports in public modules add 141k paths (15.4%), private modules 43k more (20.2% in all); standard-library imports would be another 84k. Public snapshots are byte-identical on 68 releases (the latest of each package plus the cases' releases). Re-ingesting the six packages of the cases (614 releases) on the published index: 14,471 internal rows against 92,431 public spans (147 against 519 bytes a row), 4,267 more hints, all for internal paths (graded 40 at random: right). Benchmark on it: real cases root cause + boundary 93.2% → 96.9%, replacement 64.8% → 67.0% (53.8% → 56.0% without curated hints); holdout 89.7% → 93.1%; S2 identical; `explain_error` p95 3.8 ms on both. Eight of the nine cases pass: `_check_name`, `_QUERY_OPTIONS` (→ `cursor_shared`), `handle_response_model` (→ `processing.response`), `IDF_EMBEDDING_MODELS` (now removed after 1.18.0, not 1.13.3; → `fastembed_common`), sqlmodel's `AsyncConnection` (→ sqlalchemy's), `_ContextManagerWrapper`, and flask's `_app_ctx_stack` and `_request_ctx_stack`. The ninth, weaviate's `_Collections._get_all`, is a private class's inherited member (DT-DATA-46).
  - Verified 2026-11-05 on the published index (`build-index` run 42090887259, cold, 162 minutes): 262,963 internal rows in all 52 packages, and for the six packages above the same rows as the scratch index. Public spans and events are unchanged apart from two new releases (anthropic, celery). The `.xz` grows from 71.1 to 76.2 MB and the `.gz` from 114.0 to 121.1 MB. Both benchmarks, S2 and every answer are identical to the scratch index's; `explain_error` p95 is 3.9 ms. S1 stays 1.2%, and counting internal rows as present, the pre-registered rule falls from 18.2% to 1.1%. The eight cases are now expected to pass, and the weaviate case points at DT-DATA-46.
- [x] **DT-DATA-23**: Index modules in folders without `__init__.py` · `P0` `M` · deps: DATA-18, DATA-35
  - Why: griffe deliberately skips a folder of `.py` files that has no `__init__.py` ("Missing __init__ module?"), but Python imports it as a namespace package. litellm has 180 such folders in 1.106.5 (1,596 files, 24 MB of source), including all of `litellm.types` and `litellm.litellm_core_utils`, so `from litellm.types.utils import ModelResponse` looks absent. Also `flask.sansio` (3.x), `qdrant_client.embed`, selenium's generated `webdriver.common.devtools`. 39 of 52 packages have none. 1 bench miss (`litellm.llms.prompt_templates`, gone in 1.55.0).

  - Measured 2026-10-28 on litellm 1.106.5 (empty `__init__.py` written into a scratch copy): 55,645 → 306,860 symbols, snapshot 785 KB → 4.1 MB gz, 4 s → 15 s, peak 252 → 627 MB. Most of it is the bundled proxy server (`litellm.proxy`, 33k → 192k); leaving `litellm/proxy` out still gives 162k symbols (2.3 MB gz), with `litellm.types` 20k and `litellm.llms` 41k. With the cold build's per-release memory growth (DT-DATA-18), either variant would likely exhaust the runner over 290 releases, so DT-DATA-18 comes first; whether to index `litellm.proxy` is a scope decision.
  - S1 (2026-10-29): litellm is the second-worst package, at 32/205 sampled (1,370 of 7,734 runtime names). 27 of the 32 are top-level names that `litellm/__init__.py` star-imports from `litellm.types` (`OpenAIChatCompletionSystemMessage`, `ToolResourcesFileSearchVectorStore`).
  - Measured 2026-11-01 on 26 litellm releases spread over the 795 indexed, with empty `__init__.py` files written into a copy (the sample's spans and events are within 7% of the index's real counts). Loading every such folder takes 1.106.6 from 55,635 to 306,910 symbols and multiplies litellm's rows in the index by 5.4: about 6.4 → 35 MB of the `.gz`, so the index would be ~123 MB. Leaving out the folders below `litellm/proxy` gives 161,994 symbols and 2.9× (~18 MB; index ~107 MB). Nothing is lost either way. Since DT-DATA-18, memory isn't the limit: peak per release is 712 MB and 491 MB, and litellm's cold build (31 minutes, 305 MB peak) would take about 2.8× and 2.2× as long. Without the proxy, most of what's added is API: `litellm.types` (20.6k symbols), `litellm.llms` (36.9k), and 499 top-level names that `litellm/__init__.py` star-imports from `litellm.types`, which are S1's misses. 19 other packages have such folders, all small (`flask.sansio`, chromadb's `api/models` and `segment/impl`, `qdrant_client.embed`, selenium's devtools); a few hide real packages below them (`fastmcp.contrib`, mistralai's `azure` and `gcp`). Both variants pass the 100 MB target, so this waits on DT-DATA-35.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 10): before griffe loads a release, an empty `__init__.py` is written into each folder under an import root that holds `.py` or `.pyi` files and has an identifier for a name (not a keyword or a test folder), unless a module of the same name shadows it. A package's `namespace_skip` in `coverage.toml` leaves the folders below a module unloaded: `litellm.proxy`, whose packages with an `__init__.py` are still indexed, and selenium's generated `webdriver.common.devtools` (~7,700 names per Chrome version, three versions per release and one swapped each release, so +20–24k names a release of churn). Measured against format 9 on the first, middle and latest release of every package (156 releases): 123 don't change and none loses a name. litellm 1.106.6 goes from 55,635 to 161,994 names (6.9 → 16.5 s, 265 → 511 MB peak), and 904 of its aliases now resolve. pinecone 5.4.1 gains 4,789 (`core.openapi`, the gRPC protos), fastmcp 2.12.3 513 (`experimental`, `contrib`), flask 3.1.3 500 (`flask.sansio`, whose `App` members `Flask` now inherits), and chromadb, gradio, weaviate-client, qdrant-client, langchain-community, crewai, mistralai, numpy and streamlit up to 680 each. On a scratch index (the published one with litellm, django and marshmallow rebuilt): litellm's spans 79,275 → 229,087 and replacements 170 → 1,610, the `.gz` 94.8 → 107.3 MB and the `.xz` 59.3 → 66.8 MB, p95 unchanged. The litellm case passes, and S1's litellm misses fall from 32/205 sampled to 3 (1,370 → 178 of 7,734 runtime names); the 3 left are modules litellm binds at runtime under names with no file of their own.
  - Verified 2026-11-02 on the published index (build-index run 41468095717, cold, 101 minutes; litellm's job 66 minutes, the assembly 30): 6,568 releases (unchanged), the `.xz` 59.3 → 67.7 MB and the `.gz` 94.8 → 108.7 MB, about 1 MB above the scratch index for pinecone, flask, chromadb, fastmcp and the other small gains. Both benchmarks, S1 and S2 match the scratch index case by case; p95 is unchanged.
- [x] **DT-DATA-24**: Re-exports in plain modules (`sqlalchemy.schema`, `sqlalchemy.types`) · `P0` `S`
  - Why: the public-import rule (9c1a21f) only covers package `__init__`s. sqlalchemy's `schema.py` and `types.py` are plain modules that only re-export (`from .sql.schema import Column` in 1.4, `... import Column as Column` in 2.x) without `__all__`, so `sqlalchemy.schema.Column`, `MetaData` and `Table` are absent in every release, and the published index says `sqlalchemy.types.Integer` (with 47 more `sqlalchemy.types` names) was removed in 2.0.0: a confident wrong answer. 1 bench miss (`sqlalchemy.schema.ThreadLocalMetaData`, last in 1.4).
  - AC: those names are present in the releases that have them, with no flood of implementation imports from ordinary modules (measure across packages); the bench case passes.
  - Done 2026-10-28: in any module without `__all__`, a top-level `from x import y as y` re-exports, and so does every non-stdlib `from x import y` in a module that is nothing but imports and a docstring (sqlalchemy 1.4's "compatibility namespace" `schema.py`, numpy 2's `lib/scimath.py`). Other plain-module imports stay private: an ordinary module imports to use (langchain-community 0.4.2 has ~9,900 such names). Compared with the previous rule on the first and latest indexed release of all 52 packages: +12,979 symbols (+1.3%), nothing lost, no build-time change; 94% is sqlalchemy, whose re-exported classes now carry their members. The rest is mostly real API that was missing: `fastapi.middleware.cors.CORSMiddleware`, `fastapi.responses`, `fastapi.concurrency.run_in_threadpool`, `matplotlib.pyplot.rcParams`, `numpy.emath.sqrt` (2.x), langgraph's `utils.config.get_config`. On a scratch sqlalchemy index the bench case passes, `sqlalchemy.types.Integer` and `sqlalchemy.schema.Column` span 1.4.0–2.1.6, the 48 false `sqlalchemy.types` removals at 2.0.0 are gone, and no other sqlalchemy case changes. It passes once a `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 4 index is published.
- [x] **DT-DATA-25**: A stub's bare `__all__: list[str]` hides runtime names · `P1` `S`
  - Why: numpy's `core/fromnumeric.pyi` declares `__all__: list[str]` with no value; griffe reads that as an empty `__all__` that overrides the `.py`'s literal one, so the `from .fromnumeric import *` chain brings nothing and the top-level names come from `numpy/__init__.pyi` alone. That stub dropped `product`, `cumproduct`, `sometrue`, `alltrue`, `round_` and `find_common_type` in 1.25, when they were deprecated, so the index dates their removal to 1.25.0 instead of 2.0. 1 bench miss (`numpy.product`).
  - AC: an annotation-only `__all__` in a stub doesn't override the source's; measure what it changes across numpy 1.24–2.x before shipping; the bench case passes.
  - S1 (2026-10-29): numpy is the worst package, at 53/250 sampled (5,374 of 17,532 runtime names), all names listed in a computed `__all__`. Seen in `numpy.matlib` (`fliplr`, `append`, missing at 1.24 but present elsewhere), `numpy.core` (`log`, `longfloat`), `numpy.core.numeric` and `numpy.core.numerictypes`. Check how many of them this fixes.
  - Tried 2026-10-30, not shipped: a griffe extension that drops a stub's annotation-only `__all__` when griffe reads the whole `.py` list. The chain to `numpy.product` breaks in three places, and this fixes only the first. `core/numeric.py` adds fromnumeric's names at runtime through `extend_all(fromnumeric)`, a helper that calls `__all__.append`, and `numpy/__init__.py` uses `__all__.extend(core.__all__)`. griffe follows neither, so `numpy.product` stays missing. The top-level name only ever came from `numpy/__init__.pyi`, which dropped it in 1.25. The fix alone loses more than it gains: 1.24.0 +568/−468 symbols, 1.26.4 +526/−489, 2.0.0 +110/−296, and 2.2+ unchanged (no bare stubs). An empty `__all__` makes griffe call every non-private name public. With the real list back, importable names it leaves out disappear: `numpy.lib.NumpyVersion` (documented), `numpy.core.getlimits.MachArLike`, `array_function_dispatch`. A real fix needs a static evaluator for `__all__` mutations (`.extend`, `.append`, numpy's `extend_all`) that can't drop names `is_public` lets through today; measure losses first.
  - Holdout (2026-10-31): 4 of its 8 misses. `numpy.product` ×3 (last 1.24.4, expected 1.26), and `numpy.math`, which 1.24 lists in `numpy.lib.__all__` and 1.25–1.26 serve from `__deprecated_attrs__` inside a `try` (the `__getattr__` shape DT-DATA-20 doesn't follow).
  - Done 2026-10-31 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 7). The bare stub `__all__` is one of three ways numpy makes names at runtime that the index missed. All three are now read from the `.py` source, still without importing anything.
    - **Runtime `__all__`:** each module's `.py` is read for the names it surely puts in `__all__` (literals, `+`, `+=`, `.extend`, `.append`, `.remove`, another module's `__all__`, and a helper like numpy's `extend_all(module)`), and those names are added to what's public. Nothing public is dropped, which is what sank the 2026-10-30 attempt. `numpy.product`, `cumproduct`, `sometrue`, `alltrue` and `round_` now last until 1.26.4, and numpy 1.x's `numpy.core` goes from about 25 indexed names to about 330. django builds `django.db.models.__all__` from its submodules' lists, so `CharField`, `Count`, `Field` and the rest of its star imports had been missing from every indexed release.
    - **numpy 2's `numpy.core` forwarders:** numpy 2.0 renamed `numpy.core` to `numpy._core` and left modules whose `__getattr__` warns and returns `getattr(<module>, name)`. Such a forwarder now serves its target's public names at its own path, with the warning as the deprecation. Without this, the runtime `__all__` would have added 1,754 false `numpy.core.*` removals at 2.0.0. Forwarded classes don't carry their members (DT-DATA-32). A replacement hint never points at a private module (`numpy._core.numeric`, `numpy.linalg._linalg`).
    - **A table read in a `try`:** numpy 1.24–1.26 serve deprecated aliases from `__deprecated_attrs__`, which `__init__.py` fills at import time and reads in `try: val, msg = __deprecated_attrs__[attr]` / `except KeyError` / `else: ... return val`. That lookup is now followed, with the table read from literals, comprehensions over literals, `.update()` and `T[k] = v`. `numpy.math` is deprecated in 1.25.0 and removed in 2.0.0, and `numpy.bool8`, `int0`, `str0` and four more are indexed for 1.24–1.26. django 6.0's `core.mail` reads a table the same way, so `BadHeaderError`, `SafeMIMEText` and `SafeMIMEMultipart` read deprecated in 6.0, not removed.
    - Of 172 releases (the first, middle and latest of all 52 packages, and quartiles of 8), 146 don't change. The others gain 54,655 symbols (+3.0%): django 8–10k per release, numpy 743–2,520, google-genai 2.34.0's `interactions` (1,899), scikit-learn's vendored `array_api_compat` (119) and one huggingface-hub name. The only records lost are 83 star imports griffe couldn't expand, which had been recorded as names (`numpy.core.multiarray.numpy/core/_multiarray_umath/*`).
    - Left: names a loop adds, such as 1.x's scalar types (`numpy.core.longfloat`, `int8`; DT-DATA-02). The reader gives a lower bound, so a name a module imports without listing it still reads removed (`numpy.core.numeric.ALLOW_THREADS` in 2.x). `numpy.product` has no deprecation text in 1.25–1.26, because numpy warns from its dispatcher.
  - Verified 2026-11-01 on the published index (build-index run 41307821451, 50 minutes): 6,564 releases (unchanged), all built again with no new failures, in 94.6 MB (from 93.7). numpy events 13,344 → 15,165 and replacements 443 → 1,162; django spans 95,651 → 106,629. `numpy.math` reads deprecated in 1.25.0 and removed in 2.0.0, and `numpy.product` lasts until 1.26.4. numpy and django match the local measurement apart from DT-DATA-30's three numpy 2.2.2 paths. Releases outside the 172 change too: pydantic 1.10.16's `pydantic.v1` is a forwarder (`return getattr(pydantic, name)`) and now has its 118 names with their real kinds; langchain 0.0.302–0.0.304's root imports carry their deprecation, which a helper warns (from 0.0.305 the helper first checks for an interactive session, which isn't read); pinecone 7.x loses false gaps (`pinecone.configure_index` now spans 3.0.1–7.3.0), and with them 13 false `moved` hints. The `numpy.product` case in `bench/cases` and the four holdout cases pass, and no other case changes. S1: 4.2% → 2.4%.
- [x] **DT-DATA-26**: pydantic 1.x names re-exported by `from .types import *` are missing when defined under `if TYPE_CHECKING:` … `else:` · `P1` `S`
  - Why (S1): `pydantic.types.StrictBool` is indexed for every 1.10 release, but `pydantic.StrictBool` isn't; the index has it only from 2.0 on. `pydantic.ConstrainedInt`, defined unconditionally, comes through. So for 1.10 users, `StrictBool`, `UUID1`, `DirectoryPath` and about 20 more look added in 2.0. The same 24 names are missing from `pydantic.v1` in every 2.x release. 2.x's top level also misses 13 names per release (`NaiveDatetime`, `FutureDatetime`) that `pydantic.types` has.
  - AC: trace griffe's wildcard expansion for conditionally defined names; the 1.10 and `pydantic.v1` names are present; measure the change across packages.
  - Done 2026-10-30 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 5). The cause is a griffe 2.3 bug: `visit_if` marks the whole `if TYPE_CHECKING:` statement as type-checking only, its `else:` branch included, and `import *` skips objects that aren't available at runtime. A griffe extension (`_RuntimeElse`) marks objects and imports under that `else:` as runtime. Measured against DT-DATA-27's commit on the first and latest release of all 52 packages, plus pydantic 1.10.26, 2.0 and 2.7.0 and 15 middle releases of five lazy-export packages: only pydantic changes, and nothing is lost. 1.10.x gains its 24 top-level names (`StrictBool`, `PositiveInt`, `UUID1`, `DirectoryPath`…; 118 symbols with members), and so does `pydantic.v1` in 2.x and in 1.10.26. 2.7+ gains 9–12 top-level names (`NaiveDatetime`, `FutureDatetime`, `EmailStr`, `ImportString`), and 2.0's change from kind `getattr` to the real class.
  - Verified 2026-10-30 on the published index (build-index run 41067535328, 54/54 jobs, cold, 53 minutes): only langchain and pydantic spans changed (pydantic 8,677 → 9,113). `pydantic.StrictBool` spans 1.10.0–2.14.2. S1's pydantic misses: 20/250 sampled → 0, every runtime name 168/3,650 → 3. The benchmark is unchanged.

The gap was wider than S1 showed. langchain 0.1–0.3's migration shims (`langchain.llms`, `tools`, `vectorstores`, `document_loaders`, `chat_models`…) list their names in a literal `__all__` and serve every name with "Importing … from langchain is deprecated … `from langchain_community.llms import AI21`". The first and latest release of all 52 packages, plus middle releases of langchain-community, llama-index-core, openai-agents and huggingface-hub, don't change.

S1's langchain misses: 3/242 sampled → 1, every runtime name 53/4,964 → 4. Sampled misses that read as removed fell from 37 to 30 overall. The benchmark is unchanged, case by case.
