Both are computed at import time: DT-DATA-05.
- [x] **DT-DATA-29**: numpy `ndarray` methods missing from the index: stub methods defined only through `@overload`, and methods the stub leaves out · `P1` `S`
  - Why (holdout, 2026-10-31): `numpy.ndarray` has 44 indexed members. Methods that `numpy/__init__.pyi` defines inside the class only through `@overload` are absent (`reshape`, `astype`, `sum`, `view`, `item`, `resize`, `itemset`), while module-level overloads (`numpy.sum`, `numpy.prod`) are present. `tostring` is absent because the stub leaves it out on purpose ("deprecated and therefore excluded"); the runtime registers it through `_core/_add_newdocs.py` until 2.2.6. 2 holdout misses: `ndarray.itemset` (removed in 2.0) and `ndarray.tostring` (removed in 2.3).
  - AC: overload-only stub methods are indexed with their overload signatures (find whether griffe or the walker drops them; measure across packages); decide whether to read `add_newdoc(..., 'ndarray', (name, ...))` for methods the stub omits; both holdout cases get the right boundary.
  - Done 2026-10-31 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 6): griffe keeps `@overload` signatures aside until an implementation follows, and a `.pyi` has none; merging numpy's stub `ndarray` into itself then deleted all 97 overload groups. A griffe extension now records each such name as a function whose signature accepts every overload's call: a parameter is required only when every overload requires it, it's annotated as griffe annotates an implementation from its overloads, and the function is deprecated only when every overload is. `numpy.ndarray` goes from 37–44 to 70 members, and `numpy.random.RandomState` (42) and `Generator` (36–38) get their methods. Of 172 releases (the first, middle and latest of all 52 packages, and quartiles of 8), 153 don't change. The others gain 5,163 names (numpy; protobuf `WhichOneof` in qdrant-client and streamlit; pandas `Timestamp.round`), or take a callable's signature from the stub where the `.py` has an alias or an attribute (`numpy.arange`, werkzeug's `CallbackDict.update`) or inherits it (weaviate's sync client). numpy 2.4's stub makes `keepdims`, `initial` and `where` keyword-only, and the index reports that, although the compiled methods still take them positionally.
  - Verified 2026-10-31 on the published index (build-index run 41215772094, 48 minutes): 93.3 → 93.7 MB, numpy events 10,808 → 13,344, and `numpy.ndarray.itemset` is indexed 1.24.0–1.26.4. Both benchmarks are unchanged, case by case; the `itemset` case also needs DT-CORE-21. `tostring` is the one public `ndarray` method missing: the stub omits it, and the gap moves to DT-DATA-02.
- [x] **DT-DATA-30**: Local and CI snapshot builds disagree on a few numpy re-exports · `P2` `S`
  - Why: with the same wheel and the same griffe, a macOS build and the CI (Linux) build resolve some re-exports to different definitions. On `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 5, `numpy.char.add` differed in numpy 2.2.2, 2.4.1 and 2.5.4. On format 6, 171 of 172 radius releases match the published index, and numpy 2.2.2 differs in 6 records: `numpy.lib.npyio.DataSource` and its methods (`_npyio_impl` against `_datasource`) and `numpy.lib.array_utils.normalize_axis_tuple`. Only canonical paths and annotations differ, so no event changes, but a radius check run locally can't be compared exactly with the published index.
  - AC: the cause is found (file order is the first suspect) and the builds agree, or radius checks build their baseline on CI.
  - Done 2026-11-07 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 20): the build replaces griffe's `ModuleFinder._filter_py_modules` with the same walk, sorted (folders and files). numpy 2.2.2 built on macOS and in the format-20 CI build agree on all 10,425 records (symbols, internal names, classes, members), namespaces and module aliases included; format 6 differed in 6.
  - Found 2026-11-06: griffe 2.4's `ModuleFinder._filter_py_modules` lists a package's modules with `os.walk` and doesn't sort them, so they load in the file system's order: APFS's on macOS, ext4's hash order on CI. Next: sort the walk (directories and files) in the build, which changes snapshots, so at the next `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` bump, and check that a macOS and a CI build of numpy 2.2.2 then agree.
- [x] **DT-DATA-31**: Read `@deprecated(...)` arguments instead of storing their source text · `P2` `S`
  - Why: for a deprecation decorator, `deprecation_message` returns the call's argument text, so 4,726 of the published index's 10,836 deprecation events (31 packages) carry source code, not a message: langchain's `'0.0.260', alternative='from_messages classmethod', pending=True` (2,040), matplotlib's `'2.0', name='wx', obj_type='backend', …` (991), numpy's `'Resizing a NumPy generic inplace has been deprecated in NumPy 2.5'` with its quotes. 89 of them say `pending=True`: not deprecated yet.
  - AC: the message is the string literal, unquoted; `alternative=`, `since=` and `removal=` become structured detail or replacement hints; pending deprecations are handled on purpose; the change in the replacement metric is measured.
  - Since DT-CORE-23 (2026-11-03): matplotlib quotes its alternatives in reST, ``alternative='``matplotlib.colormaps.register(name)``'``, which `LANGCHAIN_ALTERNATIVE_RE` doesn't read: `cm.register_cmap` (a benchmark case) and `cm.get_cmap` (S2) get no hint. `matplotlib.colormaps` is an instance, so its members aren't indexed paths: the resolver should say it can't check them rather than contradict them.
  - Done 2026-11-04 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 13): a `deprecated(...)` decorator's keywords are read by name (message, reason, details, addendum, alternative, alternative_import, since, version, removal, name) and its positional arguments by value (a version, then langgraph's alternative, else the message). The text is the message if it says what is deprecated, else "<name> was deprecated in <since> and will be removed in <removal>." before it; then the alternative as "Use … instead.", code in backticks (matplotlib's reST ``literals`` too); then the addendum. langchain's `Chain.run` reads "run was deprecated in 0.1.0 and will be removed in 0.2.0. Use `invoke` instead." The decorator applied by hand to bind a name is read too: matplotlib 3.7's `get_cmap = _api.deprecated(…)(_get_cmap)`, its `_api.deprecated(…)(property(…))` attributes, langchain-core's deprecated fields. A pending deprecation (`pending=True`, or PendingDeprecationWarning as the category) isn't recorded, whether a decorator, a helper or a warning in the body says so; docstring notes already weren't.
  - The miner reads a name called or indexed in backticks, up to the closing backtick ("Use `matplotlib.colormaps.register(name)` instead"). The pattern that read langchain's raw `alternative='…'` is gone, so those hints read "Use `x` instead." at 0.85, not 0.9. A target under a name the package binds as an attribute (an instance, such as `matplotlib.colormaps`) can't be checked, so it isn't contradicted.
  - Radius (156 releases): 50 change, only in deprecation text: 3,116 texts rewritten, 104 pending deprecations dropped (12 of them celery warnings about configuration behaviour), 285 deprecations found (262 matplotlib attributes). A local rebuild of the 30 packages that change, reassembled with the published index's releases: the other 22 packages' rows are identical. Replacement rows 25,235 → 26,094, at ≥ 0.75 8,052 → 8,954: 868 added, 20 lost, all of the lost from pending deprecations (langchain-core's `ChatPromptTemplate.from_strings`, matplotlib's `Figure.set_tight_layout`). 45 of 45 sampled new hints name what the text says; 7 are relative to an instance (matplotlib's `callbacks.process`). bench/cases: replacement 53.7% → 54.9% on real cases (`cm.register_cmap`), root cause and boundary unchanged; the holdout unchanged; S2 27 → 28 of 50 at ≥ 0.75 (`cm.get_cmap` → `matplotlib.colormaps`). Its other two listed alternatives, `colormaps.get_cmap()` and `pyplot.get_cmap()`, are graded wrong against the one documented migration, so 28 of the 31 hints at ≥ 0.75 are graded right.
  - Verified 2026-11-04 on the published index (build-index run 41861243071, cold, 124 minutes, xz 70.4 MB, 6,591 releases): every replacement row of the local build is there unchanged, plus 180 litellm hints from its new 1.108.0; the other row differences come from 10 releases that appeared meanwhile, and numpy's from building on Linux (DT-DATA-30). bench/cases, the holdout and S2 match case by case, and S1 stays at 1.2%.
- [x] **DT-DATA-32**: Members of a class that `__getattr__` serves · `P2` `S`
  - Why: a name served through `__getattr__` is recorded without its members. Since DT-DATA-25, django 6.0's `core.mail.SafeMIMEText` and `SafeMIMEMultipart` read deprecated in 6.0, but their 9 members (`SafeMIMEText.as_string`, `set_payload`…) still read removed in 6.0. numpy 2's `numpy.core` forwarders leave their classes' members out on purpose. From 2.2 on, griffe hits an alias cycle on the stub-merged `numpy.ndarray` (`numpy._core.multiarray.ndarray` → `numpy.ndarray` → `numpy._core.ndarray` → …) and resolves numpy's private stubs differently from release to release, so with members about 3,600 came and went. Without them, about 800 members of `numpy.core.*` classes read removed in 2.0.0.
  - AC: a served name whose `__getattr__` returns `getattr(<module>, name)` carries its target's members; griffe's `CyclicAliasError` on numpy 2.2+ is resolved through the stub's class; the release-to-release noise across numpy 2.x is measured before shipping.
  - Done 2026-11-06 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 17, with DT-DATA-49). From numpy 2.2, `numpy._core.multiarray.ndarray` imports `numpy.ndarray`, which the stub defines, but griffe reports an alias cycle for every import of it under `numpy._core` (39 in 2.2.2): an import griffe calls cyclic is now followed by hand, path by path, to the first object that isn't an import, and a real cycle stays unresolved. The `numpy.core` forwarders then serve `ndarray`, `dtype`, `nditer` and `broadcast` as classes in 2.2–2.4 too, not as unresolved imports. A name a `__getattr__` serves from a branch that returns `getattr(<module>, name)` is recorded as that module's object, with the branch's warning: django 6's `core.mail.SafeMIMEText` and `SafeMIMEMultipart` are classes, with members, and `BadHeaderError` is `ValueError`; instructor 1.17's `utils.*` and transformers 5's `distributed.tensor_parallel.*` get their kinds and signatures.
  - With own members (DT-DATA-49), a forwarded class has the members of the class it is. Measured on all 44 numpy releases built that way: 630 members per release in 2.0–2.1, 1,548 in 2.2–2.3 and 4,063 from 2.4; 200 go while their class stays, all on the same class at its own public path too (`chararray`'s 69 at 2.2.0, from how griffe reads the stubs), so the forwarders add no noise of their own. Without the cycle fix, a simulation on the published index gave 578 (416 at 2.2.0, 143 at 2.5.0) and 50 gaps, now 20. About 800 `numpy.core.*` members used to read removed in 2.0.0; now 90 do.
- [x] **DT-DATA-33**: pydantic 1.10.15 and 1.10.17–1.10.27's `pydantic.v1` top level is missing · `P1` `S`
  - Why: from 1.10.17, `pydantic/v1/__init__.py` is one line, `from pydantic import *`, and griffe doesn't expand that star import of the package's own parent. So `pydantic.v1` has 40 indexed names in those releases (its submodules), against 117–118 in 1.10.16 and 2.x, and `pydantic.v1.BaseModel` reads removed in 1.10.17 and added again in 2.0: a confident wrong answer for the namespace meant for code that runs on both 1.x and 2.x. The format 6 index had it too. Since DT-DATA-25 reads 1.10.16's `pydantic.v1` forwarder, 75 of these false removals also carry a 0.6 `moved` hint to a submodule (`pydantic.v1.networks.AmqpDsn`).
  - AC: find why the star import isn't expanded; `pydantic.v1.BaseModel` spans 1.10.16–2.x; check other packages for a subpackage that star-imports its parent.
  - Done 2026-11-01 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 8): griffe expands a package's star imports but merges what they bring only after it has recursed into the package's submodules, so a submodule that star-imports the package itself gets only the names the package imports by name. The walker now adds the names a module's top-level star imports bind that griffe left out, by griffe's own rule (`is_wildcard_exposed`), and never changes a name griffe already bound. The range was wider than reported: 1.10.15's `pydantic/v1.py` is the same one line, and 1.10.27 is affected too. Of the first, middle and latest release of every package (172), only pydantic 1.10.23 changes; the only other module there that star-imports its own package, `numpy.matlib`, comes out the same.
  - Verified 2026-11-01 on the published index (build-index run 41318628865, 49 minutes): 6,564 releases (unchanged), in 94.6 MB; only pydantic changed. `pydantic.v1` has 94 names in 1.10.15 (from 17) and 117 in 1.10.17–1.10.27 (from 40), the same as 2.0, and `pydantic.v1.BaseModel` is added in 1.10.15 with no later events. pydantic 2.7.0–2.7.3 also shipped `pydantic/v1/v1.py`, whose `from pydantic import *` binds pydantic 2's top level; its 112 names now read added in 2.7.0 and removed in 2.7.4. Replacements 310 → 135: the 131 false `moved` hints into `pydantic.v1`'s submodules are gone, and so are 44 hints from paths 2.0 removed into `pydantic.v1` (`pydantic.ConstrainedBytes`, `pydantic.types.ConstrainedBytes`, `pydantic.tools.parse_obj_as` → `pydantic.v1.…`), whose evidence ("appeared in the same release") the gap had made up (DT-CORE-22). Both benchmarks and S1 are unchanged.

- [x] **DT-DATA-34**: A module whose top level always raises reads present · `P1` `S`
  - Why: mcp 2.x ships `mcp/server/fastmcp.py` (in the index for 2.0.1 and 2.1.1–2.3.0), whose top level is a docstring, a message and `raise ModuleNotFoundError(...)`: a placeholder that tells v1 code `FastMCP` is now `MCPServer`. The index records it as a module, so `No module named 'mcp.server.fastmcp'` on mcp 2.3.0 gets `present_in_installed_version` at low confidence instead of "removed in 2.0.0, last in 1.30.0". Seed case `mcp-fastmcp-module`.
  - AC: a module whose top level raises unconditionally (not under `if` or `try`) reads absent, and the seed case passes, replacement included (DT-CORE-06 already yields `mcp.server.MCPServer` from its message); other such modules among the first, middle and latest release of every package are counted first.
  - Done 2026-11-01 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 9): the walker leaves out a module whose body has a top-level `raise`, and everything below it. A raise under `if` or `try`, the guard of an optional extra, doesn't count. Of the first, middle and latest release of every package (172), only two modules raise like that: mcp 2.3.0's `mcp.server.fastmcp`, and dspy 3.4.1's `dspy.core.types`, which says its 3.3 LM types moved to `dspy.lm15`. About 220 more raises sit under a top-level `if` or `try`. On the published index, `mcp.server.fastmcp` spans 1.2.0–1.30.0 and reads removed in 2.0.0, and `dspy.core.types` ends at 3.3.1 instead of 3.4.1; nothing else changes. The seed case passes, replacement included (`mcp.server.MCPServer`, from the message), and no other case changes.
- [x] **DT-DATA-35**: Publish the index as `.xz` too · `P0` `S`
  - Why: the `.gz` is 94.6 MB of a 100 MB target, and DT-DATA-23 (litellm's folders without `__init__.py`) and DT-DATA-16 (transformers) both need more room. xz, through the standard library's `lzma`, packs the same 939 MB file into 59.1 MB, 37% smaller, in about 2.5 minutes, and Python decompresses it in under 3 seconds. No new dependency.
  - AC: `pack` writes `index.sqlite.xz` next to `index.sqlite.gz`, and the manifest describes both with new keys only (released clients keep only the fields they know, so they still download the `.gz`, which may then pass 100 MB but stays far below their 2 GB cap); the client downloads the `.xz` when the manifest lists it, with the same checks (sha256, size cap, schema, atomic replace, a failed download leaves the current index alone); the self-hosting docs name both files; the 100 MB target applies to the `.xz`.
  - Done 2026-11-01: `pack` writes both files from the same VACUUMed copy (xz preset 6), and the manifest adds `xz_sha256` and `xz_size`; `sha256` and `size` still describe the `.gz`. The client downloads the `.xz` when the manifest lists it and falls back to the `.gz` only on a Python built without `lzma`. `build-index` uploads both files and then the manifest, and warns when the `.xz` passes 100 MB. The first build that publishes it (2026-11-01, warm, 31 minutes, about 3 of them packing the `.xz`) gives a 59.2 MB `.xz` beside the 94.7 MB `.gz`. Checked against the published files: `deptime index pull` from `main` fetches the `.xz` (13 s with decompression), and the result is byte-identical to the `.gz`'s content. The released 0.1.3 wheel, run against files packed by the new code, still fetches the `.gz`. Benches unchanged.
- [x] **DT-DATA-36**: Function warnings with a `RemovedIn…Warning` category aren't read (django, marshmallow) · `P0` `S`
  - Why: `deprecation_message` reads a `warnings.warn` in a function body only when the category's name contains `Deprecat` (or is `FutureWarning`). The module `__getattr__` reader also accepts `RemovedIn…Warning`; the function reader doesn't. django flags deprecations with `RemovedInDjangoNNWarning`, marshmallow with `RemovedInMarshmallow4Warning`. In the published index, 5 of django's 106,629 spans carry a deprecation and 3 of marshmallow's 1,693; django 3.2's `force_text`, `smart_text`, `ugettext_lazy` and `django.conf.urls.url` carry none, though each warns "… is deprecated in favor of …".
  - AC: these warnings are read like the others; django 3.2's `force_text` carries its message and the `force_str` hint; the change is measured on the first, middle and latest release of every package; it ships with DT-DATA-23's `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` bump.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 10, with DT-DATA-23): a function's `warnings.warn` with a `RemovedIn…Warning` category is read as its deprecation. Against format 9 on the first, middle and latest release of every package, deprecated names change in django 3.2 (121), 4.2.21 (181) and 6.1.2 (84), marshmallow 3.20.0 (42) and 3.25.1 (44), and sqlalchemy 1.4.0 (13), and in no other package. Nearly all read "… is deprecated in favor of …". The false ones are deprecation machinery that warns as its job (django's `deprecate_posargs`; sqlalchemy's `deprecated_20`, `warn_deprecated_20`, `expect_deprecated_20`), left to DT-DATA-37. As before, a function that warns only for some arguments reads deprecated as a whole. On a scratch index django's deprecated spans go from 5 to 573 and its replacements from 16 to 220; marshmallow's from 3 to 97 and 4 to 48. `force_text` carries the `force_str` hint, and 3 cases in `bench/cases` and 3 holdout cases get the right replacement (`force_text`, `ugettext_lazy`, `django.conf.urls.url`); no other case changes.
- [x] **DT-DATA-37**: Deprecation helpers recognized by name · `P1` `M`
  - Why: only decorators named `deprecated` count. polars marks renames with `@deprecate_renamed_function("map_elements", ...)`, so `polars.Expr.apply` carries nothing; pandas marks renamed parameters with `@deprecate_kwarg(old_arg_name=..., new_arg_name=...)`; streamlit binds the old name to a wrapper, `experimental_rerun = deprecate_func_name(rerun, ...)`, which the index records as a plain attribute.
  - AC: survey such helpers' names and argument shapes across the covered packages first; decorators and wrapper calls whose name starts with `deprecate` mark the name or parameter deprecated; a new name among their arguments becomes a replacement hint, checked against the index (DT-CORE-12); both benchmarks measured.
  - Also (2026-11-02, since DT-DATA-36): the deprecation machinery itself reads deprecated, because warning is its job: django's `deprecate_posargs`, sqlalchemy's `deprecated_20`, `warn_deprecated_20` and `expect_deprecated_20`. Recognizing helpers by name should also stop marking the helpers.
  - Also (2026-11-02, DT-DATA-42): warnings in nested functions no longer count, so most such machinery no longer reads deprecated. Decorators named like `_deprecate_method(message=...)` (huggingface-hub) and `deprecated_args(...)` (redis) are no longer read by accident with the body: huggingface-hub's `cached_download` lost its `hf_hub_download` hint (bench case `huggingface-hub-cached-download-removed-sentence-transformers`), to come back here.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 11, committed with the rest of the format-11 items): a survey of the 156 radius releases found about 40 helpers; the 26 that deprecate a name or an argument are recognized by name, each with the role of its positional arguments (`_HELPERS` in `snapshot.py`), and keywords are read by name (`message`, `reason`, `alternative`, `new_arg_name`, `deprecated_args`, …). As decorators they deprecate the function or class (polars' `deprecate_renamed_function` and `deprecate_function`, redis' `deprecated_function`, huggingface-hub's `_deprecate_method`, sqlalchemy's `deprecated_20`, `became_legacy_20`, `moved_20`, `deprecated_cls`, weaviate's `docstring_deprecated`, and `typing_extensions.deprecated` imported under another name) or some of its arguments (polars' `deprecate_renamed_parameter` and `deprecated_alias`, pandas' `deprecate_kwarg`, redis' `deprecated_args`, sqlalchemy's `deprecated_params`, huggingface-hub's `_deprecate_arguments`, matplotlib's `rename_parameter` and `delete_parameter`, langgraph's `deprecated_parameter`). A name bound to a wrapper call is deprecated too (streamlit's `deprecate_func_name`, pandas' `deprecate`, matplotlib's `deprecate_privatize_attribute` in a class), and so is a name bound to a function that is (streamlit 1.36's `experimental_rerun = _experimental_rerun`). The text is "<name> is deprecated. Use `<new>` instead. <message>"; streamlit's new names are qualified with the package (`streamlit.rerun`), sqlalchemy's reST roles read as its warnings print them (`Session.get()`), and the miner reads sqlalchemy's "now available as X" (0.8). Warning helpers called in a body (`warn_deprecated`, `issue_deprecation_warning`, `show_deprecation_warning`, and streamlit's message builder `make_deprecated_name_warning`) read like `warn(...)`, except that under a branch whose condition isn't about an argument, or about a subject that isn't a name (an rcParam), they don't deprecate the whole function: matplotlib uses them for behaviours ("Applying clabel() to filled contours is deprecated"); the same rule isn't applied to `warn()` (unmeasured). Functions whose job is to warn (`warn_*`, `maybe_warn*`, `issue_*_warning`, `deprecate_*`, …) no longer read deprecated. Not recognized, because they deprecate a way of calling: `deprecate_nonkeyword_arguments`, `_deprecate_positional_args`, `deprecate_parameter_as_positional`, `deprecate_posargs`, `make_keyword_only`. The query side counts an argument a decorator renamed as accepted until the decorator goes (polars `shift(periods)` reads removed in 1.0.0, not in 0.19.11), and `check_symbols` doesn't reject it.
  - Radius (156 releases, against `bdc51c0`): 27 releases change, only in deprecations (no path, kind or signature changes); deduplicated by canonical path, 300 whole deprecations and 332 argument deprecations are gained, and the 25 lost are all machinery (redis' and pinecone's `warn_deprecated`, polars' `issue_deprecation_warning`, instructor's `warn_deprecated_mode`, langchain's `raise_deprecation` validators, pandas' `maybe_warn_args_and_kwargs`). Graded by hand: the argument readings and the decorator readings hold up; 7 of 42 matplotlib whole readings were behaviours before the branch rule, none after.
  - On a scratch index (DT-DATA-40's, with the 15 affected packages rebuilt): deprecation events 9,999 → 11,541 and argument events 2,623 → 4,849; symbol hints at ≥ 0.75 2,795 → 3,422 and argument hints at ≥ 0.75 359 → 1,717 (polars 975, matplotlib 223), none lost; a random sample of 30 added hints at ≥ 0.75 had one wrong (DT-CORE-12). Real cases: replacement 40.2% → 46.3% (huggingface-hub `cached_download` back, polars `Expr.apply`, `Expr.map`, `list.lengths`, streamlit `experimental_rerun`); holdout 63.6% → 68.2% (sqlalchemy `orm.relation` → `relationship`); root cause and boundary unchanged, no case lost. S2: 17 → 21 of 50 with a right hint at ≥ 0.75 (sqlalchemy `Query.get`, polars `groupby`, streamlit `experimental_get_query_params`, redis `close`), 21/21 right. streamlit `image(use_column_width)` stays a miss: `streamlit.image` is `_main.image`, a method of a module-level instance.
- [x] **DT-DATA-38**: Deprecated parameters marked in the signature or the docstring · `P1` `M`
  - Why: scikit-learn deprecates a parameter by changing its default to the string `'deprecated'` and warning later, in `fit`: `OneHotEncoder(sparse='deprecated')` in 1.2–1.3, with the replacement (`sparse_output`) only in the docstring's `.. deprecated::` note. 93 indexed signatures have that default. Sphinx's `.. deprecated::` directive is common (numpy, scikit-learn, matplotlib, pandas).
  - AC: a parameter whose default is the string `'deprecated'` reads deprecated; a `.. deprecated::` note in a function's docstring, or under a parameter in it, becomes deprecation text; precision is checked on a sample before shipping.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 11, committed with the rest of the format-11 items): a survey of the 158 radius releases found about 950 `.. deprecated::` notes (about 460 at the top of a docstring, 490 under a parameter), 108 `'deprecated'` defaults (all scikit-learn) and 243 docstrings that open with "Deprecated" in 31 packages. What the code says still wins; a docstring fills in what it doesn't, or replaces a warning whose text can't be read (`_documented_deprecations` in `snapshot.py`):
    - a `.. deprecated::` note before any section or field, not inside another directive, deprecates the object, unless its wording deprecates parameters (then those: sqlalchemy's "Setting the Enum.inherit_schema parameter is deprecated") or it is about arguments, a value or a usage ("creating the and_() construct with no arguments is deprecated", "The current behavior of dt.to_pydatetime is deprecated");
    - a note under a parameter entry deprecates that parameter: numpy's `name : type`, google's `name (type):`, and Sphinx fields (`:param convert_unicode=False:`, sqlalchemy); scikit-learn documents constructor parameters in the class docstring, so `__init__` reads those; with `**kwargs`, any documented name counts (sqlalchemy's `create_engine(convert_unicode)`). Not when it deprecates one of the parameter's values ("The `"auto"` option", "The loss 'deviance'", "the `xlwt` engine"), a way of passing it ("Passing booleans as index", "Negative values for `power_t`", "not as a positional argument") or says it will be deprecated; a version alone is the parameter's, unless the entry's own text says what is deprecated (numpy's `binary_repr(width)`, "This behavior is deprecated");
    - a parameter whose default is `'deprecated'` is deprecated, with the note under it as text, else "deprecated (marked by its default value)";
    - a docstring that opens with "Deprecated" ("Deprecated in favor of `embed`.", "[DEPRECATED] …", "**DEPRECATED** - …") or has a google-style `Deprecated:` section deprecates the object, whatever the rest says.
    The text is "Deprecated since version <v>: <note>", reST read as plain text (`:py:func:` roles too). `*args` and `**kwargs` are never deprecated parameters.
  - Radius (158 releases, against `ce8d7eb`): 46 releases change, only in deprecations; deduplicated by canonical path, 131 whole deprecations and 202 argument deprecations are gained, none lost or changed. A first version read sqlalchemy's `:param:` fields and notes inside `.. note::` as the whole function's, and value and usage notes as the parameter's (about 15 wrong); after the rules above, every gain graded right. Held out (releases outside the radius, on the scratch index below): a random sample of 40 distinct gains, 40 right.
  - On a scratch index (DT-DATA-37's, with the 27 packages that have such docstrings rebuilt): 1,617 deprecation events gained with docstring text (470 whole, 1,147 argument: pandas 642, polars 315, gradio 288, scikit-learn 112, streamlit 71) and none lost to it (litellm's 19 lost events are DT-DATA-42's: its snapshots there were still format 10), and 680 hints at ≥ 0.75 from them (352 symbol, 328 argument). Real cases: replacement 46.3% → 48.8% (scikit-learn `OneHotEncoder(sparse)` → `sparse_output`, `AgglomerativeClustering(affinity)` → `metric`); holdout unchanged (68.2%); root cause and boundary unchanged, no case lost. S2: 21 → 24 of 50 with a right hint at ≥ 0.75 (ollama `embeddings` → `embed`, scikit-learn `base_estimator` → `estimator`, pytest `console_main` → `pytest.main`); flask's `JSONEncoder` → `app.json` ("Use `app.json` instead") is graded wrong by the scorer's path rule, 24/25 = 96%, but means `flask.Flask.json` (DT-CORE-12).
  - Not read: `.. deprecated::` under a class's Attributes (scikit-learn's fitted attributes, `base_estimator_`), which aren't class members statically; module docstrings.
- [x] **DT-DATA-39**: Warnings raised one call away · `P2` `M`
  - Why: some deprecations warn from code that `deprecation_message` doesn't read: a helper whose message is a constant in another module (pytest's `skip(msg=...)` goes through `_resolve_msg_to_reason`, which warns with `KEYWORD_MSG_ARG` from `_pytest/deprecated.py`), another class (`Starlette(on_startup=...)` warns in `Router.__init__`), or another method (scikit-learn's `fit`).
  - AC: a function's deprecations include the warnings of same-package helpers it passes the deprecated parameter to (one level, like DT-DATA-25's numpy helper reader), with messages from module constants resolved; false positives are measured first.
  - S2 cases of this shape (2026-11-02, after DT-DATA-42): httpx `Client(cert)` (warned in `create_ssl_context`), anthropic `Anthropic(proxies)` (in the base client), selenium's chrome `Service(log_path)` (in `ChromiumService`), fastapi's `Query(regex)` (in `params.Query`), instructor's `create(validation_context)` (in a nested function of `patch`).
  - Done 2026-11-04 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 14): an argument a function passes on under its own name (by keyword or position) to a function or class of the loaded packages takes that function's deprecation of it, up to three calls down (httpx's `Client.__init__` → `_init_transport` → `HTTPTransport` → `create_ssl_context`). The call is followed by name in the module (a class for its `__init__`), through a module the function imports, through `self` or `cls`, and through `super()` along the MRO. A function that passes its `**kwargs` on whole takes the deprecations of the arguments it doesn't name, pass explicitly or look up in them itself, unless it rebinds them first (marshmallow's fields, aiohttp's `ClientSession.get`). String literals the call passes fill in the message (pytest's `_resolve_msg_to_reason("skip", …)`), and a warning kept in a module constant is read, also a warning instance (`UnformattedWarning(PytestRemovedIn8Warning, "…")`, `.format(func="exit")`). The function's own warnings and docstring notes come first.
  - Only the called function's deprecation of the argument itself is taken: a docstring note on it, a decorator's or a default's marking, or wording that deprecates it as its subject ("`regex` has been deprecated", "The on_startup and on_shutdown parameters are deprecated", "`cert=...` is deprecated"). Its reading of a guarded warning sometimes takes a warning about a value ("Using dict as a `filter` is deprecated", "Omitting `issuer` is deprecated"), which a caller passing the argument along doesn't make. A warning whose condition tests another argument that the call doesn't pass on is left out too (pandas warns about `describe(include)` only for a Series), unless the text names it beside the first. A condition `not isinstance(x, T)` now reads as one about values, like `isinstance(x, T)`.
  - Radius (156 releases: the first, middle and latest of each package): 36 change, only in deprecations. Deduplicated by canonical path, 317 argument deprecations are gained, graded by hand: 315 right (fastapi's `regex` and `example` on every parameter function, httpx's `cert` and per-request `cookies`, aiohttp's `ssl_context`/`verify_ssl`/`fingerprint`, starlette's `on_startup` and `TestClient(allow_redirects)`, selenium's `port`/`service_log_path`, marshmallow's `missing`/`default` on every field, sqlalchemy's `create_engine` options on every dialect, pytest's `skip(msg)`, pandas' `where`/`mask(try_cast)`); 2 wrong (scikit-learn's `DetCurveDisplay.from_estimator` and `PrecisionRecallDisplay.from_estimator`, whose `**kwargs` are meant for matplotlib, reach `from_predictions(y_pred)`). 7 argument deprecations are gone, all wrong before (warnings about a value or a type: "Passing in `embedding` as a Callable is deprecated", pandas' `isin` behaviour, django's `JSONBAgg(default)`). pytest gains 4 whole deprecations (`gethookproxy` and `isinitpath`, warned with a constant), and 3 messages read in full instead of "message not statically recoverable". Build time 80 → 83 s for the 156.
  - On the S2 releases' wheels: anthropic 0.40's `Anthropic(proxies)`, selenium 4.11's chrome `Service(log_path)`, fastapi's `Query(regex)`, httpx 0.28's `Client(cert)` and starlette's `Starlette(on_startup)` read deprecated with their messages.
  - Left out: an argument passed under another name (in the radius, only pandas' `check_index_type` passed as `exact`, whose warning is about `exact`'s default value). Not read: pytest's `exit(msg)` (warned under `if not reason`), instructor's `create` (it calls `self.create_fn`), scikit-learn's `fit` (another method warns about a constructor argument), a condition around the call in the calling function.
  - Verified 2026-11-04 on the published index (build-index run 41912568902, cold, 162 minutes, xz 70.9 MB, 6,595 releases), against a local rebuild of all 52 packages reassembled with the previous index's releases: every replacement row is the same except one numpy hint whose confidence differs from building on Linux (DT-DATA-30), and the other differences come from 4 releases that appeared meanwhile. In 23 packages only the deprecation columns change. Against the previous index, `deprecation_message` hints 11,110 → 11,727 and all hints at ≥ 0.75 8,954 → 12,476 (with DT-CORE-22). bench/cases: replacement 54.9% → 56.1% on real cases (`Starlette(on_startup)` → `lifespan`), root cause and boundary unchanged; the holdout unchanged; S2 28 → 32 of 50 at ≥ 0.75, 32 of 35 graded right; S1 stays at 1.2%. litellm 1.57.0 timed out on PyPI in this run and was carried over from the previous index, as DT-DATA-12 intends.
- [x] **DT-DATA-40**: A function takes the deprecation its dispatcher warns (numpy) · `P1` `S`
  - Why: numpy 1.25–1.26's `product`, `cumproduct` and `alltrue` warn in `fromnumeric.py` ("`product` is deprecated as of NumPy 1.25.0, … Please use `prod` instead."), but the index records no deprecation for them. First filed as a stub hiding the `.py`; that isn't it: griffe merges a stub into the `.py` function it describes and keeps the `.py` body. The functions only forward (`return prod(*args, **kwargs)`); the warning is in the dispatcher their decorator is given, `@array_function_dispatch(_product_dispatcher, verify=False)`, and read as `_product_dispatcher`'s it deprecates another name.
  - AC: a function takes the warning of a dispatcher its decorator is given when that warning deprecates it by name; numpy 1.25's `product` carries its message and the `prod` hint; radius measured.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 11, committed with the rest of the format-11 items): a function whose own body deprecates nothing reads the warnings of each function of the same module or class that a decorator call is given, as its own. It takes one when, read that way, it deprecates the function as a whole and names it, other than as what to use instead, and doesn't name the helper. pandas' `@doc(lookup)` passes a function for its docstring, and "lookup() is deprecated, use select() instead" stays `lookup`'s. Across the 156 releases of the radius and every decorator call given a function that warns, only numpy 1.25–1.26 has this shape (5 functions: `product`, `cumproduct`, `alltrue`, `sometrue`, `round_`); pandas' `@doc(where)` and `@doc(argmax)` are the only others, and stay unchanged. Radius (the 156 plus numpy 1.25.2 and 1.26.4): numpy 1.25.2 and 1.26.4 gain 25 deprecated paths each (the five under their re-export paths), nothing else changes. On a scratch index (DT-DATA-43's, with numpy rebuilt): numpy's deprecation events 880 → 906 and symbol hints at ≥ 0.75 59 → 84 (`product` → `prod`, `cumproduct` → `cumprod`, `alltrue` → `all`, `sometrue` → `any`, `round_` → `round`, bare: DT-CORE-12). The 4 `numpy.product` cases get `prod`: real replacement 39.0% → 40.2% (`numpy-product-chaospy`), holdout 50.0% → 63.6% (3 cases); root cause and boundary unchanged, no other case changes; S2 unchanged (17/50, 17/17 at ≥ 0.75).
- [x] **DT-DATA-41**: Assemble only the packages that changed · `P1` `M` · deps: SPIKE-04
  - Why: the publish job rebuilds every package's rows from its snapshots each night: 21 of the 30 minutes on 2026-11-02, when 4 packages had one new release each (django alone takes 3.4 minutes, openai 3.1, langchain 2.9, sqlalchemy 2.1, litellm 2.0). DT-DATA-23 and DT-DATA-16 add more.
  - AC: a package whose snapshots and assembly code are unchanged since the published index keeps its rows, judged by a fingerprint stored per package in the index (its snapshots' hashes, `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` and a version of the diff and replacement code); any change or doubt rebuilds it; a test shows the result equals a full assembly; a nightly with a few new releases assembles in a few minutes.
  - Done 2026-11-02: each package's rows are stored with a fingerprint (table `assembly`): a hash of its snapshot files, release dates and artifact names, its `coverage.toml` entry, and the assembly code (`store.py`, `schema.sql`, `diff.py`, `replacements.py`, `snapshot.py`, `pipeline.py`, with the Python and `packaging` versions). A cached-only run whose inputs hash the same keeps the rows and only stamps `built_at`. The files are hashed, not loaded (litellm's 795 take 1.4 s to hash and 109 s to load). Rows that took a release from the index itself, or from a `--versions` run, have no fingerprint and are always rebuilt. A test checks that every module the assembly code imports is in the hashed list. The per-package jobs no longer assemble a throwaway index (`--snapshots-only`): django's spent 3.4 of its 3.8 minutes on it. Locally, on litellm, django and marshmallow at format 10: 525 s to assemble, then 2.8 s to keep all three.
  - Verified 2026-11-02: the first run with it (build-index run 41488997053) assembled every package once, in 30 minutes, and stored the fingerprints; the next (41495147430), with no new release, kept all 52 packages' rows: the assembly took 16 seconds and the run 8 minutes, from 38. Both benchmarks pass on its index. A package with a new release is assembled in full; the first nightly with new releases measures that.
- [x] **DT-DATA-42**: Every warning in a function body is read, not only the first · `P1` `M`
  - Why (S2, 2026-11-02): `deprecation_message` keeps the first string literal in a function that reads like a deprecation, and the parameter miner only sees that one. pydantic 2's `validator` carries "The `whole` keyword argument is deprecated…" instead of "Pydantic V1 style `@validator` validators are deprecated… migrate to… `@field_validator`"; `Field(min_items)` carries the `const` message; gradio's `Interface(allow_flagging)` and langgraph's `StateGraph(config_schema)` likewise. A parameter that only arrives through `**kwargs` (pydantic-ai's `Agent(result_type)`: "`result_type` is deprecated, use `output_type` instead") gets no parameter hint either. S2 has a right hint for 11 of 50 migrations, and this is its largest group of misses.
  - AC: each warning's message is read; one that names a parameter of the function, or of its previous release, marks that parameter (with its hint) rather than the whole function; a function reads deprecated as a whole only for a message that names no parameter; measured on the first, middle and latest release of every package, both benchmarks and S2; ships with the next `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` bump.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 11, committed with the rest of the format-11 items): the function body is parsed with `ast` and every `warn(...)` in it is read, not in nested functions. A warning counts when its category reads like a deprecation, now also subclasses not named `…Warning` (pydantic's `PydanticDeprecatedSince20`, langgraph's `LangGraphDeprecatedSinceV10`), or when its text says "deprecat" with any category or none (gradio). Its text is what the code builds: a literal, an f-string, `+`, `%` or `.format()`, the nearest earlier string bound to the name it passes, or a module-level constant. Each warning is then placed by the conditions around it:
    - a warning guarded by an argument being passed (`if x is not None`, `x != "deprecated"`, `if x`, a name looked up in `**kwargs`, a loop over `**kwargs` compared with names) whose text names that argument, or whose wording deprecates a parameter ("'x' is deprecated"), deprecates the argument: new record field `deprecated_params` and spans column of the same name, one `param_deprecated` event per argument, emitted only when its text is new. A name taken out of `**kwargs` stands in for "a parameter of its previous release" in the AC: pydantic 2's `Field(min_items)` and pydantic-ai's `Agent(result_type)` are read that way.
    - an unguarded warning deprecates the function, unless its wording deprecates a quoted name that is neither an argument nor the function's or its class's own name (scikit-learn's `fit` warning about its constructor's `base_estimator`);
    - a warning guarded by the argument's absence (`if x is None`, "without state_schema"), by some of its values (`x == "auto"`, `isinstance(x, bool)`), or by an argument its text doesn't name, deprecates nothing.
  - `check_symbols` reports an argument's deprecation when the caller passes it, and the symbol's own otherwise. Argument alternatives also take a parameter named after a cue ("please use log_output", "in favor of `.condition`"), and the miner no longer takes "using", "one" or "from" for names. The `spans.deprecated_params` column is added to an existing index file when ingestion opens it; readers don't need it, so `schema_version` stays 1.
  - Radius, on the first, middle and latest release of every package (156): whole-function deprecations lost 3,757 and gained 264, 305 reworded, 2,605 argument deprecations, no other change. In samples the lost ones were false (langchain's 600 `raise_deprecation` validators read their docstring; pandas and django warnings about one value; aiohttp's "Chunk size is deprecated" now on `chunk_size`; deprecation machinery; test helpers that filter warnings). Of 40 sampled argument readings, 27 deprecate the argument, 13 one of its values or a combination (pandas `skipna=False`, polars "Using dict as a `filter`"), with text that says so, and none is wrong. No function's source failed to parse on its own in pandas, numpy, scikit-learn and django.
  - On a scratch index (the published format-10 index with the other 51 packages rebuilt at format 11; litellm unchanged): whole-function deprecation events 11,998 → 9,961, argument events 326 → 2,623, argument hints at ≥ 0.75 108 → 359, symbol hints at ≥ 0.75 2,823 → 2,758 (nearly all of the 185 lost named an argument or a value for a whole function, such as `pydantic.Field` → `Literal`). S2: a right hint for 16 of 50 migrations (from 11: langgraph `config_schema`, pydantic `Field(min_items)`, pydantic-ai `result_type`, django `CheckConstraint(check)`, gradio `allow_flagging`), all 16 hints at ≥ 0.75 right. Real cases: replacement 32.9% → 39.0% (marshmallow ×2, redis `errors`, scikit-learn `squared`, urllib3 `method_whitelist` ×2), root cause and boundary unchanged; holdout replacement 45.5% → 50.0%. One case loses its replacement: huggingface-hub 0.24–0.25's `cached_download` took "Use `hf_hub_download` instead." from its `@_deprecate_method(...)` decorator only because the old reader scanned the decorator lines with the body (DT-DATA-37).
- [x] **DT-DATA-43**: A `__getattr__` that looks names up in a dict defined inside it · `P1` `S`
  - Why (S2, 2026-11-02): starlette 0.48's `status.__getattr__` maps old status-code names to new ones in a dict local to the function and returns `deprecation_changes.get(name)`'s target. DT-DATA-20 follows module-level dicts only, so the index says `HTTP_413_REQUEST_ENTITY_TOO_LARGE`, `HTTP_414_REQUEST_URI_TOO_LONG`, `HTTP_416_REQUESTED_RANGE_NOT_SATISFIABLE` and `HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY` were removed in 0.48.0, a confident wrong answer, and misses the replacement the dict spells out.
  - AC: a dict literal bound inside `__getattr__` and read with `[name]` or `.get(name)` serves its keys like a module-level one, deprecated, with each value as a replacement; the four starlette names read deprecated in 0.48.0 and not removed; radius measured.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 11, committed with the rest of the format-11 items). starlette's shape turned out a little different from the note above: its `__getattr__` serves the old names from the module-level `__deprecated__` through `deprecated = __deprecated__.get(name)` and `if deprecated:`, and the dict it defines itself only fills the warning's `{deprecation_changes[name]}`. pandas 3.1's `pandas.api.typing.__getattr__` is the shape the AC describes (`if name in deprecated:` on a dict it defines), and its six old `*Groupby` names read removed in 3.1.0 too. The shim reader now:
    - reads a literal table the function binds itself like a module-level one, both for which names reach a `return` and for the warning's text; the local name shadows a module-level table of the same name;
    - reads `x = TABLE.get(name)` (or `TABLE.get(name, None)`), `x := TABLE.get(name)` and a bare `TABLE.get(name)` as a test of the table's keys: `if x:` holds for the keys whose value isn't a false constant (`0`, `""`, `None`), `x is not None` for those whose value isn't `None`, and `not x` and `x is None` the other way round. A walrus binds `x` for the branch's warning;
    - fills an f-string's `{TABLE[name]}` or `{TABLE.get(name)}` with the table's text, and `{new.__name__}` after `new = TABLE[name]` when the value is a bare name griffe finds: a class's or function's own name, or a module's path (pandas' `"RollingGroupby": RollingGroupBy`). Anything else stays "…";
    - keeps what `try: x = TABLE[name]` `except KeyError: raise` bound for a warning after the `try`; before, only a warning in its `else:` could use it;
    - records a served name that griffe sees only for type checkers (bound under `if TYPE_CHECKING:`), since at runtime it reaches `__getattr__`. A record the walker already made (a name listed in `__all__`) is kept.
  - The miner no longer takes a name cut short by "…", which stands for a part of an f-string the source doesn't say statically: "Use pandas.api.typing.… instead" gave `pandas.api.typing` at 0.6.
  - Radius (the first, middle and latest release of every package, 156): 148 unchanged; 8 gain 20 names, all kind `getattr`, with nothing lost and no other field changed. Each was checked against its source: starlette 1.10's four old status codes and 0.24–0.42's `WS_1004_NO_STATUS_RCVD` and `WS_1005_ABNORMAL_CLOSURE` (deprecated, naming the new name); pandas 3.1's six `pandas.api.typing.*Groupby` (deprecated, "Use pandas.api.typing.RollingGroupBy instead."); and names bound only for type checkers that `__getattr__` serves: pandas 2.2.1's `core.internals.api.Block` (deprecated), pydantic-ai 2.64's `tools.ToolKind` (deprecated, "renamed to `ToolDefinitionKind`"), google-genai 2.34's `types.McpClientSession`, `McpCallToolResult` and `mcp_types`, and litellm 1.106's `images.main.ImageEditRequestUtils`.

- [x] **DT-DATA-44**: Documented names that a module's `__all__` leaves out · `P1` `S`
  - Why (DT-CORE-12, 2026-11-02): pandas' `pandas/api/types/__init__.py` star-imports `pandas.core.dtypes.api` (39 names: `is_float_dtype`, `is_integer_dtype` and the like), then sets `__all__` to six other names. The walker follows `__all__`, so no release in the index has `pandas.api.types.is_float_dtype`. Yet that is where pandas documents these functions, and where its own deprecations point ("Use pandas.api.types.is_float_dtype instead"). Since DT-CORE-12 those hints are scored 0.5, because the index contradicts them. The index does have all 41 `pandas.core.dtypes.api.is_*` names. django's `OpClass` is the same question in a plain module: `django/contrib/postgres/indexes.py` defines it but leaves it out of its `__all__`. Django documents it as `django.contrib.postgres.indexes.OpClass`, and `ExclusionConstraint(opclasses)` says to use it.
  - AC: survey the radius releases for modules whose `__all__` leaves out names they define or import (by a star import or by name), and decide which of those count as public (by PEP 8, `__all__` is the public list; by the libraries' docs, not always); pandas' 39 names and django's `OpClass` read present, or the decision explains why not; radius measured; S1 rerun for pandas if anything changes.
  - Done 2026-11-03 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 12).
  - Survey (the first, middle and latest release of every package, 158): the non-private names a module's `__all__` leaves out are 59,457 imports by name (30,876 from inside the package), 4,970 classes and functions the module defines, 3,180 attributes it defines, 1,959 names a star import brings from inside the package, and 3,908 names bound only under `if TYPE_CHECKING:`. On the format-11 index, 918 of those names read removed, or added late, because a module's `__all__` changed while the name stayed: openai 1.x still binds `openai.api_key`, `organization`, `api_type` and `api_version`, which read removed in 1.0.0; `django.db.close_old_connections` read added in 5.1 and `chromadb.api.types.GetResult` removed in 0.3.26.
  - The rule: `__all__` says what `from m import *` binds, and libraries document names it leaves out. So a module's own definitions count, as they do without `__all__`, unless the module deletes them (pandas' `del v`), only annotates them (`timeout: float`) or binds them only for type checkers. So do the public names a star import brings from inside the package (pandas' `pandas.api.types.is_*`); its source's own imports don't (`pymongo.database.Any`). Imports by name stay implementation, as PEP 8 says. Of the 918, this fixes 545 (510 defined, 35 star-imported). The other 373 are DT-DATA-45 (273 imports by name, 93 that a star import brings from its source's imports, 7 deleted).
  - Now present: all 41 `pandas.api.types.is_*` names (1.5.0 on), `pandas.tseries.holiday.USFederalHolidayCalendar` and `Holiday`, django's `contrib.postgres.indexes.OpClass`, `db.close_old_connections` and `reset_queries` from 3.2, `anthropic.AnthropicBedrock` and `AnthropicVertex`, and `openai.api_key` through the latest release.
  - Radius (the same 158 releases): 97 change, gaining 48,851 symbols, with nothing lost and no other field changed. django gains 14,543, mostly the inherited members of classes its `__all__` leaves out, and numpy 11,815 (`numpy.distutils`, `numpy.f2py`).

Those checked are real: aiohttp 3.11.3 restored `streams.FlowControlDataQueue`, matplotlib 3.10 re-added `sanitize_sequence` as a deprecated function, and pydantic 1.10.17–1.10.27's `pydantic.v1` is `from pydantic import *`, which leaves out the error classes that 1.10.16 (through `__getattr__`) and 2.x serve there. 49 paths lose one, among them the members of `pydantic.fields.FieldInfo` (read absent in 2.7–2.11), `langgraph.graph.message.REMOVE_ALL_MESSAGES` and `chromadb.api.types.EmbeddingFunction`.

  - Both benchmarks are unchanged case by case, and so is S2 (no migration in it names these). S1 is unchanged at 1.2%. Its runtime rule samples a module's `__all__` when it has one, so the names this adds are outside what it measures, and a pandas probe wouldn't sample them; not run. p95 latency is unchanged (explain 31 ms, history 11 ms).

- [x] **DT-DATA-45**: Names a module still imports read removed, or added late, when its `__all__` changes · `P2` `M`
  - Why (DT-DATA-44 survey, 2026-11-03): with an `__all__`, a module's imports by name are implementation, as PEP 8 says; without one, a package's `__init__` re-exports what it imports from the package. So when a module gains an `__all__`, or names its re-exports another way, names it still imports drop out of the index or come in, though `from m import x` works throughout. langgraph's `langgraph.graph.state.StateNodeSpec` and `langgraph.channels.base.EmptyChannelError` read removed in 0.6.0, and sqlalchemy 1.4's `sqlalchemy.types.NullType` and `MatchType` read added in 2.0.0, where `types.py` switched to `as` re-exports. In the radius releases, 273 imports by name and 93 names that a star import brings from its source's own imports have this shape (numpy 178, sqlalchemy 96, langgraph 28).
  - Approach: keep a module's left-out imports in its snapshot, flagged, and at assembly keep a flagged path only where some release of the package has it unflagged, so a name that was public anywhere stays present while it's still bound.
  - AC: those names read present for as long as they're bound; no name that no release counts public is added; radius measured.

It needs the left-out imports recorded as the walk records public names, and their paths' events worked out again at assembly; both fit the next format bump.

Assembly loads each release once more first, and counts a bound path public in the releases that bind it wherever some release of the package has it as public API; a name no release has as public API stays internal. A class member's events also follow a label whose path now names another class (the old class's members compared with the new one's, not all removed). On a 33-release sample (the DT-CORE-39 sample plus numpy 1.26.4 and 2.0.0, sqlalchemy 1.4.54 and 2.0.0, langgraph 0.5.4 and 0.6.0, typer 0.18.0 and 0.19.0, qdrant-client 1.13.3 and 1.14.0), every release's public names are unchanged, and assembled events change by: pydantic −230 +13, sqlalchemy −268 +74, langgraph −150 +34, numpy −44 +6, qdrant-client −18, matplotlib −10, mcp −10 +1. Gone are false `removed` and `added` events (`langgraph.channels.base.EmptyChannelError` in 0.6.0, qdrant's `qdrant_fastembed.IDF_EMBEDDING_MODELS`, sqlalchemy's `types.NullType` added in 2.0.0, matplotlib's `pyplot.rcParams`); the new events are the same changes read across the boundary (1.4's `sql.expression.and_`, an attribute, then a function in 2.0, as at `sqlalchemy.and_`), and member events at a shorter label (`langgraph.func.Pregel` beside `langgraph.pregel.Pregel`). Snapshots are 12% larger, and building them takes no longer.
  - Live 2026-11-09 (build-index 42560611346, rows as its dry run 42543976365's): against the format-22 index, 3,520 fewer events (transformers −1,475, litellm −633, sqlalchemy −215, pydantic −214, langgraph −189), 2,880 more spans and 1,101 fewer replacement rows. typer's `testing.Result` reads present from 0.8.0 (click's class, then typer's own), so 0.19's no longer reads added; langgraph's `channels.base.EmptyChannelError` reads present to 1.2.20 and qdrant's `qdrant_fastembed.IDF_EMBEDDING_MODELS` to 1.18.0, both removed in the release after. pinecone (+177 events) and llama-index-core (+40) gain events from the member rule: `PineconeAsyncio` (9.0) and `ReActAgent` (0.13) name another class, and their old members are now compared with it rather than reported removed (158 in pinecone). The forwarders were measured first (DT-DATA-32): with the cycle fix they add no noise, so a forwarded class has its class's members. Every other check runs on the first, middle and latest release of all 53 packages (158 releases): built with own members and expanded, every snapshot equals the classic walk, path for path, apart from those forwarders. They hold 893,308 records against 4,387,858 (20.4%) and build in 454 s against 654 s. Indexes built both ways from the same three releases of 52 packages (all but transformers, checked in DT-DATA-16; up to 15,000 sampled paths each) give the same spans, spans by release, events per path and internal names for every path.

- [x] **DT-DATA-50**: A class its module rebinds to a call on itself reads as an attribute · `P1` `S`
  - Why: transformers' `modeling_auto.py` defines `class AutoModelForCausalLM(_BaseAutoModelClass)` and then `AutoModelForCausalLM = auto_class_update(AutoModelForCausalLM, head_doc=...)`. griffe records the second binding as an attribute, so the 41 Auto classes had no members, and `AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained`, transformers' most used call, read `never_existed` at medium confidence (found while adding transformers back, DT-DATA-16).
  - Done 2026-11-05: a griffe extension keeps the class when its module (or class) rebinds the name to a call whose first argument is the class, unless the call is a deprecation wrapper, whose message is read from the attribute. Of the first, middle and latest release of all 53 packages, only transformers rebinds a class (41 to 65 per release), so no cached snapshot changes and the format stays 16. The same shape on functions (django's 8 `args_check = staticmethod(args_check)` in class bodies, sqlalchemy's private `_raw_append`) would change cached snapshots, so it waits for the next `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` bump.
  - Functions done 2026-11-06 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 17): a function rebound to a call on itself stays the function, with `classmethod` and `staticmethod` as its kind of method, unless the call is a deprecation wrapper; `property(f)` stays an attribute, as griffe has `@property`. In the radius releases: django's `QuerySet.as_manager = classmethod(as_manager)` (1,199 member paths, every queryset class), `args_check`, and `django.contrib.gis`'s ctypes callbacks (`notice_h = NOTICEFUNC(notice_h)`).
- [x] **DT-DATA-46**: Private members of classes, and the members of private classes · `P2` `M`
  - Why: DT-DATA-22 indexes module-level names only. haystack called weaviate's `client.collections._get_all()`, a method `_Collections` inherited from `_CollectionsBase` until 4.7.0 made the sync class a wrapper. Such a miss is now `never_existed` at `low`, saying DepTime can't tell.
  - Measured 2026-11-05 on the latest release of all 52 packages: own private members of classes in public modules 38k (4.1% of the public paths), members of private classes 25k, before inherited members.
  - AC: the `_get_all` case passes; size measured; a class's private members never read as its public API.
  - Done 2026-11-06 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 17, with DT-DATA-49): each class record keeps its own members that aren't public API, kind and canonical path only, as other internal names: its private names, and for a class neither public nor a public class's base, every member. Such classes are recorded after every public walk, with their MRO, from the classes `internal` finds (weaviate's `_Collections`). The store keeps them in `internal_members`; `internal_spans` resolves `<class path>.<name>` through the class and its MRO, for a public or an internal class path, and returns them flagged `internal`, with no events and never in a suffix search for public paths. An attribute error on a private class (`'_Collections' object has no attribute '_get_all'`) searches internal class names when no public path has it. A member the index doesn't find on a class it has records of is `never_existed` at `medium`, as public ones are; the "can't tell" note stays for indexes from before.
  - Radius (158 releases): 147,247 internal members beside 572,496 own public ones, and 9,576 classes no public path reaches. On the three radius releases of 52 packages: 80,334 `internal_members` rows beside 237,713 `members` rows.

### Core logic (DT-CORE)
- [x] **DT-CORE-01**: Benchmark-driven tuning of candidate selection and ranking · `P0` `M` · deps: QA-01
  - AC: root-cause accuracy ≥ 80% (M1 floor: 70%).
  - Done 2026-11-02: root cause is 95.6% on the 159 real cases and was 82.8% on the holdout's single pre-registered scoring (DT-QA-04), both above 80%; `bench.yml` fails on a drop of more than 2 points. DT-QA-01 keeps growing the cases.
- [x] **DT-CORE-02**: Instance-chain hints for `object_attribute` (prefer frame and referenced-name evidence; show ambiguity) · `P2` `M`
  - Bench: user subclasses of library classes (`LogParam.get_metavar(ctx)` in click 8.2, `ValidatorSchema.context` in marshmallow 4) and an owner-less `sort()` that should be `DataFrame.sort`, not `Expr.sort` (3 cases). Holdout: `'MACAddressField' object has no attribute 'fail'`, a user `marshmallow.fields.Field` subclass in marshmallow 4 (no finding).
  - Done 2026-11-06 (no index change) for the subclasses; the owner-less `sort()` moved to DT-CORE-31. A class the package doesn't have, with evidence tying it to one it has: (1) an attribute missing from a class whose name ends with a package class's name (`ValidatorSchema`, `Schema`; `MACAddressField`, `Field`) reads as that class's removed member, by its defining class, at medium (low and several findings when 2 or 3 classes fit, none past 3); (2) an unexpected keyword passed by the package's own code (the frame that raised is the package's) to a method of such a class reads as the base method's new required parameter, the base being the class the other candidates inherit from (`ParamType.get_metavar(ctx)`, not `Choice.get_metavar`), with an explanation that the user's override has to accept it. pytest's and IPython's location lines are now frames too, so (2) works on their output (IPython's function names drop their argument lists). The click and marshmallow cases pass: 202 real cases, root cause + boundary 99.5% (201/202, the polars case left); the holdout's `Field.fail` case passes with its `make_error` replacement: 96.6% (28/29), replacement 81.8% (18/22); S3 968/968 and 736/736, from 93.8% on the holdout, whose 46 misses were this click break. All are scores on cases these fixes were designed from.
- [x] **DT-CORE-03**: Import-name collision disambiguation (`agents`) via installed versions · `P1` `S`
  - AC: when `installed_versions` or `pip_freeze` shows that a shared import name belongs to a distribution DepTime doesn't cover, `explain_error` doesn't explain the error with the covered package's history; a covered package installed under another distribution name gets its installed version read.
  - Done 2026-11-05: two `coverage.toml` keys, read only at query time, so the index doesn't change. `shares_import_name_with` lists the distributions that install other code under the same import name: `agents` (TensorFlow Agents, 1.4.0) and `ai-agents` (aiwaves-cn, 0.0.52) both install a top-level `agents`, as their artifacts show; a benchmark case (`openai-error-module-removed-ai-agents`) runs under ai-agents. openai-agents stays a candidate when it's installed (with a note when a rival is installed too: whichever was installed last overwrote the other's files). When a rival is installed instead, or a whole `pip freeze` leaves openai-agents out, it is dropped, also as the called package, and the rival (or `agents`) goes to `not_covered` with a note; with nothing to go on, the answer says it assumed openai-agents. `installed_as` lists distributions that install the same code with the same version numbers, with the range that does, checked on PyPI: `pinecone-client<6` (pinecone 2.2.2–5.0.1 pin it; 6.0.0 only raises "renamed"), `typer-slim>=0.12,<0.22` (from 0.22.0 it requires typer), `fastmcp-slim`, `pydantic-ai`. When the covered name isn't listed, its version is read from these, with a note: on the published index, `module 'pinecone' has no attribute 'Pinecone'` with `pinecone-client==2.2.4` now reads `symbol_not_yet_added` at `high`, where it read present in the latest release at `low`. `check_symbols`, `symbol_history` and `diff_versions` called with such a name say which package to ask for. Both benchmarks are unchanged case by case.

- [x] **DT-CORE-05**: Library-specific migration exceptions (openai `APIRemovedInV1`, pydantic `PydanticImportError`) · `P1` `S` · deps: SPIKE-03
  - AC: bench cases `openai-v1-chatcompletion` and `pydantic-basesettings-moved` pass; also numpy 2's "`np.float_` was removed in the NumPy 2.0 release" messages, pydantic's `PydanticUserError` for removed kwargs (`regex`), and pytest's `PluginValidationError` for hook arguments (bench cases with `fixed_by` DT-CORE-05).
  - Done 2026-10-28: the parser matches these by exception class, with message templates copied from the numpy 2.0.2, pydantic 2.9.2, openai 1.55.0 and pluggy 1.6.0 wheels, and reads the lines after the exception line (openai and pluggy put the details there). It also strips pytest's `INTERNALERROR> ` prefix. pydantic's `removed-kwargs` takes its owner from the raising frame (`conlist` raises it too), else `Field`. Query: a `**kwargs` catch-all no longer counts as accepting a parameter that earlier releases declared by name (pydantic 2's `Field(**extra)`). pytest now also indexes `_pytest.hookspec`, the path pytest documents its hooks under. 9 of the 11 cases pass; the two hook cases pass on a scratch index built from the 8.4.2–9.1.1 wheels (last with 9.0.3) and need the format-4 index. Real cases: root cause 85.5% → 89.9%, root cause + boundary 72.3% → 76.7%. The messages also name the replacement ("Use `np.float64` instead"); DT-CORE-06 can mine those.
- [x] **DT-CORE-06**: Mine replacement hints from the live error text (e.g. mcp 2.x's FastMCP → MCPServer hint), checked against the index · `P1` `S`
  - Done 2026-11-01: `explain_error` reads the exception message with the patterns used for deprecation text ("Use `np.nan` instead", "use `pattern` instead") and for "moved to the `X` package". The traceback is untrusted input, so a target counts only if the index has it, at the installed release or the first release without the old name: a public path of the package (numpy's `np.` read as `numpy.`, then as written, next to the missing name, or at the top level); for a removed parameter, a parameter of the same function; for a package move, the name in that covered package. The head of a longer expression is skipped (`np.dtype` in "Use `np.dtype(obj).type` instead", `arr` in "Use `arr[index] = value` instead"). These hints have the new source `error_message`, with the message as evidence, control and format characters removed, capped at 300 characters. Python's own "Did you mean" isn't used: in the 19 bench messages that have one, it was the replacement 4 times. Real cases: replacement 19.5% → 28.0% (numpy 2.0's removed aliases ×3, pydantic's `regex` → `pattern` ×2, `BaseSettings` → `pydantic_settings.BaseSettings` ×2); holdout 0% → 31.8% (7/22); no root cause or boundary changes in either. Of numpy 2.0's 52 expired-attribute messages, 22 now give a hint, all of them right; the rest name an expression, a builtin, the standard library, or nothing. mcp 2.x's "FastMCP was renamed to MCPServer" yields `mcp.server.MCPServer`, but only once the module raising it reads removed (DT-DATA-34).
- [x] **DT-CORE-07**: `check_symbols` from a code snippet (extract imports and attributes with `ast`) · `P2` `M`
  - Done 2026-11-06 (no index change): `check_symbols(code=...)` (MCP, HTTP; `symbols` is now optional). `snippet.py` reads with `ast`, never running it, the package's modules and names the code imports, attribute chains off them, each call's keyword arguments, and attributes of an object built by calling one (`with httpx.Client() as c: c.get(...)`), kept only where the index shows the call builds a class at that version, so a function's return value isn't guessed at. Up to 300 names; the note says how many it read.
- [x] **DT-CORE-08**: Sanitize mined text (control characters, instruction-like content), with adversarial tests · `P0` `S`
  - Done so far (2026-11-01): evidence quoted from the traceback (DT-CORE-06) has control and format characters removed and is capped at 300 characters. Mined deprecation text isn't stripped yet.
  - Done 2026-11-02 (`e91427f`): every field that carries text from library source (deprecation messages, replacement evidence, event details, signature defaults and annotations) passes through `untrusted.py` as a validator on the public models, so no code path skips it. Characters that render as nothing or reorder text are removed (bidi overrides, zero-width and Unicode tag characters, variation selectors, private-use code points), control characters become spaces, length is capped at 400 (5,000 for defaults and annotations, above the longest real one), and Markdown images become plain links. Text that reads like instructions to an assistant is replaced by a placeholder, checked after removing invisible characters and NFKC normalization, and replacement hints mined from it are dropped. 26 adversarial tests; none of the 60,097 distinct texts mined into the index matches (one candidate pattern, "system prompt", matched a typer option's help text and was dropped); both benchmarks are unchanged case by case and the models' JSON schemas are identical.
- [x] **DT-CORE-31**: An owner-less method error names no class: which one it is · `P2` `S`
  - Why: polars' `TypeError: sort() got an unexpected keyword argument 'descending'` (0.16.7) names only `sort`, which `DataFrame`, `LazyFrame`, `Series` and `Expr` all have, and the traceback's frames are the user's `<stdin>` and polars' deprecation wrapper (DT-CORE-02's last benchmark case).
  - AC: rank the candidates by what the traceback shows (the caller's code line, the wrapper's frame) and say when it can't tell; the case passes or reads as ambiguous.
  - Done 2026-11-07 (no index change): a candidate whose class the frames' code names by its own name ranks first (`pl.DataFrame(data).sort(...)` names polars' `DataFrame` through an alias the traceback doesn't resolve). The benchmark's polars case has no such line (`<stdin>` and polars' wrapper), so it lists the four classes and says it can't tell, which the AC allows; the case now says so instead of pointing at a fix.
  - Done so far (2026-11-06): when the classes that fit changed the same way, the note says the error names no class and lists them ("4 classes' `sort` changed the same way (`Expr`, `Series`, `DataFrame` and `LazyFrame`)"). Ranking by evidence is left; the case still reads `Expr.sort` first.
- [x] **DT-CORE-32**: `check_symbols` says `missing` only where the index can know · `P1` `M`
  - Why: all 138 of its false positives on the 2026-11 holdout (3.8% of 3,600 working uses) are `missing`: pytest 42, sqlmodel 30, fastapi 13, langchain-community 11, weaviate-client 10, langgraph 9, django 6, matplotlib 6.
  - Shapes: (1) a path through an object that is neither a module nor a class, whose attributes are made at runtime (`pytest.mark.parametrize`, `django.conf.settings.configure`, sqlmodel's `func.avg`, `SQLModel.metadata.create_all`, `matplotlib.pyplot.cm.viridis`); (2) a member a class inherits from another distribution (`fastapi.FastAPI.mount` and `add_middleware` from starlette, `sqlmodel.Session.add` from sqlalchemy, langchain-community's `FAISS.from_documents` and `QuerySQLDataBaseTool.invoke` from langchain-core), including an override that's gone while the inherited one stays (sqlmodel's `Session.get`, which `diff_versions` also reads as removed); (3) a module another distribution installs under the same root (`langgraph.checkpoint.memory`, `langgraph.store.memory`, from langgraph-checkpoint); (4) a class attribute bound to another class (weaviate's `Configure.Generative.cohere`, `Configure.NamedVectors.text2vec_openai`).
  - AC: (1) and (3) read `unknown` with the reason, not `missing`; (2) resolves through the base's distribution where it is covered (starlette, sqlalchemy, langchain-core) and reads `unknown` where it isn't; (4) follows the alias; scored on DT-QA-08's set.
  - Done so far (2026-11-07, no index change): `SymbolCheck.status` has a new value, `unknown`, with the reason in `hint`. A path with no span at the release whose nearest indexed prefix is an attribute, function, type alias or `__getattr__` name (shape 1, and shape 4 for now: `Configure.Generative` is an attribute) reads `unknown`; an alias that leads into another covered distribution (`fastapi.status` is `starlette.status`, `fastapi.Request` is starlette's) is checked there, at the newest indexed release the package's own requirement allows (`requirement()` from DT-DATA-14's Requires-Dist), and reads `unknown` when that distribution isn't covered or required; a module imported under another path (`matplotlib.pyplot.style` is `matplotlib.style`) is checked at the module's own path. A module or class prefix still means `missing`. Re-run on 2026-11 (a check; `score.py` counts `unknown` as an abstention, like `not_indexed`): false-positive rate 3.8% → 1.4% (48/3,519), precision 47.9% → 71.6%, recall 97.6% of the 124 breaks it answers; 81 working uses and 6 breaks abstain. The 6 are weaviate's `Configure.Vectorizer.text2vec_weaviate` ×2 and google-generativeai's `HarmCategory.*` ×4 (an enum from google-ai-generativelanguage), which read `missing` before only because the index had never seen them; the holdout baseline records the change.
  - Done 2026-11-07 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 19-20): snapshots record each class's direct bases from outside its package (`ClassRecord.external`, `classes.external`: 39,696 classes in all 53 packages), each release's namespace packages (`releases.namespaces`), and a class attribute bound to a class by name, or a nested class, as that class with its own members (14,688 more class records), which the store resolves through a member class; a module attribute bound to a class (`masked_array = MaskedArray`, weaviate 4's `auth.AuthApiKey = _APIKey`) is the class too. `check_symbols` looks a name a class lacks up in those bases: in the covered package at the newest release the requirement allows, in the standard library's class (`enum.Enum`), or reads `unknown` for a base it can't read; under a namespace package it reads `unknown` only when the release requires a distribution named after the root that may install the module (langgraph-checkpoint; mistralai 2.0's root is a namespace package too, and its `Mistral` really went). `diff_versions` drops a used member's `removed` event when the class still inherits the name. Re-run on 2026-11 (a check) on the format-20 dry run: false-positive rate 0.3% (11/3,521), precision 91.9%, recall 98.4% of the 126 breaks it answers; 79 working uses and 4 breaks abstain (google-generativeai's `HarmCategory.*`, an enum from another distribution). The 11: matplotlib's runtime colormaps (`cm.coolwarm`, `globals().update`) ×3 and `pyplot.cm.ScalarMappable` (absent from 3.10's `matplotlib.cm` in the index), `polars.__version__` ×2, transformers 5's `tokenization_utils` ×2 (DT-DATA-52), and langchain-community's `PyPDFLoader.load` ×2 and `AsyncHtmlLoader.load`, whose base comes through the package's own re-export of langchain-core's class (DT-DATA-55). Since class-valued members are now classes, a class member that switches between class and attribute is no longer a `kind_changed` event (2,593 on the published index: transformers 4.54's `config_class`, set by `PreTrainedModel.__init_subclass__` from the `config: XConfig` annotation, 2,525; sqlalchemy's `dialect.type_compiler`; langchain's `args_schema`); a module-level one still is (474: openai 1.99.2's `ChatCompletionMessageToolCall` union, pydantic 2.0's `DirectoryPath`).
- [x] **DT-CORE-33**: `diff_versions(used_symbols=...)` reports a removed parent · `P1` `S`
  - Why: 25 of its 30 missed breaks on the 2026-11 holdout are members whose class or module was removed (mcp 2's `mcp.server.fastmcp.FastMCP.tool`, `.run` and the rest ×16, `LLMChain.from_string`, langchain-community's `Qdrant.from_documents`, weaviate's `Configure.Vectorizer.text2vec_weaviate`): `used_symbols` keeps events on the path and below, never above, so the answer has no event for the use.
  - AC: a used path's removed (or, for an older `to`, not yet added) ancestors are reported with it, marked as the reason; the 25 are flagged on a re-run, and the false-positive rate stays at 0.3% or below.
  - Done 2026-11-07 (no index change): two causes. 20 of the 25 are class members whose own history has the change, but the filter read the events of the class that defines them, at its label (DT-DATA-16): mcp's 16 were there under another path (`mcp.server.FastMCP.tool`), and four members of subclasses added after `to` had none, since the base didn't change. A used class member now gets its own events at its own path, as `symbol_history` works them out, in place of its defining classes' (which also drops base-class changes from before the subclass existed, or that an override doesn't take). For the rest, a parent module or class removed, moved or retyped in the range, or added there (read back from `to`), is reported once per change, the outermost one (the module, not also each class in it), and the new `HistoryEvent.affects` lists the used paths it takes with it; none where the index has the used path itself across the change (a re-export). Re-run on the 2026-11 set (a check, not a score; `score.py` now counts `affects`): 23 of the 25 flagged, recall 76.9% → 94.6%, false-positive rate 0.3% (the same 7). The other two are weaviate's `Configure.Vectorizer.text2vec_weaviate`, a class attribute bound to another class (DT-CORE-32's fourth shape). Benchmarks, golden tests and `check_symbols` unchanged.
  - Also: the path filter tested every event of the range against every used path (all 335k events of transformers 4.30..5.22 per query): 20 used paths took 5.0 s, 100 took 20 s. It now reads each path's events through `events_by_path`: 19 ms and 45 ms, the same rows on 160 random path sets across eight packages.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-34**: Constructor keywords of dataclasses and pydantic models, and required arguments in `check_symbols` · `P2` `M`
  - Why: on the 2026-11 holdout, `ModelSettings(extra_args=)` (a dataclass), qdrant's `VectorParams(multivector_config=)` (a pydantic model with `extra="forbid"`) and fastmcp's `StdioTransport(log_file=)` before those keywords existed aren't flagged by `diff_versions`, and `check_symbols` takes the pydantic one; `streamlit.pyplot()` (1.67 requires `fig`) and `instructor.patch(create=, mode=)` (0.3 requires `client`) aren't flagged by either, since `check_symbols` asks for keywords only.
