  - Done so far (2026-11-07, no index change): `SymbolQuery.positional` and `SymbolCheck.missing_args` (required parameters passed neither by position nor by keyword; a method's `self` or `cls` comes bound, `*args` takes the rest); `code=` counts positional arguments from the snippet's calls (the most any call passes, none once one spreads). The holdout scorer passes the labels' `npos` (a rule for sets from DT-QA-08 on); on 2026-11 it adds no false alarm. Dataclasses already have their `__init__` (griffe's extension); a used class's `__init__` events now come at the used path (DT-CORE-33), so `ModelSettings(extra_args=)` and `StdioTransport(log_file=)` are flagged by `diff_versions`. Left: pydantic models' fields as keywords (DT-DATA-09), and `streamlit.pyplot`, bound to a `DeltaGenerator` instance's method.
- [x] **DT-CORE-35**: `explain_error` on a module that became a name, and on `AttributeError` subclasses · `P1` `S`
  - Why: three of the four 2026-11 holdout traceback misses. `No module named 'fastmcp.tools.tool'` (fastmcp 4.0.3, 4.0.9) reads `present_in_installed_version`, because the index has `fastmcp.tools.tool` as the `tool` decorator function; polars 2's `AttributeRemovedError` ("read_csv_batched was removed in version 2.0; use scan_csv instead", an `AttributeError` subclass) isn't parsed at all.
  - AC: a `ModuleNotFoundError` for a path the index has only as a non-module name reads `module_removed` at the last release it was a module (or importable as one, DT-DATA-52); an exception named `*Error` whose message says a name was removed is read as an `AttributeError` when the traceback's last frame is in a covered package; both cases pass.
  - Done 2026-11-07 (no index change): a `ModuleNotFoundError` for a path the installed release has as another kind of name, and that was a module before, reads `module_removed` at the last release with the module, at `medium` confidence: the explanation names what the path is now ("the function `fastmcp.tools.function_tool.tool`") and says the package may have kept the old module importable through an alias the index doesn't see. A library's own exception class (not a builtin) whose message starts "`` `name` was removed``" or "has been removed" reads as the `AttributeError` it stands for: `module_attribute` in the package that the exception's own module names (`polars.exceptions.AttributeRemovedError`; the traceback often ends in the caller's frame), else the one whose frame raised it, and `class_attribute` for a dotted name; neither, and it stays `unknown`. polars' case passes with `scan_csv`; fastmcp's two get the right root cause at 3.1.1, the last release with the file, while the case says 3.4 (a `sys.modules` alias kept it importable): they're now DT-DATA-52's. Real cases: root cause 98.3% → 99.6%, root cause + boundary 97.9% → 98.3%; TMB, S3 (both sets) and the golden tests unchanged.
- [x] **DT-CORE-36**: A bare `TypeError` gets a finding for a package that isn't installed · `P1` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11b holdout: `TypeError: create() got an unexpected keyword argument 'file_ids'`, with no frames and only openai 1.28.1 installed, reads litellm's `create(file_ids)` (`confidence=low`), not openai's `Assistants.create`, whose `file_ids` 1.21 removed.
  - AC: when the installed versions are known, a callee found by its bare name is ranked among the installed packages only; the openai case passes.
  - Done 2026-11-08 (no index change): two causes. openai was searched first, but its lookup of `create` (542 paths across releases) kept the five shortest, all 0.x module-level ones that never took `file_ids`, so every finding was `never_existed` and the callee search moved on to other packages. A bare function name with an argument now ranks first the paths whose signature had that parameter in some release (`find_by_suffix(param=)`, a match on the stored JSON) and that exist in the installed release, the only ones the call can have reached: `Assistants.create`, `AsyncAssistants.create` and `threads.Messages.create`, all `param_removed` after 1.20.0, and not `Containers.create`, which took `file_ids` from 1.81. Then, when installed versions are given, the callee search for a bare name looks only at packages installed or required by the raising code (26 packages have a `create`). The case passes; the two fastmcp `tools.tool` cases, passing since the format-20 index (DT-DATA-54), are marked `expected_pass` too. Real cases: root cause 98.5% → 98.9%, root cause + boundary 98.2% → 98.5%; the 2026-11b holdout's tracebacks 91.9% → 94.6% (35/37; a check, not a score); TMB, S3 (both sets), both holdouts' symbol checks and the golden tests unchanged; p95 9.1 ms (8.3 ms before, within run-to-run noise). The parameter lookup costs at most what the plain one does (26 ms for transformers' `__init__`).
- [x] **DT-CORE-37**: `ImportError: cannot import name X` with no quotes and no module · `P2` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11b holdout: scikit-learn 1.3's `cannot import name SCORERS`, raised in Cython-compiled code (`File "piml/data/base.py", line 7, in init piml.data.base`), reads as a shape DepTime doesn't analyse.
  - AC: the shape parses (a failing case in `tests/test_tracebacks.py` first), the name is looked up among the installed packages, and the case passes (`sklearn.metrics.SCORERS`, last 1.2, replacement `get_scorer_names`).
  - Done 2026-11-08 (no index change; committed on the format-21 branch): `cannot import name X`, quoted or not, with no module and no import line naming one, parses as `import_name` with no module, and so does a quoted message trimmed of its module. The name is looked up among the candidate packages (the installed ones, when no frame is a covered package's) as a module's name in any indexed release, shortest path first; a class's attribute doesn't count, since `from M import X` can't take it. Confidence is `medium` at most when more than one module has the name, and a note says the module was inferred. The case passes on root cause and boundary (`sklearn.metrics.SCORERS`, last 1.2.2); the replacement doesn't (the index has no hint for `SCORERS`). Real cases: root cause + boundary 98.5% → 98.9%; the 2026-11b holdout's tracebacks 94.6% → 97.3% (36/37; a check); TMB, S3 (both 100%) and the holdouts' symbol checks unchanged.
- [x] **DT-CORE-38**: Module dunders and generated attributes read `missing` · `P2` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11b holdout: `mcp.__name__` and `polars.__version__` (never seen) and openai-agents' `ModelResponse.__pydantic_serializer__` are 6 of `check_symbols`' 38 false positives.
  - AC: a module's standard dunders (`__name__`, `__file__`, `__path__`, `__doc__`, `__spec__`) read present, `__version__` reads present where the module binds it and `unknown` otherwise, and a dunder on a class that the index doesn't record reads `unknown`.
  - Done 2026-11-08 (no index change; committed on the format-21 branch): a dunder name with no span under a module or class reads `present` when every module or class has it (fixed lists, `MODULE_DUNDERS` and `CLASS_DUNDERS`, not `dir()`, so answers don't depend on the Python running DepTime), and `unknown` with the reason otherwise, or for a path through one (`__spec__.origin`). A dunder the index has at that path in another release stays `missing`: werkzeug 3.1 really dropped `__version__`. Re-run on both holdouts (a check, on the format-20 index): 2026-11b `check_symbols` false positives 38 → 32 (rate 1.5% → 1.3%, precision 78.7% → 81.4%), 2026-11 11 → 9 (`polars.__version__` ×2), recall unchanged (100%, 98.4%); benchmarks and golden tests unchanged.
- [x] **DT-CORE-39**: `missing_args` counts a pydantic v1-style `__init__`'s first parameter · `P1` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11b holdout: crewai 0.41's `Agent(...)` and `Task(...)` read `missing_args=['__pydantic_self__']` (5 false positives): DT-CORE-34 treats only a first parameter named `self` or `cls` as bound.
  - AC: the first parameter of a method or `__init__` is bound whatever its name; those uses read present.
  - Design (2026-11-08): the snapshot drops a method's first parameter only when it's named `self` or `cls` (`_params`), so the index can't tell `__pydantic_self__` from a real first argument, and a query-only fix would have to guess by name. On the live index about 30 stored methods keep a self-like first parameter: `__pydantic_self__` in pydantic, pydantic-settings, crewai and sqlmodel, click's `__self`, gradio's `self_or_cls`, weaviate's `self_`. Fix it at extraction in the format-21 batch: drop the first parameter of any function defined in a class unless it's a `staticmethod` or the parameter is `*args` or keyword-only. That also drops pydantic's spurious `__pydantic_self__` → `self, /` signature change.
  - Done 2026-11-08 (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 21-22, live in build-index 42515969306): `_params` drops the first parameter of a function defined in a class unless it's a `staticmethod` or that parameter is `*args` or keyword-only, and a function made from a stub's overloads keeps their `staticmethod` label. Radius, old and new extraction on 17 releases of 14 packages (django, sqlalchemy, numpy, pandas, gradio, openai, langchain-core, fastapi, weaviate-client, click, crewai, pydantic 1.10/2.5/2.14, pydantic-settings, sqlmodel): 47 signatures change. 36 are `__pydantic_self__` (pydantic, pydantic-settings, crewai, sqlmodel) and 4 click's `__self`; pydantic's mypy plugin's `get_is_required` is a `staticmethod` whose first parameter is named `cls`, which the old rule dropped and now stays. The other 3 are functions defined in a class without `@staticmethod` that work only when called through the class (crewai 0.41's `YamlParser.parse(file)`, sqlalchemy's test-suite decorator `quote_fixtures(fn)`): their first parameter now reads bound, the price of not guessing by name. On the format-22 index crewai's `Agent(...)` and `Task(...)` read `missing_args=[]`, and the 2026-11b holdout's 5 false positives go to 0.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-40**: Replacements the holdout tracebacks miss, though the package's own text states them · `P2` `M`
  - Why: moved from DT-DATA-52 (2026-11-09). On 2026-11's tracebacks (replacement 76.9%, 10/13): transformers' `evaluation_strategy` → `eval_strategy` and `shard_checkpoint` → huggingface_hub's `split_torch_state_dict_into_shards` (both in the deprecation text), gradio's `buttons` (got `copy`). On 2026-11b's (40.0%, 8/20): transformers 5's `StaticCache` and `AutoModelForImageTextToText`, `torch.optim.AdamW`, `langchain_classic`, mcp 2's `Client`, dspy's `ChainOfThought`, huggingface_hub's `get_token`, sqlalchemy's `get_table_names`, matplotlib's `sharex`/`sharey`; pydantic-ai's `MCPServerHTTP` gets `MCPServerSSE`, not `MCPToolset`. Most are stated in release notes, migration guides or deprecation text.
  - AC: each is read from the package's own text (a deprecation message, a docstring, the changelog DT-DATA-31 mines) or added as a curated hint that passes ADR 0006's checks; S2 and both benchmarks re-scored.
  - Looked at 2026-11-09, case by case on the live index: four misses come from text the miner reads wrongly or not at all, five from text the snapshot doesn't record, and six only from release notes and migration guides.
  - Done so far (2026-11-09, assembly and query only, no `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` change). The miner reads "refer to X" when X is quoted, dotted, has an underscore or is called: sqlalchemy 1.4's `Engine.table_names` → `Inspector.get_table_names`, and 9 more sqlalchemy names (`Table.exists`, `Query.as_scalar`, `Session.close_all`). It reads a keyword argument written out after a cue as the parameter, never its value as one: gradio 5.50's "use 'buttons=["copy"]' instead" gives `buttons`, not `copy`, and anthropic's `edits`, httpx's `transport`, scikit-learn's `response_method`, streamlit's `width` and pydantic-ai's `retries` lose the dict keys and values quoted beside them (130 messages in 11 packages). A class whose members' warnings say the class is deprecated gets their replacement: transformers' `AutoModelForVision2Seq` → `AutoModelForImageTextToText`, `Wav2Vec2Tokenizer` → `Wav2Vec2Processor`, langchain-community's `MlflowAIGateway` → `Mlflow` (33 classes in 9 packages). And a replacement the installed release no longer has is followed to its own replacement, listed first: pydantic-ai 2's `MCPServerHTTP` → `MCPToolset` (then `MCPServerSSE`); on the live index that changes one case of the benchmark and holdouts, that one.
  - Left, needing what the snapshot records (a format batch): deprecations a `logger.warning`/`warning_once` states (transformers' `shard_checkpoint` → huggingface_hub's `split_torch_state_dict_into_shards`, `HybridCache` → `StaticCache`), a dataclass's `__post_init__` warnings as its `__init__`'s (`TrainingArguments(evaluation_strategy)` → `eval_strategy`), a warning under `if not no_deprecation_warning` (transformers' `AdamW` → `torch.optim.AdamW`, in a distribution DepTime doesn't index), and a module attribute bound to an instance whose methods warn (scikit-learn 1.2's `SCORERS` → `get_scorer_names`). Stated only in release notes or guides (curated candidates): huggingface_hub 1.0's `HfFolder` → `get_token`, mcp 2's `create_connected_server_and_client_session` → `Client`, dspy 2.6's `dspy.functional` → `ChainOfThought`, matplotlib 3.6's `GrouperView.join` → `Axes.sharex`/`sharey`, langchain 1.0's `langchain.memory` → `langchain_classic` (a distribution DepTime doesn't index).
  - Curated 2026-11-09 (5 hints, 328 in all; quotes checked on their pages): huggingface_hub's `HfFolder` → `get_token` (the 1.0 migration guide), mcp's `create_connected_server_and_client_session` → `mcp.client.Client` (the 2.0 migration guide), dspy's `TypedPredictor` → `ChainOfThought` (2.5.17's release notes; `Predict` was there), matplotlib's `GrouperView.join` → `Axes.sharex` and `Axes.sharey` (3.6's API changes). Not curated: no official text names `dspy.functional` itself, `TypedChainOfThought`, or `GrouperView.remove`/`clean` with a replacement, and `langchain_classic` isn't indexed.
  - Live 2026-11-09 (the assembly, the query's following and the curated hints; scheduled build-index 42620876964): against the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 23 index, 196 replacement hints go and 144 come, all from the changes above: the dict keys and values once read as parameters (gradio, streamlit, polars, pydantic-ai, scikit-learn, anthropic, huggingface_hub's `type` → `protected`), the classes deprecated in their members' words (transformers, langchain, langchain-community, flask, gradio), sqlalchemy's ten "refer to", and the five curated; httpx's curated `app` → `transport` gives way to the same hint mined from httpx's own warning, as a curated hint does when the library states it. The traceback benchmark's replacement rate goes from 67.7% to 73.8% (uncurated 60.8% to 63.1%); its other metrics, the TimeMachine-bench holdout and the per-release holdouts are unchanged (bench 42627305780).

Run again on the 2026-11c holdout (not a score): `diff_versions`' false alarms 6 → 5 and breaks found 127 → 125 of 128, all in that langgraph upgrade, whose one flagged break was flagged for the module's removal; the other holdouts don't change.

- [x] **DT-CORE-42**: `explain_error` on a private member of an unindexed subclass's covered base · `P1` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11c holdout: gplearn's `SymbolicRegressor.fit` calls `self._validate_data(...)`, which scikit-learn 1.7 removed from `BaseEstimator` (1.6 deprecated it for `sklearn.utils.validation.validate_data`). The index has `sklearn.base.BaseEstimator._validate_data` among its internal members (1.2.0 to 1.6.1), but `explain_error` searches only public members for a class it doesn't know, and gives no finding.

Its replacement needs the method's warning, which internal members don't record yet.

On the live index the case reads `sklearn.base.BaseEstimator._validate_data` removed after 1.6.1 (no replacement, as expected); the benchmark's only change (98.4% → 98.7%).
  - Live 2026-11-10 (query side, in the next release): the case passes on the `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 25 index too.

On pandas 2.2.3-3.0.3, `NDFrame.fillna(method)` and `interpolate(method)` get `NDFrame.ffill` (0.8) and `NDFrame.bfill` (0.79), and the case passes.   - Why: from the 2026-11c holdout's replacements: fastmcp 4.0 removed `fastmcp.server.apps`. The moved heuristic pairs names, so the module itself gets no hint.

Radius over the live index: 40 modules, all read right (matplotlib 3.9's 17 `axes.m*` aliases such as `axes.mcolors` → `matplotlib.colors`, mcp 1.2's `server.types` → `mcp.types`, fastmcp 4.0's seven, transformers 5.18's `models.logging` → `transformers.logging`, sqlalchemy 2.0's `databases.mssql` → `dialects.mssql.base`); on fastmcp 3.4.11-4.0.0 the case gets `fastmcp.apps`.

- [ ] **DT-DATA-70**: Replacements that need extraction: a renaming wrapper bound to the old name, and "Deprecated name for the X keyword argument" · `P2` `S`
  - Why: split from DT-CORE-48, the two of its five 2026-11d replacement misses the index has no text for. streamlit 1.x binds `experimental_data_editor` to a wrapper of `data_editor` that renames it, and the snapshot records an attribute with no deprecation; numpy's `percentile` docstring says `interpolation` is the "Deprecated name for the method keyword argument", and the snapshot keeps only "Deprecated since version 1.22.0." for it.
  - AC: measure each shape across a sample of releases first; then the wrapper's call records the old name as deprecated with the new one as its replacement, and the docstring's "Deprecated name for the X keyword argument" gives the parameter X as its replacement; the two cases get it in their first three. Next `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` batch, with DT-DATA-68.
- [ ] **DT-DATA-71**: A membership-test shim serves the names its module imports, and a submodule's imported `__version__` is public · `P3` `S`
  - Why: from the format-26 dry run (DT-DATA-64, DT-DATA-65). instructor 1.15.3-1.17.0's deprecated `instructor.hooks` serves what `hasattr(core.hooks, name)` accepts, and `core.hooks` star-imports a module that imports `Any`, `Enum`, `defaultdict` and `inspect`, so those read as public, deprecated names of `instructor.hooks` (with `added` events). transformers' `utils.hub.__version__` and instructor's `auto_client.__version__`, each imported from the package, read public. All of them resolve at runtime, so no answer is wrong, but they are noise in `diff_versions`.
  - AC: a membership forward serves the target's names that the walker would make public (not names it imports from outside the package), and only a package's top-level `__version__` is public however it is bound; next `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` batch, with DT-DATA-68 and DT-DATA-70.
- [ ] **DT-DATA-72**: A name a package's stub re-exports, but its runtime module serves only by raising, reads present · `P3` `S`
  - Why: from DT-CORE-52 (2026-11e's tensorflow-onnx traceback): numpy 2.0.0-2.0.2's `numpy/__init__.pyi` still imports `cast` from `numpy._core.numerictypes`, which doesn't define it (in neither its `.py` nor its `.pyi`), and `disp` from `lib._function_base_impl`, which does. `numpy/__init__.py` binds neither, and its `__getattr__` raises `AttributeError` for both, from `__expired_attributes__`; numpy 2.1 fixed the stub. The index reads the stub, so `numpy.cast` is an alias it can't follow and `numpy.disp` a function in 2.0.x, and `explain_error` answers `np.cast` at 2.0.2 with `present_in_installed_version`. Of numpy 2.0.2's 56 expired names, these are the two the stub still exports.
  - AC: measure first: public names across the index that only a stub binds, where the runtime module doesn't and its `__getattr__` raises for them. Then such a name isn't public at those releases, and the case passes. A blanket rule that an alias the index can't follow is absent doesn't work: 2,274 public aliases in the format-26 index point to a path in their own package that the index doesn't have, most through standard-library imports and compiled modules. Next `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` batch.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-49**: `explain_error` quotes what a callable that only raises raises · `P2` `S`
  - Why: split from DT-DATA-67, which records a callable whose body only raises (`raises`, `now_raises`) for `check_symbols` and `diff_versions`. A traceback that ends in one (llama-index-core 0.14's `ServiceContext.from_defaults` raising "ServiceContext is deprecated. Use llama_index.settings.Settings instead, …") gets no finding: the raise is the library's own, not an `AttributeError` or `TypeError` shape the parser reads.
  - AC: when the error's raising frame is a callable of a covered package that the installed release records as raising that message, `explain_error` says so, with the release where it began (`now_raises`) and the message's replacement; a case in the benchmark.
- [x] **DT-CORE-48**: Replacements the miner doesn't read · `P2` `M`
  - Why: from the 2026-11d holdout's tracebacks (5 of the 9 replacement misses): gradio 3.x warns "gr.Box is deprecated. Use gr.Group instead." in `Box.__init__`'s body (the docstring's "DEPRECATED" names no replacement); streamlit binds `experimental_data_editor` to a renaming wrapper of `data_editor` and no deprecation is mined; numpy's `percentile` docstring says `interpolation` is the "Deprecated name for the method keyword argument"; huggingface_hub 0.25 removed the private `utils._errors`, whose classes all moved to `huggingface_hub.errors` (2 cases).
  - AC: measure each shape across the index first; then a warning call in `__init__`'s body, a wrapper that renames, "Deprecated name for the X keyword argument" and a removed private module whose classes all moved to one module give their replacement, and the five cases get it in their first three.
  - Done 2026-11-10 for 3 of the 5 cases (no extraction change; the two shapes that need one are DT-DATA-70): a class's hints include its `__init__`'s, as a constructor's deprecation is its class's (the index already had `gradio.Box.__init__` → `gradio.Group` at 0.85; only the query didn't read it); and a module that isn't public API and goes away, where the two or more classes it defined are all classes of one module in the release that drops it, points there at 0.6 (one that defines them first, then a public one, then the shortest; functions alone pair by accident, as numpy 2.0's `array_api._sorting_functions` with `ma.core`). Measured on the live index: 30 such modules in 12 packages, all plausible (`huggingface_hub.utils._errors` → `huggingface_hub.errors`, anthropic's `_base_exceptions` → `_exceptions`, pydantic's `_internal._annotated_handlers` → `annotated_handlers`, typer's `_click.testing` → `typer.testing`). On the live index the gradio case gets `gradio.Group` (benchmark replacement 75.5% → 76.1%, 2026-11d 9/17 → 10/17), and with huggingface_hub reassembled both `utils._errors` cases get `huggingface_hub.errors`; nothing else changes.

Both `utils._errors` cases get `huggingface_hub.errors`. With DT-CORE-47 and the format-26 extraction, 2026-11d's tracebacks get the right replacement for 13 of 17 (8 at the scored run), and the benchmark's replacement goes from 74.8% to 78.1% (uncurated 64.5% → 67.7%).
- [ ] **DT-CORE-50**: A call that passes more arguments by position than the signature takes · `P2` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11e holdout: pydantic 2's `FieldInfo.__init__` takes keywords only (`**kwargs` to 2.13, keyword-only parameters in 2.14), so pydantic 1 code's `FieldInfo(default)` fails with too many positional arguments. `check_symbols` checks that a call passes the required parameters (DT-CORE-34), not that the signature can place its positional ones, and reads it present; `diff_versions` reports `default` removed into `**kwargs` at 2.0, which the scorer's rule doesn't read for a positional argument (1 missed break for each tool).
  - AC: `check_symbols(positional=N)` flags a call whose N positional arguments exceed the signature's positional slots at that release, where it has no `*args` (method `self` bound, as DT-CORE-34 counts it), naming the last release that took them; the holdout scorer counts a `diff_versions` event that removes the slot a positional argument filled (pre-registered with DT-QA-12's set); the case reads as a break in both.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (no index change): with a call's `positional` count, `check_symbols` sets the new field `max_positional` (the most the callable takes by position at that release) when the call passes more and the signature has no `*args`, and `hint` names the last indexed release that took them ("takes 0 arguments by position in 2.14.3, and the call passes 1; 1.10.27 is the last indexed release that took 1"). A method reached through its class's path isn't flagged for one argument more: the project may pass the instance itself (2026-11d's `dspy.Adapter.__call__(self, lm, ...)`, which read as a false positive without that), and the index doesn't record which methods are static. The holdout scorer counts `max_positional` for `check_symbols`, and for `diff_versions` a call whose positional arguments DepTime's signature at `to` has no room for (no `*args`) where an event in the range removes, makes keyword-only or adds a parameter the call fills by position at `from` (slots counted, not names, so a renamed parameter still takes its argument; a method called through its class gets the same one spare). Run again on the five holdout sets on the format-26 index (a check, not a score): of 5,183 answered calls (2,809 passing an argument by position) exactly one answer changes per tool, 2026-11e's `FieldInfo(default)`, now a break in both: 2026-11e's `check_symbols` finds 140 of 140 breaks (139 at the scored run) and `diff_versions` 138 of 140 (137), false positives unchanged; the benchmark doesn't call `check_symbols`.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-51**: `explain_error` answers the parent package an import error names, not the module the import line asks for · `P2` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11e holdout: claudette's `from anthropic.types.beta.tools import ...` fails at anthropic 0.29.0 with "No module named 'anthropic.types.beta'", because 0.27.0 dropped `types.beta` whole. DepTime answers `anthropic.types.beta` removed after 0.26.1; `types.beta` came back in 0.34.0 without `tools`, so the module the code needs, and that stays gone, is `anthropic.types.beta.tools` (1 traceback, a wrong finding).
  - AC: when a `ModuleNotFoundError` names a module that the failing frame's import line extends (`from X.Y import` where the error names X), and the index has X.Y removed at the same boundary, `explain_error` answers X.Y, noting that X went too; the case passes.
  - Done 2026-11-11 (no index change): IPython's frames take their code from the arrow at their own line (`---> 15 from x import y`), which the parser skipped, so claudette's frame had no import line to read. Of the repo's 2,273 traceback texts, 215 IPython ones gain their frames' code (188 also gain `referenced_names`), and nothing else in any signal changes. For `No module named 'X'`, the innermost frame with an import line gives the module it asks for; when that's under X, the deepest of its modules that the index has gone at the installed release, or not there yet, is the finding, with a sentence on X: "The error names `anthropic.types.beta`, the package it's in; the import line asks for `anthropic.types.beta.tools`. `anthropic.types.beta` went in the same release and is back from 0.34.0, without `tools`." Not when its versions are X's and X never comes back without it: langchain 1.0's `chains.retrieval`, `schema.runnable.config` and `callbacks.tracers.schemas`, and litellm 1.55's `llms.prompt_templates.factory`, went with their package for good, X's finding says the same, and their cases expect X. Beyond the AC's same boundary, a module gone before X gets its own pin, and one not there yet (code for a later release) its own upgrade, since X's finding would point to a release that still fails. Of 277 `module_missing` texts, 11 (7 cases) have an import line under the module (2 are `distutils`, which the standard-library path answers); only claudette's answer changes, in the benchmark and in 2026-11e. On the format-26 index: the benchmark's root cause + boundary 97.9% → 98.1% (real cases 98.1% → 98.4%), 2026-11e's tracebacks 25/28 → 26/28 (a check, not a score); S3 968/968 and 736/736, TMB, the other holdouts and the 82 golden tests unchanged.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-52**: Two traceback shapes the parser misses: a message without its exception class, and a traceback flattened onto one line · `P2` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11e holdout (2 tracebacks, both numpy 2): imgaug's page quotes only "`np.sctypes` was removed in the NumPy 2.0 release. Access dtypes explicitly instead.."; tensorflow-onnx's puts the frame, the code and `AttributeError: np.cast was removed in the NumPy 2.0 release. ...` on one line. `explain_error` finds no exception line in either.
  - AC: failing cases in `tests/test_tracebacks.py` first; then an exception class followed by `: ` in the middle of a line counts as the error line when no line starts with one, and a message alone is read with the exception left unknown when it matches a known message shape (numpy's "`np.X` was removed in the NumPy 2.0 release"); both cases pass, and S3 stays at 100%.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (no index change): when no line starts with an exception class, a line with one in its middle is split back into the lines Python prints: at `Traceback (most recent call last):` and the chain markers, then at each `File "...", line N, in f`, a frame's code running to the next frame and the last one's to the first exception class after it (a bare `Error` or `Exception` mid-line is a logger's word, not a class). That exception counts unless it's a shape the parser doesn't classify and a known message is quoted on another line. The fallback for a message without its exception line now also reads libraries' own messages, each raised with one class only, as that class: numpy 2's two "was removed" messages (`AttributeError`), pydantic's two `PydanticImportError` messages and openai's `APIRemovedInV1`. The AC said to leave the class unknown; the fallback already infers it for Python's own messages, and numpy raises these only as `AttributeError`. numpy's messages also read without their backticks, which Markdown drops, and a frame's path cut to start at `site-packages/` is a library's. imgaug's case passes (`numpy.sctypes` removed after 1.26.4). tensorflow-onnx's now parses (`module_attribute` `numpy.cast`, with its frame and code), but the index has `numpy.cast` in 2.0.0-2.0.2, where numpy's stub re-exports it and `numpy.__getattr__` raises for it, so the answer is `present_in_installed_version`; the case now waits on DT-DATA-72. Of the repo's 2,273 traceback texts (the benchmark, the five holdouts, TMB and S3), only these two signals change, and five cases that already passed keep their kind and names with the message starting after the class. On the format-26 index: the benchmark's root cause + boundary 97.6% → 97.9% (real cases 97.8% → 98.1%), 2026-11e's tracebacks 24/28 → 25/28 (a check, not a score); S3 968/968 and 736/736, TMB and the golden tests unchanged.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-53**: A class whose own `__init__` is deprecated doesn't read deprecated · `P3` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11e holdout's deprecations: huggingface_hub 0.20 to 0.36 put `Repository`'s deprecation on its `__init__` (0.19 had it on the class), and `check_symbols` reads the class's, so 3 working uses at 0.20.3 aren't flagged deprecated (the probe's rule counts a wrapper on a class's own `__init__`).
  - AC: `check_symbols` and `diff_versions` read a class as deprecated where its own `__init__` (not a base's) is, with that message, as DT-CORE-48 already reads its replacement; the three uses read deprecated.
  - Done 2026-11-11 (no index change): a class with no deprecation of its own takes its own `__init__`'s (canonical path the class's plus `.__init__`, so not one it inherits) when the text is about the class: it names the class, outside a sentence about a way of passing something, doesn't deprecate one of the `__init__`'s parameters (`deprecates_name`, as the index reads a parameter's wording), and doesn't say the constructor alone is deprecated. Measured first on the format-26 index: 261 classes in 22 packages have such an `__init__` (290 texts); the rule takes 179 texts, 170 classes in 17 packages (transformers 115, django 18, gradio 7, huggingface_hub 4), all of them read as deprecating the class (`Repository`, `gr.Box`, `ModelFilter`, transformers' feature extractors and caches, django's old password hashers). It leaves out 105 texts that name neither the class nor its public name (django `Settings`' settings, werkzeug `Request`'s `charset`, `BaseCommand`'s `requires_system_checks`, gradio 3's `gradio.inputs` module warning), 3 about an argument (`ExclusionConstraint`'s `opclasses`, `BasePaginator`'s `orphans`) and 2 that deprecate the constructor alone (sqlalchemy's `Inspector.__init__`, which `inspect()` replaces, and pandas' `TimelikeOps.__init__`); transformers' `GenerationConfig` ("Passing ContinuousBatchingConfig through GenerationConfig is deprecated") is the one a sentence about passing keeps out. `check_symbols` reads it; `diff_versions` (with `used_symbols`) and `symbol_history` give the class a `deprecated` event of its own with `detail.via` `__init__`, not where the class said the same in the release before (huggingface_hub 0.20 moved `Repository`'s from the class to its `__init__`); `explain_error`'s `deprecated_in` takes the earliest of the class's and its `__init__`'s. Run again on the five holdout sets on the format-26 index (a check, not a score): 2026-11e's three `Repository` uses read deprecated (deprecation recall 72.7% → 86.4%, 19/22), and nothing else in it changes; 2026-11b's `RunnableWithMessageHistory` at langchain-core 1.7.6 (2 uses, both tools) and 2026-11d's `ViTFeatureExtractor` at transformers 4.46.3 (`check_symbols`) now read deprecated without the probe's evidence, though both warn unconditionally at the top of `__init__` (langchain-core's `warn_deprecated(since="1.3.3")`), which the probe can't see without calling it (DT-QA-12). Of the 2,273 traceback texts, 7 answers change, only in `deprecated_in` and `deprecation_message`: removed classes now say when they were deprecated (huggingface_hub's `ModelFilter`, transformers' `HybridCache` and `SlidingWindowCache`), and `gradio.Box` from 3.37.0 with "gr.Box is deprecated. Use gr.Group instead." (3.41.2, a docstring, before). The benchmark, S3, TMB and the 82 golden tests are unchanged.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-54**: `diff_versions` can't report a used name no indexed release has · `P3` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11e holdout: going from langgraph 0.3.31 down to 0.0.37, `langgraph.checkpoint.memory.InMemorySaver` breaks. In 0.3 the module comes from the `langgraph-checkpoint` distribution, and in 0.0.37 it is langgraph's own, without the name. `check_symbols` reads it missing at 0.0.37; `diff_versions(used_symbols=...)` has no event for it (2 missed breaks).
  - AC: for a used path that no indexed release has, whose module the package has at one end of the range, `diff_versions` says so in its result (a note naming the path and the release that lacks it, not an invented event); the scorer reads it, pre-registered with DT-QA-12's set.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-55**: A finding on a public alias doesn't say which class it is · `P3` `S`
  - Why: from the 2026-11e holdout: selenium 4.36's "__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'chrome_options'" gets `selenium.webdriver.Chrome.__init__(chrome_options)`, removed after 4.9.1, with `options`: right, on the public name the project calls, but the case expects the defining class, `selenium.webdriver.chrome.webdriver.WebDriver.__init__`, and nothing in the finding links the two (1 traceback, scored a wrong finding).
  - AC: a finding on an alias also gives the path where the object is defined (an additive field), and the benchmark and holdout scorers accept either path; the case passes.
- [ ] **DT-CORE-56**: The traceback parser takes seconds on some inputs · `P1` `S`
  - Why: found while timing DT-CORE-52's change: the parser runs on untrusted text on every `explain_error` call, and some of its patterns are quadratic in the input. The four argument-error patterns start with an unanchored `[\w.]+` (`(?P<owner>[\w.]+)\(\) got an unexpected keyword argument`), so a single long word is retried from every character: 11 seconds for one 19,000-character word at HEAD, inside the 20,000-character cap. The frame patterns end a lazy name with `\s*$` (a long run of spaces after a frame line), the suggestion pattern starts with `\.?\s*`, the call-site lookup and DT-CORE-52's caret stripping repeat from every character of a run, and the `site-packages` path pattern and pluggy's hook message from every occurrence of their prefix. In hosted mode one request could hold a worker for seconds.
  - AC: a test that times the parser on adversarial inputs of the capped size (each over a second before), each well under a second; the parser's answers unchanged on every traceback text in `bench/`.
  - Done 2026-11-10 (no index change): each repeat that can start anywhere gets a `(?<![\w.])` lookbehind, so it starts only at the beginning of a name (the leftmost match is the same); a trailing name ends at a non-space character (`.*\S`) instead of a lazy `.+?` before `\s*$`; the suggestion pattern starts at "Did you mean" (its caller already strips the " ." before it); `takes ... positional` allows at most 80 characters between, which Python's messages fit, and pluggy's hook message 2,000 and 500 (56 and 1 in S3's); trailing carets go with `str.rstrip`; and a frame's module is found by its extension first, then a literal search for `site-packages`. The 12 adversarial inputs in `tests/test_tracebacks.py` took 0.27 to 12.2 seconds each at `40e1424` and take at most 53 ms (the repeated hook prefix; the rest 10 ms or less). A scan of every pattern in the request-time modules, timed at 1,000 and 8,000 characters on 30 repeated units, finds no other that grows faster than the input (`query.PARAM_SYMBOL_RE` flags only under `search`; it is used with `fullmatch` on the index's paths). All 2,273 traceback texts in `bench/` (the benchmark, the five holdouts, TMB and S3) give the same signal as before.
- [x] **DT-CORE-09**: Positional-argument changes (`too_many_positional` → `param_kind_changed`) · `P2` `S`
  - Done 2026-11-06 (no index change): the error read, and gave no finding. Now the last indexed release whose signature still took that many arguments by position, and the first parameter it took by position that the installed release doesn't: keyword-only now gives the new issue `param_kind_changed` (symbol `func(param)`, `pin_to_restore`, a `fix` of the new kind `keyword` with source `signature`), gone gives `param_removed`. Only when the installed signature takes exactly the count the message gives, a method's including its instance. No benchmark case has this shape yet; synthetic tests cover a function, a method and a removal.
- [x] **DT-CORE-10**: `diff_versions` filters, sorts and counts in SQL, so `truncated` is truthful on large diffs · `P0` `S`
- [x] **DT-CORE-11**: Long tracebacks keep their tail; the MCP input limit is 200K characters · `P0` `S`
- [x] **DT-CORE-12**: Replacement hints are trustworthy before they reach prose · `P0` `M`
  - AC: only hints with confidence ≥ 0.75 appear in `explanation` (done); the generic `use X` pattern only takes identifier-shaped targets; targets are checked against the next indexed snapshot.
  - Since DT-CORE-06 (2026-11-01), hints named by the error text are checked against the index; mined deprecation hints aren't yet. numpy 1.25's `find_common_type` carries `np.result_type` at 0.75, a spelling the index doesn't have as a path.
  - Bare targets should be qualified from the index: starlette's `HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_CONTENT` (DT-DATA-43), and numpy 1.25's `product`, `alltrue`, `sometrue` and `round_`, which carry `prod`, `all`, `any` and `round` (DT-DATA-40); the last three are also Python builtins, and the answer means `numpy.all`.
  - Since DT-DATA-37 (2026-11-02): renames on the same class are bare (polars `Expr.apply` → `map_elements`, `Expr.map` → `map_batches`), and so are matplotlib's attribute alternatives (`Colorbar.patch` → `ax`). `param_alternatives` scores any quoted parameter of the function 0.8 even when no cue names it, so incidental mentions become hints: polars' `top_k(nulls_last)` → `k` ("…only be included if `k` is larger…"), `strptime(tz_aware)` → `fmt`, numpy's `array2string(style)` → `legacy`, pymongo's `Collection(create)` → `kwargs`, sqlalchemy's `SchemaType(inherit_schema)` → `metadata`: about 8 distinct cases, 44 hints at ≥ 0.75 on the DT-DATA-37 scratch index (9 before).
  - Since DT-DATA-38 (2026-11-02): docstring hints can name an instance attribute rather than a path (flask's `JSONEncoder` → `app.json`, meaning `flask.Flask.json`), and the incidental-parameter score above applies to docstring notes too (polars `strptime(tz_aware)` → `format`, from "auto-inferred from the given `format`").
  - Done 2026-11-02 (assembly only, no `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` change; committed with the format-11 items). A target mined from deprecation text is looked up in the package's own rows, as of the release whose event carries it, and stored as the public path it means. The lookup tries, in order:
    - the name as written, after expanding `np.`, `pd.`, `pl.`, `plt.`, `mpl.`, `st.` and `gr.`;
    - the name under each ancestor of the deprecated path, nearest first (`prod` on `numpy.product` → `numpy.prod`, `map_elements` on `polars.Expr.apply` → `polars.Expr.map_elements`, `Session.get` on `sqlalchemy.orm.query.Query.get` → `sqlalchemy.orm.Session.get`);
    - a bare name anywhere in the package, if every path with that name is one object (`GoogleModel` → `pydantic_ai.models.google.GoogleModel`). The deprecated object itself doesn't count here, though it does at another path: numpy 2's `numpy.linalg.linalg.det` says to import `numpy.linalg.det`, the same function.
  - A target the index contradicts stays listed with its evidence at 0.5, below anything stated as advice. That is a path in the package's own namespace it doesn't have, or a bare name no indexed release has that isn't a builtin or a standard-library module. A bare name only earlier releases have isn't contradicted: langchain's `alternative='batch'` names a member Chain inherits from langchain-core, which the index doesn't follow. Names outside the package (another distribution, the standard library, `name.map` on an instance) keep their score. A name followed by a hyphen is no target ("use langchain-nvidia-ai-endpoints", "google-generativeai").
  - A parameter scores 0.8 only when its sentence has a cue before it or "instead" after it ("Please configure the 'retry' object instead"; "renamed `metric`" counts too), else 0.6.
  - On the DT-DATA-38 scratch index reassembled with this code (spans identical; events differ only in the stored alternatives' scores):
    - symbol hints at ≥ 0.75 went from 3,770 rows to 3,377. 2,575 now name a full path (2,369 of them were bare, 42 spelled `np.` and the like); a random 50 of those are all right. 802 are unchanged.
    - 384 rows (41 distinct targets) dropped to 0.5. By hand, about 32 of the 41 are wrong: values and keys (django's `'__all__'`, `invalid_list`, flask's `FLASK_DEBUG`, matplotlib's `tool_removed_event`), prose (`newer`, `two`, `our`, `chaining`, `https`), library typos (langchain's `create_self_ask_with_search` for `create_self_ask_with_search_agent`, `get_usable_table_name`, pandas' `is_bool_type`, openai's `LogsDataSourceConfig` for `DataSourceConfigLogs`), and names numpy's `numpy.linalg.linalg` shim suggests that `numpy.linalg` doesn't have (`fortran_int`, `transpose`). Nine are right and lost: pandas' six `pandas.api.types.is_*_dtype` and django's `OpClass`, which the index lacks (DT-DATA-44), plus matplotlib's `pytest` and pydantic's `pydantic_extra_types`, bare names of other distributions.
    - 9 rows are gone: 5 distribution names and 4 duplicates that now share one path.
    - Argument hints at ≥ 0.75 went from 2,046 to 1,973. Of the 16 distinct ones demoted, 14 are wrong (polars `top_k(nulls_last)` → `k`, `strptime(tz_aware)` → `fmt`, pandas `between_time(include_end)` → `include_start`, pymongo `Collection(create)` → `kwargs`, sqlalchemy `inherit_schema` → `metadata`) and 2 arguable.
  - Both benchmarks are unchanged case by case. Their checks are substring matches, so the change shows in prose: "Use numpy.prod instead." S2: 24 of 50 with a right hint at ≥ 0.75, 24/25 right, as before; the right hints that name the documented path itself went from 9 to 24.
  - Not done: a target in another covered package (`langchain_openai.ChatOpenAI` from langchain-community) isn't checked against that package; all 9 such distinct hints at ≥ 0.75 are right. flask's `app.json` stays as written (an instance, not a path) and is the one S2 hint at ≥ 0.75 that the scorer grades wrong.
  - Verified 2026-11-03 on the published `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 11 index (build-index run 41572678023), which also carries DT-DATA-42, -43, -40, -37 and -38: both benchmarks and S2 match this scratch index case by case, and S1 is unchanged (1.2%).
- [x] **DT-CORE-15**: Optional → required parameters: `param_now_required` / `param_now_optional` events, used for "missing required argument" and checked against the installed release · `P0` `S`
- [x] **DT-CORE-16**: Errors raised inside a dependency consider the package being called · `P0` `S`
  - AC: other indexed packages are searched, installed first, keeping only concrete changes (done; bench case `openai-httpx-proxies-internal`); candidates ranked by `Requires-Dist` once per-release dependency data exists.
  - Faster, 2026-11-05 (query only; outputs identical on all 196 bench and holdout cases with the case's versions, none, or every package installed). The search ran two queries with bad plans. `events(package, path)` ordered by `ordinal`, so SQLite read the package's events through `events_by_ordinal` and filtered on path: every event of the package, 4.5 ms a call, hundreds of calls. A suffix lookup matched on the member's own tail: `Client.__init__` read all 61,023 `__init__` rows (1.3 s cold), where its parent's tail `Client` has 38; it now matches the parent, then looks the member up by path (and the whole suffix by path, since `google.genai`'s `google` has no row). 3,931 sampled suffixes return the same rows as before. The `openai-httpx-proxies-internal` case takes 1.9 ms warm, from 55 ms (1.5 s when the index's pages weren't yet in memory; 22 ms now). Tests check both plans.
  - Done 2026-11-05 (with DT-DATA-14): the search starts with the packages that the package whose code raised (the innermost frame's) lists in its requirements, at the installed release (the nearest indexed one at or below it), or in any indexed release when it isn't installed. The rest follow, with installed packages first within each group, and the search stops at the first group with a concrete finding. Being required outranks being installed, because `installed_versions` may name only some packages. A requirement only ranks: langchain-community 0.3 calls anthropic without listing it. The note now names the package whose frame raised, not the innermost library frame, so an error in the user's own code under pytest's frames no longer reads "made inside _pytest's code"; it says "which it lists in its requirements" when it does. On the published index with requirements filled from PyPI for the 17 packages that raise in benchmark cases, 7 of 588 answers change (196 cases, each with the case's versions, none, or every package installed). openai's `Client(proxies)` without versions, or with every package installed, no longer also blames weaviate-client, which removed the same argument. The other five are notes that now say the changed package is in the caller's requirements: httpx for openai, sqlalchemy for sqlmodel, anthropic for langchain-community (behind its `extended-testing` extra). Both benchmarks and p95 are unchanged.
  - Verified 2026-11-05 on the published `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 15 index (build-index run 42044165626): the same 7 of 588 answers change, and nothing else. Both benchmarks are identical to the format-14 index's (real: root cause + boundary 96.2%, replacement 67.1%; holdout unchanged), and all 309 curated hints still check. p95 for `explain_error` is 3.7 ms warm (3.5 before), 4.9 ms on the first pass.
- [x] **DT-CORE-17**: `never_existed` for a name the traceback shows was in use: say it may predate the first indexed release or be created at runtime, at `low` confidence, instead of "may be misremembered" at `medium` · `P0` `S`
  - Found by the first benchmark run (2026-10-28): `numpy.float` comes back `never_existed` at medium. numpy is indexed from 1.24.0, the release that removed it, and before that numpy served it from a module `__getattr__`. Bench case `numpy-float-alias`.
  - Done 2026-10-28: when the owner module or class defines `__getattr__` at the resolved release, `never_existed` is `low` and says the name may be created at runtime. Every `never_existed` explanation (symbol and parameter) names the earliest indexed release. Still open: `medium` should mean the index covers the package's whole history, which needs ingest to record whether earlier releases exist.
- [x] **DT-CORE-18**: `*_not_yet_added` between indexed releases: say "added between X and Y" instead of "first appears in Y" · `P0` `S`
  - Found by the pilot cases (2026-10-28): `langchain_core.tools.InjectedToolArg` was added in 0.2.21, but older series are indexed only at their first and last release, so DepTime says it "first appears in 0.2.43". The `>=0.2.43` advice works; the stated fact doesn't. Removals already get a window; additions should too. A matching `first_version_with` needs denser indexing of older series (a data-policy decision).
  - Since DT-DATA-19 (2026-10-29), every release of a series active since 2023 is indexed, and its bench cases pass. It still applies to series that ended before 2023 and to releases whose snapshot failed.
  - Done 2026-11-04: a `*_not_yet_added` finding carries `last_version_without` and `first_version_with` (new `Finding` fields) and reads "appears between X and Y: X doesn't have it, Y does", so it holds whether or not releases lie in between (crewai goes from 0.108.0 to 0.114.0). An installed version that isn't indexed and lies between the two gets the window wording removals use, at `medium`. For a parameter, X is the last release whose signature is known to lack it, not one with an unknown signature; a parameter the installed release has but only positionally is no longer reported as added. The bench scores `first_version_with` from the new field. No benchmark case changes. `check_symbols` still answers an unindexed version from the nearest indexed release below it, and says so with `exact: false`.
- [x] **DT-CORE-19**: Parse real-world error text, not only clean tracebacks · `P0` `M`
  - Why: 13 bench misses where the error is plainly there but the parser finds no exception line: log prefixes (`api-1 | AttributeError: …`, `ERROR - …`), pytest's `E   TypeError: …` followed by `path:line: TypeError`, indented or `#`-commented lines, errors wrapped in JSON or in another exception (`Exception: module 'dspy' has no attribute 'Assert'`), warnings printed after the exception, bare messages without the type, `module aiohttp has no attribute X` without quotes, and `got an unexpected keyword argument` without a callable name.
  - AC: the bench cases with `fixed_by` DT-CORE-19 get a finding; a failing case in `tests/test_tracebacks.py` for each shape first; still deterministic and still capped in length.
  - Done 2026-10-28: 12 of the 13 cases pass; the 13th now parses and moved to DT-CORE-20. Real cases: root cause 78.0% → 85.5%, root cause + boundary 64.8% → 72.3%.
- [x] **DT-CORE-20**: A parameter check against an unknown signature isn't "never accepted" · `P0` `S`
  - Why: `pytest.skip` is indexed as an alias without parameters, so `skip(msg=...)` comes back `never_existed` at medium confidence although pytest 7.x accepted `msg`. A missing signature should give no parameter verdict (or a note), never a negative one. Bench case `pytest-skip-msg-kwarg-odl`.
  - Done 2026-10-28: the cause wasn't a decorator. pytest's API lives in the private top-level package `_pytest` in the same wheel, which `resolve_external=False` never loaded, so 80 of pytest's 82 public names were aliases (no signatures, classes or members). Ingest now uses griffe's default, which loads only a `_<package>` sibling; pytest 8.0.2 goes from 82 to 859 symbols. No other covered package has one. `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 4, so the next build is cold. The query side gives no parameter verdict when the installed release, or any release in a "never accepted" claim, has no signature, and an unknown signature no longer closes an unindexed-version window. Checked on real wheels: `skip(msg)` → `param_removed`, last with 8.0.2, gone from 8.1.1. The case passes once the format-4 index is published.

  - Why (holdout, 2026-10-31): DT-CORE-05 reads numpy's module-level message ("`np.NaN` was removed in the NumPy 2.0 release") but not this one, so the case gets "not a version-change symptom". A failing case in `tests/test_tracebacks.py` first. The boundary is indexed since DT-DATA-29 (`numpy.ndarray.itemset` last in 1.26.4).
  - Done 2026-10-31: numpy 2.0.0–2.3.5 raise six such messages from compiled code, for `itemset`, `newbyteorder` and `ptp` on the ndarray class and on scalar types. Each now gives the signal of the standard message that 2.4.0 raises instead (`'numpy.ndarray' object has no attribute 'itemset'`), with `numpy.generic` as the owner for scalar types. The holdout `ndarray.itemset` case passes (removed in 2.0.0, last in 1.26.4), and no other case changes in either benchmark. A scalar's `itemset` reads `never_existed`: the stub never defined it, and on a scalar it always raised. The messages' replacements (`arr[index] = value`) are left to DT-CORE-06.
- [x] **DT-CORE-22**: Suggest the path a removed name is still public under · `P2` `S`
  - Why: pydantic 2.0 removed 44 paths that `pydantic.v1` still serves (`pydantic.ConstrainedBytes`, `pydantic.types.ConstrainedBytes`, `pydantic.tools.parse_obj_as`); 43 of them were the same object under `pydantic.v1` in 1.10.27. The `moved` heuristic pairs a removal only with an addition in the same release, so since DT-DATA-33 these get no hint.
  - AC: when a name is removed, another public path to the same canonical object in the last release that had it, still present in the removing release, is offered as a replacement with that evidence; measure across all packages before shipping.
  - Done 2026-11-04 (assembly only): a removed module-level name whose canonical object, in the last release that had it, was also public under another module-level path that the removing release still has, gets that path as a hint (`moved_heuristic`): at 0.75 when it is still the same object, at 0.6 when the path now holds another object (pydantic 2.0's `pydantic.v1` copies). Not when the name already has a hint at 0.75 or more. One path is offered: the same object, then not deprecated, then in a module that holds the definition (`numpy.exceptions.VisibleDeprecationWarning`, not `numpy.f2py.…`), then the same name, then the shortest. A class's members aren't paired: an inherited member's object is shared by every subclass, which on the published index gave 12.1 million pairs, up to 3,685 for one path.
  - Measured on the published index of 2026-11-04 (build-index run 41861243071), computing the rows from its own spans and events: 5,787 hints on 5,783 removed names in 37 packages, 3,289 at 0.75 and 2,498 at 0.6 (litellm 923, pydantic 803, mistralai 630, numpy 561, pinecone 412, sqlalchemy 364). A random 40 at 0.75 are all the same object under a path that imports (pandas 2.0's `pandas.util.testing.assert_extension_array_equal` → `pandas.testing.…`, polars 1.0's `polars.SchemaError` → `polars.exceptions.SchemaError`, mcp's `mcp.server.types.*` → `mcp.types.*`); some only name another incidental re-export where the package documents a facade (qdrant's `qdrant_client.http.models` rather than `qdrant_client.models`). Neither benchmark nor S2 changes: none of their cases has this shape.
  - Verified 2026-11-04 on the published index (build-index run 41912568902, with DT-DATA-39): `moved_heuristic` hints 15,164 → 20,947, and the rows are the local build's (details under DT-DATA-39). No benchmark or S2 case changes from this item.
- [x] **DT-CORE-23**: Replacement mining misses on messages the index has · `P1` `S`
  - Why: the deprecation text is indexed, but it yields no hint or a wrong one. pandas 1.5's `DataFrame.iteritems` says "Use .items instead." (a method with a leading dot); selenium's "please pass in a Service object" names no identifier; werkzeug 3.0's `__version__` ("Use feature detection or 'importlib.metadata.version("werkzeug")' instead") yields `feature` at 0.6. The urllib3 `Retry(method_whitelist)` case also misses although the message names `allowed_methods`: check the parameter-path lookup.
  - AC: a method named with a leading dot resolves on the same class; prose gives no hint; each case above passes or is explained; measured on both benchmarks, together with DT-CORE-12.
  - S2 (2026-11-02) adds three: openai's `Files.retrieve_content` ("The `.content()` method should be used instead"), django 5.1's `CheckConstraint(check)` ("CheckConstraint.check is deprecated in favor of `.condition`", a parameter named as an attribute), and matplotlib's `cm.get_cmap` (`alternative='``matplotlib.colormaps[name]``'`, an expression).
  - The format-11 index (2026-11-03) has 1,010 hints whose target is a Python keyword, mostly where "use" is a noun: numpy 2's 740 `numpy.core.*` → `is` ("its use is discouraged"), aiohttp's 122 → `await`, polars' 85 → `not`, and 41 → `raise` in marshmallow and polars. All score 0.5 but still show in answers (`deptime history numpy numpy.core.ScalarType`). Prose such as `case`, `our`, `its` and `two` has the same shape.
  - Done 2026-11-03 (assembly only, no `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` change: the nightly reassembles every package because the code digest changed, DT-DATA-41). Measured by reassembling all 52 packages from the format-12 snapshots and comparing with the index the same snapshots built:
    - A name that arrives deprecated now has its deprecation event: matplotlib 3.9.1 restored `cm.get_cmap` deprecated, and a new path to a deprecated function carries its warnings. Before, `diff_snapshots` read deprecations only on paths both releases had, so these had no deprecation date and no hint: 1,438 symbol and 670 argument events, in 40 packages.
    - Never a target: a Python keyword (numpy's 755 `numpy.core.*` → `is`, aiohttp's `await`, polars' `not`, marshmallow's `raise`, `async`, `class`, `True`); an unquoted lowercase word that more prose follows ("use case documentation", "Use feature detection", `our`, `newer`, `two`, `dedicated`); and after "the", a name that isn't quoted and doesn't look like code (openai's "in favor of the Responses API" names an API). 1,129 such hints are gone, all below 0.75.
    - Read now: a leading-dot method (pandas `.items`, django `.quote_name`, openai `.content()`) and a name through an instance (`self.x`, `obj.x`, `index.inferred_type` on any `*Index`, `session.exec` on `Session`), both on the deprecated name's own class, keeping the dot when the index can't say (polars `.str.explode`); each name of "use A or B instead" and pandas' `DataFrame/Series.shift`; `from A import B` (langchain's 685 moved classes, crewai, pydantic-ai, fastmcp), unless the wording deprecates the import or an "Old way" introduces it; "call / check / access / using X instead", "call X directly", "Instead use X", "X should be used instead", and "or 'X(…)' instead" (werkzeug, flask and click `__version__`); Sphinx roles with sqlalchemy's module aliases (``:meth:`_query.Query.with_entities` ``); and for arguments, "Use the lifespan parameter instead" and ``Use ``sep='\s+'`` instead``.
    - A target that is the deprecated object itself at another path, deprecated there in the same words, is contradicted (0.5): langchain-community's `Oracle*` loaders and `HanaDB` pointed at their own re-exports.
    - Rows: 23,651 → 24,704, at ≥ 0.75 5,602 → 7,522 (1,483 new symbol hints, 390 new argument hints, 71 raised past 0.75). 19 left 0.75 by key only: `obj.union` and `session.exec` now read `pandas.DatetimeIndex.union` and `sqlmodel.Session.exec`; huggingface-hub's `task` ("use task methods") is prose. A random 50 of the new or raised hints at ≥ 0.75, graded by hand: 48 name the documented replacement, 2 are right with a caveat (`json.dumps` as the second hint for pydantic's `schema_json`; a sqlalchemy target qualified through `sqlalchemy.testing.suite`), none wrong.
    - Benchmarks on the reassembled index: real cases' replacement 48.8% → 53.7% (pandas `iteritems`, starlette `Router(on_startup)`, both werkzeug `__version__` cases; none lost, root cause and boundary unchanged); the holdout unchanged (68.2%); S2 24 → 26 of 50 at ≥ 0.75 (openai `Files.content`, langchain's `ChatOpenAI`), 26 of 27 right, the 27th still flask's `app.json`. S1 measures presence, and no span changed.
    - The cases above: django's `CheckConstraint(check)` and urllib3's `Retry(method_whitelist)` read right since DT-DATA-42; selenium's "please pass in a Service object" names the class only in prose, so no hint, on purpose; matplotlib's `cm.get_cmap` and `register_cmap` say ``alternative='``matplotlib.colormaps[name]``'``, which is DT-DATA-31's.
    - Left: the nearest-ancestor lookup can find a same-named class in another namespace (`Responses` under openai's `beta`; the "the" rule hides that case now); google-genai's "use one of the more specific methods: A, B, or C instead" yields only C; flask's `app.json`.
  - Verified 2026-11-03 on the published index (build-index run 41671329736, xz 69.6 MB): the rows equal the reassembly's, plus one django hint from a release that appeared meanwhile (6,577 releases); both benchmarks and S2 match case by case.
- [x] **DT-CORE-24**: A parameter renamed to the same name in another case is a rename · `P2` `S`
  - Why (S2, 2026-11-02): mcp 2.0 renamed `ServerSession.elicit(requestedSchema)` to `requested_schema` with no deprecation; the index has the removal and the addition but no hint.
  - AC: a parameter removed and one added in the same release whose names match ignoring case and underscores give a replacement hint; measured on the first, middle and latest release of every package for false pairs.
  - Done 2026-11-03 (assembly only, no `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` change). The `param_removed` event gets `renamed_to`, and the replacement is `moved_heuristic` at 0.8: the same function, and a name that differs only in case or underscores. No new event kind, so released clients read the index as before.
  - Measured on every release, not three per package: the published index has 1,059 same-release pairs that match ignoring case and underscores (91 distinct). Left out, as no advice: a private new name (gradio's `clear_btn` → `_clear_btn`, scikit-learn's `process_routing(obj)` → `_obj`, polars' `storage_account` → `_storage_account`), `*args` and `**kwargs` (sqlalchemy's 331 `optionaldict` → `__optionaldict`, langchain-core's `kwargs` → `_kwargs`), positional-only names, and `__x` names, positional-only by PEP 484's convention (pydantic 2.7's `__object` → `object`, starlette's `url_path_for(name)` → `__name`); and any match that isn't one to one.
  - What's left: 530 new hints, 41 distinct renames in 18 packages, none on a key that already had a hint. mcp's `elicit` and `elicit_form`, gradio 4.0's `_js` → `js` (396 event methods), llama-index's `id` → `id_`, sqlalchemy 2.1's `get_columns(tablename)` → `table_name`, polars' `fillchar` → `fill_char` and `fstring` → `f_string`, litellm's `maxTokens` → `max_tokens`, huggingface-hub's `blobId` → `blob_id`. All 41 name the same argument; one has a caveat: numpy 2's `char.str_len(a)` → `A` comes from the stub, and the function is a ufunc. Names that flip back and forth (litellm's `forceTimeout`, langchain-core's `aindex(vectorstore)`) get a hint each way, and an answer uses one only when the old name is rejected at the installed release.
  - Benchmarks on all 52 packages reassembled from cached snapshots: S2 26 → 27 of 50 at ≥ 0.75 (mcp), 27 of 28 right; real cases and the holdout unchanged, case by case. All other rows are unchanged.

- [x] **DT-CORE-25**: Read chained exceptions, `ExceptionGroup` sub-exceptions and lazy-loader messages · `P0` `S` · deps: SPIKE-03
  - Why (S3, 2026-11-05): on S3's first run, every chained case scored 0% (the parser read the wrapper, `RuntimeError("could not start the service")`), huggingface_hub's "No huggingface_hub attribute cached_download" scored 0%, and numpy, openai and pydantic's own messages were missed inside an `asyncio.TaskGroup`'s `ExceptionGroup`.
  - Done 2026-11-05: when the last exception isn't a shape DepTime analyses and a chain marker ("The above exception was the direct cause…", "During handling of the above exception…") joins it to the one before, the parser reads that one, and so on down the chain; errors in one log with no marker between them stay separate. The signal's `wrapped_in` names the last exception, and `explain_error`'s note says the earlier one is explained. An `ExceptionGroup`'s `| ` bars are removed before parsing. `No <module> attribute <name>` on an `AttributeError` reads as a missing module attribute (huggingface_hub's lazy loader, worded like scientific-python's `lazy_loader`). S3: 968/968 on the cases it was designed from, 93.8% on the holdout; `bench/cases` and `bench/tmb` unchanged, case by case.
- [x] **DT-CORE-26**: Take the class from the call when Python 3.9's message names none (`__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'tools'`) · `P1` `S`
  - Why (DT-QA-01, 2026-11-05): before 3.10, a constructor's TypeError says `__init__()`, not `GenerativeModel.__init__()`. langchain-google-genai 0.0.9 calls `genai.GenerativeModel(..., tools=...)` on google-generativeai 0.3.1, which added `tools` in 0.3.2; DepTime blames `langchain_core.llm.LLM.__init__(tools)`, `never_existed`. The raising frame's code line starts the call (`client = genai.GenerativeModel(`), and S4 found the same message shape is the slowest query (its owner is searched in all 52 packages).
  - AC: an owner of bare `__init__` is replaced by the class the raising frame's code calls; the case passes.
  - Done 2026-11-05: for an unexpected, missing or extra argument of a bare `__init__`, the owner is the last CapWords name the raising frame's code calls (`genai.GenerativeModel(` → `GenerativeModel.__init__`), which also joins the referenced names. The case passes (`param_not_yet_added`, first in 0.3.2); `bench/cases`, the holdout and S3 are unchanged, case by case.
- [x] **DT-CORE-27**: An argument a subclass forwards through `**kwargs` to its parent is reported against the parent · `P2` `M`
  - Why (DT-QA-01, 2026-11-05): gradio 6 moved `theme` from `Blocks()` to `launch()`, but `Blocks.__init__` forwards `**kwargs` to `BlockContext.__init__`, so the error reads `BlockContext.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'theme'` and DepTime answers `never_existed`. The frames show the call (`gr.Blocks(theme="ocean", …)`) and the forwarding line (`super().__init__(render=False, **kwargs)`).
  - AC: when the raising frame forwards `**kwargs` to a parent, the parameter is also looked up on the class the previous frame called; the case passes.
  - Done 2026-11-05 (no format change): when the raising frame is an `__init__` whose line passes `**`, the parser adds the last CapWords call of the frame before it (`gr.Blocks(`) as a second owner, and `explain_error` looks the argument up on both, a concrete change first. The gradio case reads `gradio.Blocks.__init__(theme)`, `param_removed` after 5.50.0. Deprecation messages that say to pass the argument to another callable ("You will need to pass 'theme' to Blocks.launch() instead") now mine that call as a replacement at 0.8, `Blocks.launch(theme)`; that needs the index reassembled.
  - Verified 2026-11-05 on the reassembled index (`build-index` run 42117073245, 38 minutes): 13 such hints, all right: gradio's `css`, `css_paths`, `head`, `head_paths`, `js` and `theme` → `Blocks.launch(…)` under both of `Blocks`' paths, and scikit-learn's `PartialDependenceDisplay(pdp_lim)` → `.plot(pdp_lim)`. No hint is lost. The gradio case gets its replacement: real cases 67.0% → 68.1% (56.0% → 57.1% without curated hints); the holdout and S2 don't change.
- [x] **DT-CORE-28**: Name the class that defines an inherited member, as release notes do · `P2` `S`
  - Why (DT-QA-01, 2026-11-05): Django 4.0 removed `HttpRequest.is_ajax()`; the error says `'WSGIRequest' object has no attribute 'is_ajax'`, and DepTime answers `django.core.handlers.wsgi.WSGIRequest.is_ajax`, right but not the name the release notes and search results use.
  - AC: a finding on an inherited member also names the defining class's public path (`django.http.HttpRequest.is_ajax`); the case passes.
  - Done 2026-11-05 (no format change): an inherited member is reported under the defining class's shortest public path with the same object, and the explanation starts "WSGIRequest inherits is_ajax from HttpRequest." Only when that path is no deeper than the subclass's, so a mixin buried in the package (qdrant-client's `QdrantFastembedMixin`) doesn't replace the class users import. The django case passes; on the benchmark one other answer changes, matplotlib's `QuadContourSet.collections` to `ContourSet.collections`, still right.
- [x] **DT-CORE-29**: Evidence to act on: both signatures, where to read the change, and a fix when the index proves one · `P1` `M`
  - Why: a finding gives the releases on each side, the pin, hints and the deprecation text, but not the signature on each side or where the release is described, so an agent still looks those up; and a mechanical fix (an import that moved, an argument renamed) is often all it needs.
  - AC: new fields only (the models are a public contract): the signature at `last_version_with` and at `first_version_without` (or the release the installed version resolves to) when the finding is about a callable; links to both releases on PyPI, and to the project's changelog or release page when its metadata names one (recorded at ingest from `Project-URL`); `fix` only where the index shows it — the same object importable from another path, an argument renamed by an event or a hint at ≥ 0.75 — with its evidence, and never written text (ADR 0006).
  - Done 2026-11-06/07: `signature_before`/`signature_after`, `links` and `fix` (`Link` and `Fix` models), computed from the index after `explain_error` builds its findings. A fix is the same object at another public path, else the one replacement at ≥ 0.75 whose target the index shows at the installed release (a module for a module), with `same_object`, the new callable's `signature` and the evidence; two targets (httpx's `proxy` or `mounts`) give none. On the benchmark, 58 of 202 top findings get a fix, 50 of them the expected replacement and the other 8 right as well (four argument changes whose value changes too, `same_object` false). `Project-URL`s are recorded per release (`releases.project_urls`, no schema bump) since `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 18: on the published index 5,677 releases name some and 1,076 none, and a finding links the changelog or release notes its later release names (httpx, pydantic, numpy's "release notes", django, click's "Changes"). explain p95 4.9 → 6.8 ms.
- [x] **DT-CORE-30**: Confidence that says why, and a threshold and suppressions a project can set · `P2` `M`
  - Why: `confidence` is high, medium or low, and the reasons are in prose; an agent can't tell an exact indexed release from a window, or static evidence from a name a `__getattr__` may create, without parsing the explanation, and a project can't say a change doesn't matter to it.
  - AC: each finding lists the factors behind its confidence (installed release indexed exactly or between two; static or dynamic module; public or internal name; hint stated by the library, inferred or curated) as a field; the tools take an optional minimum confidence; a project file lists suppressions by package, path and change kind, applied locally, with the count suppressed in every answer, never silently; docs/03 describes the factors.
  - Done 2026-11-06 (no index change; new fields and an optional argument only): `Finding.confidence_factors`, from a fixed vocabulary (how the installed version maps onto the index or where the Python version comes from, `module_static` / `module_has_getattr` / `served_by_getattr` from the index's `__getattr__` rows, public or internal name, the first replacement's source); `explain_error(min_confidence=...)` (MCP, HTTP, `deptime explain --min-confidence`) with `below_min_confidence`; suppressions from `deptime.toml` or `[tool.deptime]` in `pyproject.toml` (`suppressions.py`), read by the local server and CLI only, applied to `explain_error`, `diff_versions` and `symbol_history` with `suppressed` and a note in every answer that leaves something out. docs/03 describes the factors. Benchmarks unchanged; explain p95 7.3 ms.

### Quality (DT-QA)
- [x] **DT-QA-01**: 200-traceback benchmark from real GitHub issues (`bench/cases/*.jsonl`) · `P0` `L`
  - Done so far (2026-10-28): 159 real cases from 47 packages; none yet for langchain-openai, pinecone and celery (no verifiable report of a break since 2023). Lesson: verify against the shipped wheel, not the GitHub tag.
  - Done so far (2026-11-05): 191 real cases from 50 packages (+32). The 32 come from GitHub issues and PRs under the same rules: each traceback matched verbatim against its source page, each boundary checked in the shipped wheels, and the expected answers fixed before DepTime ran on them. **First scoring: root cause + boundary 68.8% (22/32)**, against 96.2% on the 159 earlier cases, which fixes were designed from. The ten misses: five private or implementation-only names that integration libraries import (DT-DATA-22); two removed submodules labelled `symbol_removed`, and Python 3.9's `__init__()` with no class (both fixed the same day, DT-CORE-26: 78.1%); an argument forwarded by `**kwargs` to a parent (DT-CORE-27); an inherited member named by the subclass in the error (DT-CORE-28). One case's message had been trimmed to `cannot import name 'X'`; the parser now takes the module from the frame's import line.
  - Still none for celery (every removal in the indexed range, 5.3–5.6, is internal) or langchain-openai (its one public removal, `BaseChatOpenAI.bind_functions` in 1.0.0, has no versioned report). Two cases each for google-generativeai and pinecone.
  - Done 2026-11-06: 202 real cases (210 with the 8 constructed ones), after DT-DATA-06's five and DT-DATA-47's six standard-library cases, each collected after its fix and scored once. Fresh cases go to DT-QA-07's per-release holdout from now on.

First number, 8 seed cases: root cause + boundary 50% (Tier B 80%, Tier A 0%; 4 of 8 are `known_gap`).

A default-run test keeps the golden file in step with the Tier A list. All 80 pass on the published index (built 2026-11-05 22:29 UTC).

  - Python repos whose tests pass under old dependencies and fail under new ones, with exact versions. Run the failing tests in a sandbox to get real tracebacks; take ground truth from the fix commits, never from DepTime. An outside, published source avoids the selection bias of self-picked cases.
